**Supersession.** Revision 28 supersedes every earlier packet in full for review purposes. Only text in this file is in force.

No code, script, hook, setting, memory record or operational state was modified. No fixture, test, git command or operational experiment was run to produce this packet. Nothing was implemented, committed or pushed. G-F is unchanged (CLOSED). A2.6 is not authorised. UR-19, UR-20, UR-21, UR-22, the new **UR-23** (R23Δ), P-06, P-19, P-21, P-25 and every other deferred item stay with the owner; UR-22 gains a described **Option C** (the Revision 21 review's correction (ii)), which is **not adopted** and is not the interim. SPSM was not accessed. **No outcome rule changes in Revision 28** (R28Δ). Revision 28 makes PV-33 (4) refer to §0's current-text definition (R27-01). It rewrites the S(ii) proof steps as two explicit cases and corrects four fixture rationales that used the old meaning of sk (R27-02). It names CR2's exclusivity test in every reproduction of the unspecified list, and the §6.2 enumeration in every canonical-order description (R27-03). It replaces §11.86's object-absence wording with a content statement (R27-04), and keeps the FINALIZE last-call uncertainty explicit, offering "confine EV's unordered calls to EV's slot" under UR-23 without adopting it (R27-05). It restores §11.112's Revision 26 wording, completes §0's index of superseded marks, and corrects PV-30's seven-item clause (R27-06). **Revision 27 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 27** (R27Δ). Revision 27 rewrites residual (bb) in state terms and aligns FX-K23av's wording (R26-01). It states the precondition of the "Revision 20 MERGED, interim not" predicate and corrects the `binary_present` disclosure, where the denominator grows per class-5 Kc path and the numerator only when the Tn test passes (R26-02). It updates incorporated §11.89 and §11.93 (R26-03), names the §6.2 enumeration call and CR2's exclusivity test (R26-04), and fixes a pointer and an identifier collision (R26-05). UR-23's proposed EV order remains advisory and non-operative. **Revision 26 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 26** (R26Δ). Revision 26 rewrites §1.7.3's relevance argument in net-state terms (R25-01). It declares EV's §6.2 parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 comparisons and CR1/CR2 checks to be outside the canonical order, narrows FINALIZE's determinacy accordingly, and offers a proposed EV order to the owner under UR-23 without adopting it (R25-02). It extends the field-movement disclosure to every line that prints content fields and corrects the "toward AMBIGUOUS" predicate (R25-03). It extends the pre-issued-call examples and corrects §11.106 (R25-04), makes §0's scope coherent (R25-05), relabels FX-C08's pid case as order-dependent / unproven (R25-06), and fixes seven consistency items (R25-07). **Revision 25 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 25** (R25Δ). Revision 25 adds a historical-records rule in §0 and marks the five known conflicting historical statements superseded (R24-01). It corrects the K22 → K21 disclosure predicate (R24-02), declares FP's position unspecified (R24-03), narrows the FINALIZE first-failure guarantee for calls already answered from the cache (R24-04), names FX-C12's injected call (R24-05), and makes CV3 count "copies" (R24-06). It defines that cache-answered pids do not count toward the pid limit, and splits pid failures into C10 for a non-zero exit and C11 for malformed output (R24-07). It also fixes stale references (R24-08) and FX-S10 (R24-09). Two items are definitional choices, documented in §10 and left open to the owner: the pid-limit counting rule (P-19) and leaving FP unplaced (UR-23). **Revision 24 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 24** (R24Δ): Revision 24 removes the remaining line-copy claims outside CV3 and defines "survives" for line changes as a net-count statement (R23-01). It rewrites CV1's parenthetical (R23-02), restates the order-determinacy claim to cover genuine failures (R23-03), and completes the list of unspecified pre-FINALIZE iterations (R23-04). It restores a truncated paragraph (R23-05), repairs §1.7.3 wording (R23-06), and discloses reason and field movements (R23-07). It names the calls injected by FX-C09, C11 and S11d and states how `pid` pipelines are keyed in the repeated-calls cache (R23-08). It rewrites CV3's counting unit (R23-09). **Revision 23 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 23** (R23Δ): Revision 23 corrects the CV3 PDB text and K21's stated meaning for line provenance (R22-01), widens the disclosure of Revision 21's movement toward MERGED (R22-02), narrows the call-order guarantee to what is specified and adds limitation L-30 and owner question **UR-23** (R22-03), and fixes five LOW items. **Revision 22 status, kept for traceability:** **No outcome rule changes in Revision 22** (R22Δ): the interim fail-closed behaviour ((a) × B; the UR-22 interim strict rule) is the same rule as in Revision 21; Revision 22 corrects how that rule is described, rewords the PDB text of CV3 (P-21 stays open; its emission condition is unchanged), adds history coverage and fixtures, defines the end of the repeated-calls sequence, and repairs register cross-references.

**Outcome movements disclosed (R22Δ, R21-03; R23Δ, R22-02: the movement toward MERGED widened to its actual predicate).** Revision 21 moved outcomes in **both** directions, and its header described only one:
- **Toward MERGED (looser) — R21's correction of R20-01 (R23Δ: exact predicate, §11.93).** Revision 20 applied its K22 non-textual-current-target rule to **every relevant C-text path**. Revision 21 applies it only to paths with survival class S(i). The movement therefore covers every commit with a path p for which all of the following hold:
  - p is relevant and C-text, and **has no survival class S(i)**, either because (a) End is non-textual at p (the branch replaced the file by a binary, symlink, gitlink or directory), or because (b) End is textual at p but p has **no touched line** (C's line changes there are all superseded by net count, or C's change there is a mode change S(iii), an uncounted change S(iv), or nothing survives);
  - Tn(p) is non-textual;
  - every other check passes (landing, events, PR, the other paths' content state).
  
  Revision 20 gave **K22**; Revision 21 onward gives **MERGED** (R26Δ, R25-07: "Revision 21 onward" replaces numbered ranges that went stale every revision), with **CV3 iff superseded > 0** (FX-K23ax for (a); FX-K23ay and FX-K23bc for (b)). In both cases Tn(p) is not examined. C's line may still exist and be unchecked: in (a) inside a text-in-binary container (residual (j)); in (b) because net counts cannot tell which copy the branch removed (FX-K23bc, R22-01). The Revision 21 review identified this as the first movement from AMBIGUOUS to MERGED in the series; Revision 22 disclosed only case (a) (R22-02). Residual (ff).
- **Toward AMBIGUOUS (stricter) — the UR-22 interim:** FX-K21d (MERGED → K21) and FX-K22m-type histories (MERGED + CV3 → K22); **R25Δ (R24-02):** also FX-K21f and FX-K21k (MERGED → K21: class-5 Kk alone, delete/re-add and restore/re-change). **R26Δ (R25-03); R27Δ (R26-02): conditional predicate.** **Given that Revision 20 is MERGED** (every UR-22-independent check passing), the interim is non-MERGED **iff** **either** K + J = 0, CS = ∅ and some class-5 Kk path (→ **K21**: FX-K21d, K21f, K21k, and FX-K21n where Revision 20 was MERGED although an H1-type path was present); **or**, otherwise, some class-5 **Kc** path whose content(Tn) ≠ content(End) (→ **K22**: FX-K22m). Without that precondition the two branches are not a characterisation: FX-K21l and FX-K21m satisfy the first branch but were K22, not MERGED, under Revision 20 (R27Δ, R26-02). §1.7.3's predicate compares the interim with the **options**, not with Revision 20, so Revision 25's pointer to it was wrong (residual (dd)).
- **Field movement without outcome change:** FX-K22j prints `superseded=1` where Revision 20 printed 0, and FX-K31t's internal superseded count is 1 under the interim; it is not printed, because K31 prints content fields as `-` (§1.11; §11.85). **R24Δ (R23-07):** on **MERGED** lines the UR-22 interim prints `superseded=1` and **CV3** where Revision 20 (= Option B) printed `superseded=0` and no CV3: FX-K23az and K23ba (non-text → text whose object was changed again, with another path that keeps a counted line). **R25Δ (R24-02):** on **MERGED** lines the interim also prints `binary_present=1/1` where Revision 20 printed `0/0`, for a class-5 **Kc** path (for example text → binary, edited on the branch, Tn = End) with another path that keeps a counted line. Revision 20 left such a path out of B; the interim scrutinises it (FX-K23bd). superseded and CV3 are the same in both. **R26Δ (R25-03): these movements are not limited to MERGED lines.** §1.11 prints the content fields on every K15–K24 and K26 line, so:
  - **`binary_present`** (= b/B) moves on **every** such line with a class-5 **Kc** path (R27Δ, R26-02: corrected). Its **denominator** grows by one for each such path, because the interim puts it in B and Revision 20 did not. Its **numerator** grows by one only when that path passes the Tn test. Examples: **FX-K21c** (K21 under both rules; Tn = End: 0/0 → 1/1), and likewise FX-K21e and K21i; FX-K21c with a later target change of p (K21 under both; Tn ≠ End: 0/0 → **0/1**); FX-K23bd with Tn ≠ End and a later exact revert (K18 under both: the denominator grows, the numerator does not);
  - **`superseded`** moves (+1 per path) on **every** such line with a class-5 **Kk** path — for example FX-K23az with a later exact revert (K18 under both rules; superseded 0 → 1).
  
  No outcome moves in these cases.
- **Reason movement K22 → K21 (R24Δ, R23-07; R25Δ, R24-02: predicate corrected).** Revision 24's predicate omitted CS = ∅ (so FX-K21l plus a surviving opaque change r is MERGED + CV3, not K21), required A > 0 unnecessarily, and missed two cases. **Exact predicate:** a commit moves from **K22** (Revision 20, Option B key) to **K21** (UR-22 interim, Revision 21 onward; R26Δ) iff every UR-22-independent check passes and:
  - **(i)** K + J = 0 and CS = ∅ (the interim is in state 2);
  - **(ii)** some path is class-5 Kk (non-text → text, kind surviving, content changed), so Option B's B ≠ ∅ and Revision 20 was in state 5; and
  - **(iii)** Revision 20 was insufficient in state 5, because either (iii-a) some relevant C-text path has a non-textual Tn — case (a) or (b) of the movement above, with any change of C at that path (additions, removals, a mode-only change S(iii), or an uncounted change S(iv); R26Δ, R25-07: mode-only added); FX-K21l is case (a) — or (iii-b) b < B over Option B's B (**no C-text path is needed**; one may also be present; R26Δ, R25-07: was "with no C-text path involved", which read as a condition) (FX-K21m: FX-K21d with a later target change of p).
  
  Under Options A, B and C (Revision 21 onward) the same commit is MERGED (CV3 iff superseded > 0) in (iii-a) when b = B under Option A, and K22 in (iii-b). The interim is never MERGED here.

**What changed in Revision 22 (kept for traceability; R24Δ, R23-05: restored in full — Revision 23 had truncated it).** The Revision 21 review found no CRITICAL or HIGH finding and no false MERGED, and proved that the UR-22 interim adds no MERGED in any history; its findings are about description, coverage and cross-references. Revision 22: (R21-01) restates the UR-22 interim as a **third rule** — never looser than any option in outcomes, equal to the stricter of A and B in H1 and H3, and **stricter than both** in H4 — instead of "the stricter of A and B"; rewords CV3 ("changed again on its own branch … part of such a change may still be present") and K21's meaning so that both stay true when a kind-changing path keeps some of C's content but is edited again; and corrects FX-K31t, FX-K22j, FX-K21d, §11.61, §11.78 and residual (dd); (R21-02) adds the missing histories — H6 split by Pre = ⊥ / Pre ≠ ⊥, H9 delete-then-re-add with the kind of C's result, H10 restore-then-re-change — evaluates them under the interim and Options A, B and C, shows that the rules (which see only Pre, Post and End) cannot tell an edit from an unrelated re-add of the same kind, and states that hazard as a cost of Options A, B and C; (R21-03) discloses Revision 21's movement toward MERGED (above); (R21-04) integrates UR-22 into §0, §3, §7.0, L-24, PV-06, PV-22 and §12, and fixes PV-23, the K12 row and the S(iv) reference; (R21-05) defines the end of the repeated-calls sequence for every run (the last git call issued before snap1), defines argv identity, and documents the ancestry-direct coincidence of `c_paths_in_unit` with paths(C) (FX-C10g). Nine fixtures were added (FX-K21e–j, K23az, K23ba, C10g) and one criterion (PV-33); coverage was recounted: **59 / 47 / 12** (§11.91).

## 0. Status, labels, conventions

**Status.** This is proposed design text, not approved. A review PASS does not authorise implementation. Implementation and commit each need their own authorisation.

**Evidence labels**

| Label | Meaning |
|---|---|
| [M] | Measured earlier; reported only (§7.6). Not re-measured for this revision. |
| [R] | Reasoned |
| [P] | Proposed fixture; **unexecuted** (all fixtures in this packet) |
| [U] | Unresolved; fails closed |

**Class abbreviations**

| Abbreviation | Meaning |
|---|---|
| POS / NEG / INSUF / CONTRA / NONE | Freshness evidence classes: positive, negative, insufficient, contradictory, absent |
| TF | `outcome=TOOL_FAILURE` |
| AMB | `outcome=AMBIGUOUS` |
| PDB | **Pre-decision behaviour.** The normative behaviour that applies to a PROPOSED item until the owner decides. |
| Interim | For an owner question (UR-n), the fail-closed behaviour that applies until the owner answers. Stated with each question in §2.3. |

**Fixture status (R12Δ, R11-08)**

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **B** | BINDING. **Every** asserted field is independent of every open register item. Only failure-line (TF) fixtures qualify in this revision: every classified line carries `origin_identity`, which depends on P-25(i) (§2.2). |
| **Bo** | BINDING OUTCOME. `outcome`, `trace`, `recommend`, `caveats` and every integration and content field are binding. **`origin_identity` and the freshness fields** (`fresh`, `observed_at`, `freshness_source`, `freshness_trace`, `freshness_reason`, `fetch_head`) are stated as interim values and follow the P-25(i) field mapping (§2.2). Used only where that mapping changes none of the binding fields. |
| **B(outcome); X O(P-n)** | `outcome`, `trace`, `fresh`, `observed_at` and `recommend` are binding. Field or text X depends on P-n. `origin_identity` follows §2.2 as for Bo. |
| **O(P-n)** | Pending owner decision on a proposal. The fixture gives the interim (PDB) expectation and the if-adopted / if-rejected expectation where it differs. |
| **O(P-25i)** | A special case of O. The fixture's outcome fields change under P-25 sub-decision (i), following §2.2. |
| **O(UR-n)** (R12Δ; R22Δ, R21-04: UR-22 added) | Pending an owner question (UR-19, UR-21 or UR-22). The fixture gives the interim (fail-closed) expectation and each option's expectation where it differs (§7.0 states the order for each question). |
| **V(UR-n)** | Pending verification. |
| **GATED** | Unreachable while G-F is CLOSED. |
| **OUT-OF-MODEL** | Demonstrates a limitation. Never a pass. |

"Binding-outcome fixture" in the coverage count (§3) means a fixture with status B, Bo or B(outcome).

**R28Δ** marks text that is new or changed since Revision 27. **R27Δ** … **R12Δ** mark text introduced in Revisions 27–12 and kept unchanged.

**Historical records (R25Δ, R24-01; R26Δ, R25-05: scope made coherent).** Every §9 table and every §11 section is a **record of the revision named in its heading**, kept for traceability. **Current text:**
- the header, §0–§8, §10, §12 and §13;
- the Revision 28 sections §11.121–§11.126, including the residual list §11.126 (R28Δ: the Revision 27 sections §11.116–§11.120 are now historical records; PV-33 (4) refers to this definition instead of restating it);
- the following sections **incorporated by reference, as amended** (they hold proofs and tables the current text relies on): §11.78 (paired histories), §11.85 (UR-22 inclusion proof and CV3 table), §11.86 (End classes), §11.87 (K22 → MERGED movement), §11.89 (sequence-end table) and §11.93 (movement derivation).

Where any other historical record conflicts with the current text, the current text governs, and the record makes **no current claim**. The historical statements known to conflict are marked superseded inline: §11.17, §11.45 (three places), §11.53 (two rows), §11.69 (case 4′ and "Concepts kept apart"), §11.70, §11.106's FX-C10d clause, **§11.92** (the relevance-argument note, R26Δ), **§11.112** (two bullets, restored to their Revision 26 wording and marked — R28Δ, R27-06) and **§11.113** item 1 (marked in part). This rule does **not** cover the current text: a conflict inside §0–§8 or an incorporated section is a defect, not a matter of precedence. **Pointers (R26Δ, R25-05):** the current text also cites other §11 sections (about 35, for example "(§11.33)" or "(§11.106)") as the **derivation record** behind a statement. Such a pointer does not incorporate that section. The cited statement is current, and the record shows how it was reached in its revision. Where they differ, the current statement governs, and the difference is a defect in the record, not in the rule. **No claim is made that the historical records otherwise comply with the current rules**, and none is implied by a pattern scan.

**Fixture graph names.** In fixtures, F0 is the fork commit on the target; C0 is the commit before C on the branch; X is the branch end. **Target-side commits are named H with a suffix** (Hf hotfix, Hb back-merged target change, Hu unrelated target change, **Hr** a target commit that removes or adds the line under test). Ka/Kb are criss-cross commits. sb1/sb2 are side branches. M, M1, M2 are merge commits; S, S1, S2 are squash commits. The fixture branch is **br**; binary files are **bin1**, **bin2**. The single letter b is used **only** for the content quantity b/B (§1.7.4); K23 variant letters (a, b, …) are fixture IDs, written FX-K23b etc. Lines of text are L, L0, L1, …; "line 10" means the tenth line of a file.

## 1. Normative specification

### 1.1 Scope and terms

**Scope [B-01, B-02].** A read-only helper plus isolated adversarial fixtures that replace A2.5 Observe step 4.
- The helper emits one line, including `caveats`.
- Observe prints the line, the §1.10 wording for `recommend`, then the fixed text of each caveat token in order.
- Observe derives nothing itself.

**Non-goals**
- D2–D5 and A2.6.
- Deployment, production or completion status.
- MR or author attribution.
- Writing to any repository; changing memory, status or NEXT.
- Semantic revert detection.
- Local `.git` forgery.
- Canonical-upstream identity (P-25, L-15).
- Effective transport of a past fetch (L-16).
- Line provenance (L-4).

**Terms**

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| **T** | Object id of `refs/remotes/origin/development`, resolved once. Never local `development`. |
| **X ⊑ Y** | `merge-base --is-ancestor X Y`: 0 → true, 1 → false, any other exit → C10. X ⋢ Y is its negation. |
| **present(X)** | X ⊑ T |
| **np(X)** | Parent count, from a strict `cat-file commit X` parse (≤ 64 KiB) |
| **parent0(X)** | X^1, or the empty tree `4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904` if np(X) = 0 |
| **paths(X)** | `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z parent0(X) X`, NUL-split and sorted |
| **paths(A,B)** | `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z A B`, NUL-split and sorted. paths(IU) = paths(I.base, I.tip). |
| **FP** | `rev-list --first-parent T`, newest first; pos(X) counts from the oldest end |
| **P** | When present(C): the oldest X ∈ FP with C ⊑ X |
| **GitLab pair (S, M)** | M ∈ FP, np(M) = 2, S = M^2, np(S) = 1, S^1 = M^1, tree(S) = tree(M) |
| **entry(X, p)** | (mode, type, oid) from `ls-tree -z --full-tree X -- p`, or ⊥. Types: blob (100644/100755), symlink (blob 120000), gitlink (commit 160000), tree (040000). Equality compares all three components. ⊥ equals only ⊥. **entry(empty tree, p) = ⊥ for every p, without a git call** (R12Δ, R11-09). |
| **I** | (via, base, tip, end, integrating, merge, seg_base, lineage_root, landing) |
| **CU** | (parent0(C), C) |
| **IU** | (I.base, I.tip) |

### 1.2 Inputs

**Argument model**
- argv[0] is the program name and never a positional.
- `--` ends option parsing.
- A value-taking option consumes the next element verbatim, even if it begins with `-`.
- Any other element beginning with `-` is an unknown option (A08). Everything else is positional.
- Options:
  - `--repo <path>`
  - `--now <TS>`
  - `--trace-argv <path>`
  - `--expect-origin <url>` [P-25 · PDB: not accepted → A08; if adopted: accepted, see R10 row]

**Phase A rows** (first match wins, in this order):

| Order | Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A08 | Unknown option; value-taking option as last element; `--opt=value`; `--now` or `--trace-argv` repeated | bad-input |
| 2 | A06 | `--repo` absent or repeated | bad-input |
| 3 | A07 | Positional count ≠ 1 | bad-input |
| 4 | A01 | C fails `^[0-9a-f]{40}$` | bad-input |
| 5 | A05 | `--now` fails TS syntax, is not a calendar instant, or is outside 1970-01-01T00:00:00Z … 9999-12-31T23:59:59Z | bad-input |
| 6 | A02 | `--repo` not absolute, has a `.`/`..` component, or ≠ its realpath | bad-input |
| 7 | A03 | `<repo>` or `<repo>/.git` not a real directory | integrity-refused |
| 8 | A04 | `st_uid(.git)` ≠ euid | unsafe-ownership |

**Clocks**
1. **Monotonic time** is used only for C09 (60 s per call) and C08 (300 s total).
2. **Wall-clock `now`** is integer seconds UTC. Without `--now`, it is floor(time.time()), sampled **once, immediately after snap0a**. With `--now`, it is the given value, and the system clock is not read for `now`.
3. **Stored timestamps** (reflog ts, FETCH_HEAD mtime) come from other processes' clocks.

**Comparisons**
- Integer seconds throughout.
- FETCH_HEAD is future iff floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) > now.
- 24 h means 0 ≤ now − observed_at ≤ 86400.
- A stored time later than `now` gives CONTRA `future`. **This is not a claim that a race occurred.**
- A write after snap0a produces D02.
- Missing data: R1/F01/F09. Malformed: R2/F02/F07. Inconsistent: R3.

**Test-only inputs:** `--now`, `--trace-argv`, and a library-only harness interface ([harness]) with phase hooks, git-result substitution and `time.time` injection.

### 1.3 Controlled environment

Every git call is an argv array, run with no shell, from a private empty temporary working directory:

```
env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<helper-private empty dir> LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC
       GIT_DIR=<repo>/.git GIT_WORK_TREE=<repo> GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES=<parent of repo>
       GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1 GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null GIT_ATTR_NOSYSTEM=1
       GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0 GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1 GIT_LITERAL_PATHSPECS=1 GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0
  /usr/bin/git --no-replace-objects --no-pager --literal-pathspecs
    -c core.fsmonitor=false -c core.commitGraph=false -c core.attributesFile=/dev/null
    -c core.quotePath=true -c core.bigFileThreshold=512m
    -c diff.context=3 -c diff.interHunkContext=0 -c diff.indentHeuristic=true
    -c diff.suppressBlankEmpty=false -c diff.relative=false -c diff.noprefix=false
    -c diff.mnemonicPrefix=false -c diff.renames=false -c diff.algorithm=myers
    -c diff.submodule=short -c diff.ignoreSubmodules=none
    -c log.showSignature=false -c gpg.program=/bin/false -c gpg.ssh.program=/bin/false
    -c gpg.x509.program=/bin/false -c patchid.verbatim=true -c patchid.stable=<UR-4>
```

**Allowlist**

| Command | Allowed forms |
|---|---|
| `version` | — |
| `rev-parse` | `--absolute-git-dir`, `--is-bare-repository`, `--is-shallow-repository`, `--show-object-format`, `--verify -q <oid\|ref\|oid:path>` |
| `symbolic-ref -q` | — |
| `cat-file` | `-t`, `-s`, `-e`, `blob <oid>`, `commit <oid>` |
| `ls-tree` | `-z --full-tree <commit> -- <path>` |
| `rev-list` | bounded forms in §1.8 and §6 |
| `merge-base` | `--all`, `--is-ancestor` |
| `for-each-ref` | `--contains`, `--format` |
| `diff` | DIFF form only |
| `diff-tree` | `-r --no-renames --name-only -z`; `--cc --name-only -z --no-ext-diff --no-textconv` |
| `patch-id` | `--verbatim` |
| `config` | `--list --show-scope -z` |

**Forbidden:** everything else, including `log`, `show`, `reflog`, `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote`, `gc`, `fsck`, `merge-tree`, `--textconv`, `--filters`.

**Exit codes.** Any non-zero exit → C10, except these documented false results:

| Call | Exit | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `--is-ancestor` | 1 | false |
| `rev-parse --verify -q` | 1 | absent |
| `symbolic-ref -q` | 1 | not symbolic |
| `cat-file -e` | 1 | absent |

**Limits.** stdout > 64 MiB → C08. A call longer than 60 s → C09. stderr is discarded. Paths come only from `-z` output and are passed as literal pathspecs.

**No side effects [R].** No hooks, no pager, `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, the C05 refusals, gpg disabled, no index or worktree.

### 1.4 Preflight

**Configuration.**
- Read once with `config --list --show-scope -z`. Records are `scope\0key\nvalue\0`, split at the first `\n`. git lowercases section and key names; subsections keep their case.
- Only the `local` and `command` scopes are visible (because of `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`). `command` is the helper's own pins and is excluded from checks.
- The `worktree` scope is refused (C04), and `include` is refused (C05).
- Global and system configuration is never read (L-16). The helper never parses config bytes itself, and the snapshot guards `.git/config`.

**Phase C rows**

| Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| C01 | `git version` does not match `^git version 2\.43\.[0-9]+$` | unsupported-version |
| C02 | `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` exits non-zero (this includes git's own refusal of a repository owned by another user), or its output ≠ realpath(`<repo>/.git`), or `rev-parse --is-bare-repository` prints `true`, or `--is-shallow-repository` prints `true` | preflight |
| C03 | object format ≠ sha1 | object-format |
| C04 | Integrity refusal: any of `shallow`, `info/grafts`, `objects/info/alternates`, `objects/info/http-alternates`, `refs/replace/*`, `config.worktree`, `commondir` present or non-empty; non-empty `info/attributes`; `extensions.*` other than `objectFormat=sha1` / `refStorage=files` | integrity-refused |
| C05 | Configuration refusal: `include.*`, `includeIf.*`, `diff.*.command/textconv/binary/funcname/xfuncname/wordRegex/cachetextconv`, `diff.external`, `diff.orderFile`, `core.attributesFile`, `core.alternateRefsCommand`, `core.sshCommand`, `core.worktree`, `gpg.*`, `log.showSignature`, `uploadpack.*`, `protocol.*` | config-refused |
| C06 | `refs/remotes/origin/development` absent | target-missing |
| C07 | Target symbolic, or loose ref file begins `ref:` | symbolic-target |

The redirection keys of OU1 (§1.6.3) are not refused here; they make OU fail. The configuration denylist cannot cover keys added in future git versions; the 2.43.x version pin bounds that risk.

### 1.5 Snapshot model

**Snapshot names**
- **snap0a** = the first `snap_files()` call, giving S0f.
- **snap0b** = `snap_refs()` inside phase C, giving S0r.
- **snap1** = the final `snap_files()` + `snap_refs()`, giving S1f and S1r.

**File records:** (relpath, kind ∈ {absent, regular, dir}, st_dev, st_ino, size, mtime_ns, ctime_ns, sha256) [P-17 · PDB: include ctime/inode; if rejected: exclude them].
- Opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`, inside realpath(`.git`), ≤ 4 MiB.
- Non-regular or unreadable entries → read failure.
- Scope: `config`, `packed-refs`, `FETCH_HEAD`, the C04 files; recursively `refs/heads/`, `refs/remotes/origin/`, `refs/replace/` and `logs/refs/remotes/origin/`.

**Ref record:** S0r = (T, RAW, RH).
- RAW = output of `for-each-ref --format='%(objectname) %(refname)' refs/heads/ refs/remotes/origin/`.
- RH = SHA-256(RAW).
- Equality compares (T, RH).

**`snapshot=` values (comparison scope)**

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `full` | File and ref records compared |
| `files-only` | S0r was never taken; only file records compared |
| `none` | Phase A or B01 |
| `final-unreadable` | D01 |

**Invariants**
- **I-1:** every classified line is `full`.
- **I-2:** `files-only` lets only a TF stand.
- **I-3:** every non-`full` line carries CV4.

phaseC returns `(prov, S0r)` and never raises.

**Snapshot value per row**

| Rows | Value |
|---|---|
| C01–C07; C10 from preflight / resolve_target / snap_refs | `files-only` |
| C10 from later calls | `full` |
| C08, C09, C13, C14 | by phase |
| C11, C12, C15 | `full` |

**What it detects [R]:** torn reads; change-then-restore via ctime [UR-12].

**Residuals:** object store; outside scope; coarse ticks; clock manipulation. No atomicity is claimed.

### 1.6 Freshness model

#### 1.6.1 Inputs

- snap0a bytes and stats; S0r.RAW; local configuration; `now`.
- TR = `logs/refs/remotes/origin/development`.
- OR = every file under `logs/refs/remotes/origin/`.
- FETCH_HEAD and its mtime.

#### 1.6.2 Reflog grammar

```
^([0-9a-f]{40}) ([0-9a-f]{40}) [^<\n]* <[^>\n]*> ([0-9]+) ([+-][0-9]{4})\t([^\n]*)$
```

- cmdline = the message before the first `": "`, or the whole message.
- The last line of a file is its newest entry.

#### 1.6.3 R rows

**OMS:** `remote.origin.fetch` is exactly one value, `+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*`, and no other remote's fetch destination lies under `refs/remotes/origin/`.

**RA**

```
RA         = fetch-form / pull-form
fetch-form = "fetch" *(SP fopt) [SP "origin" *(SP (fopt / src))]
pull-form  = "pull"  *(SP popt) [SP "origin" *(SP (popt / src))]
fopt       = "--prune" / "-p"
popt       = "--tags" / "--rebase" / "--no-rebase" / "-r" / "--ff-only"
src        = 1*(ALPHA / DIGIT / "." / "_" / "/" / "-")   ; must not begin with "-" or "."
```

- A bare form (no `origin`) is accepted only if origin is the only remote.
- **Rejected:**
  - any other remote, `.`, or a URL;
  - a `src` containing `:`, `+`, `*`, `^` or `~`;
  - any option not listed above;
  - `--all` / `--multiple` [P-12 · PDB: rejected; if adopted: allowed with a single remote];
  - `update by push` [P-20 · PDB: rejected].
- A command line proves nothing about the remote.

**OU — origin URL policy (renamed steps; SP0 moved first; IPv6 by value; totality fixes)**

The steps run in order, and the first decisive step sets `origin_url_class`.

| Step | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **OU0** | `remote.origin.url` absent, or more than one value | FAIL `absent` |
| **OU1** | Local-scope key present (lowercased): `url.*.insteadof`, `url.*.pushinsteadof`, `remote.origin.uploadpack`, `remote.origin.proxy`, `remote.origin.vcs`, `http.proxy`, `http.*.proxy`, `http.curloptresolve`, `http.*.curloptresolve`, `core.gitproxy` | FAIL `rewritten` (key existence and semantics: UR-18) |
| **OU2** | u contains `\` | FAIL `malformed` |
| **OU3** | u matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*::` | FAIL `transport-helper` |
| **OU4** | u matches `^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9+.-]*://` | → SC1 |
| **OU5** | u matches `^(?:[^@/:\[\]]+@)?(\[[^\]]+\]\|[^/:\[\]]+):(.+)$` and the first `:` comes before any `/` | → SP0 |
| **OU6** | otherwise | FAIL `local-path` |

**Scheme URL**

| Step | Rule | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| **SC1** | Lowercase the scheme; it must be one of {https, http, ssh, git} | FAIL `scheme` |
| **SC2** | Authority = text after `://` up to the first `/`, `?` or `#`, or end of string. Must be non-empty. | FAIL `malformed` |
| **SC3** | If the authority contains `@`, userinfo is everything up to the **last** `@`. Userinfo containing `[` or `]` is invalid. Remove the userinfo. | FAIL `malformed` |
| **SC4** | Split host and port: see below | FAIL `malformed` |

**SC4 detail**
- If the remainder starts with `[`: the literal runs to the first `]`; what follows must be empty or `:`PORT; the literal must contain `:`. Then go to HS4.
- Otherwise, if the remainder contains `:`: the part after the last `:` must be a PORT, and no other `:` may appear. The host is what precedes it.
- **PORT = 1–5 digits whose value is 1–65535, applied identically in both branches.** An empty host or invalid PORT → FAIL `malformed`.
- Non-bracketed host → HS1.

**scp form**

| Step | Rule | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **SP0** (R10-04, evaluated first) | Host (group 1) unbracketed and exactly one character | FAIL `local-path` |
| **SP1** | Path (group 2) must be non-empty | else FAIL `malformed` |
| **SP2** | Bracketed host whose content contains `:`: if the content parses as IPv6 (HS4 rules) → HS4; else it must match `^[^:]+:PORT$`, and the host part → HS1; else FAIL `malformed`. Bracketed host without `:`: strip the brackets → HS1. Unbracketed host → HS1. | — |

**Host checks**

| Step | Rule | Result |
|---|---|---|
| **HS1** | Lowercase, then strip one trailing `.`. If every dot-separated label matches `^(0x[0-9a-f]*\|[0-9]+)$` → HS3. Otherwise the host must match `^[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?(\.[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?)*$` and be ≤ 253 characters → HS5. | else FAIL `malformed` |
| **HS3** | more than 4 labels → FAIL `malformed`. 1–4 labels follow inet_aton rules: `0x` prefix = hex (`0x` alone → malformed); leading `0` on a multi-digit label = octal (digits 8/9 → malformed); otherwise decimal. The last label fills the remaining bytes (32 / 24 / 16 / 8 bits for 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 labels). Overflow → malformed. | Value in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8 → FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** |
| **HS4** | parse to a **128-bit value** (RFC 4291, `::` compression, embedded dotted IPv4). Zone ids (`%`) or parse failure → FAIL `malformed`. Reject by **value** (see below). | rejected → FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** |
| **HS5** | `localhost`, or a name ending `.localhost` | FAIL `local-host`; otherwise **pass** (`network-form`) |

HS4 rejects these values:
- `::1` (loopback);
- `::` (unspecified);
- `::ffff:0:0/96` (IPv4-mapped) whose low 32 bits lie in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8;
- `::/96` (IPv4-compatible) whose low 32 bits lie in 127.0.0.0/8 or 0.0.0.0/8 (`::` and `::1` are already covered above).

Spelling does not matter: `[0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1]`, `[0::1]`, `[::ffff:7f00:1]` and `[::7f00:1]` are all local-host.

**Determinism [R].** The steps are ordered, every step either decides or names exactly one next step, and every predicate is total. Every u therefore reaches exactly one class: network-form, absent, rewritten, malformed, transport-helper, scheme, local-path or local-host.

**What OU establishes, and what it does not.** Passing OU means only that the **repository-local** configuration names origin with a network-form URL, the host is not a recognised loopback, unspecified or `.localhost` value, and no OU1 key is present locally. It does **not** establish any of the following (L-16):
- that the host is public, reachable, trustworthy or canonical;
- name resolution;
- global or system configuration;
- environment variables at the time of the fetch;
- SSH host aliases;
- that the URL has not changed since the fetch.

**R rows** (first match wins)

| Order | Row | Condition | Class |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | R1 | TR absent or empty | NONE |
| 2 | R2 | malformed line | CONTRA(malformed) |
| 3 | R3 | an earlier ts > the last ts | CONTRA(non-monotonic) |
| 4 | R4 | last new ≠ T | CONTRA(newest-not-T) |
| 5 | R5 | last ts > now | CONTRA(future) |
| 6 | R6 | last cmdline ∉ RA | INSUF(newest-unaccepted) |
| 7 | R8 | OMS fails | INSUF(nonstandard-origin-mapping) |
| 8 | R9 | OU fails | INSUF(nonstandard-origin-url) |
| 9 | R10 | [P-25] pin given and norm(pin) ≠ norm(u) → origin-pin-mismatch; **or** P-25 adopted as mandatory and no pin given → origin-pin-missing | INSUF |
| 10 | R7 | otherwise | POS(ts) |

**`origin_identity`**

| Value | When |
|---|---|
| `pinned` | A pin was given and it matches |
| `pin-mismatch` | A pin was given and it differs |
| `pin-missing` | P-25 adopted as mandatory, no pin given |
| `unpinned` | Every other case. Always `unpinned` under the PDB. |

#### 1.6.4 F rows

**Definitions**

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| norm(u) | Strip userinfo, then one trailing `/`, then one trailing `.git`. Byte-exact, case-sensitive. |
| origin lines | FETCH_HEAD lines with norm(url) = norm(remote.origin.url) |
| D | Origin lines `branch 'development'` |
| D\* | All `branch 'development'` lines |
| WF [P-08] | RA fetch forms with no `src` (bare only with a single remote; `--all` [P-12]) |
| LAST | Last lines of the non-empty OR files |
| ts\* | max ts over LAST |
| Enew | Lines of LAST with ts = ts\* (undefined if LAST = ∅) |

FETCH_HEAD line grammar:

```
^([0-9a-f]{40})\t(not-for-merge)?\t(branch|tag|remote-tracking branch) '([^'\n]*)' of ([^\n]+)$
```

**Evaluation** (a stop is terminal for F; fc is the first failed corroboration check)

| Step | Row | Check | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | F01 | FETCH_HEAD absent or empty | NONE; stop |
| 2 | F02 | grammar failure | CONTRA; stop |
| 3 | F03 | floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) > now | CONTRA; stop |
| 4 | F04 / F05 | insteadOf or pushInsteadOf present / remote.origin.url not single-valued | → 4a |
| 4a | F23 | some D\* line has sha ≠ T [P-23 · PDB: NEG; if rejected → 4b] | NEG; stop |
| 4b | F04 / F05 | — | INSUF; stop |
| 5 | F24 | only if P-08 is rejected: some D ≠ T and P-01 in force → FROW F21 (NEG); otherwise FROW F24 (INSUF `corroboration-not-applied`) | stop |
| 6 | F06 | not OMS [P-08] | fc |
| 7 | F07 | an OR file malformed [P-08] | fc; skip 8–17 |
| 8 | F09 | LAST = ∅ [P-08] | fc; skip 9–17 |
| 9 | F08 | ts\* > now [P-08] | CONTRA; stop |
| 10 | F10 | Enew cmdlines differ [P-08] | fc |
| 11 | F12 | `--all` form ∧ P-12 adopted ∧ > 1 remote [P-08, P-12] | fc |
| 12 | F11 | Enew cmdline ∉ WF [P-08] | fc |
| 13 | F13 | a non-origin line present [P-08] | fc |
| 14 | F14 | duplicate `branch '<n>'` origin lines [P-08] | fc |
| 15 | F15 | content link: each e ∈ Enew with new ≠ 0{40} must have exactly one origin line `branch '<name>'` with sha = e.new [P-08] | fc |
| 16 | F16 | completeness: excluding development and HEAD, origin branch lines must be in bijection with the tracking refs in S0r.RAW, with equal values [P-08] | fc |
| 17 | F17 | \|floor(mtime_ns/10⁹) − ts\*\| > W [P-08; P-11 · PDB two-sided; if rejected: one-sided (fails only if mtime_s < ts\* − W); UR-1 · W = 120 s] | fc |

**Development decision** (evaluated in this order)

| Row | Condition | Result |
|---|---|---|
| F19 | corroborated ∧ D ≠ T | NEG `negative-conflict` [B-12] |
| F21 | ¬corroborated ∧ D ≠ T | [P-01 · PDB: NEG `negative-conflict-uncorroborated`; if rejected: INSUF, fc row and token] |
| F20 | corroborated ∧ \|D\| = 0 | [P-10 · PDB: NEG `negative-dev-absent`; if rejected: INSUF `dev-absent-ignored`] |
| F22 | corroborated ∧ \|D\| = 1 ∧ D = T ∧ G-F CLOSED | INSUF `unverified-association` |
| F18 | as F22, with G-F OPEN | POS `ok` — **unreachable** |
| — | otherwise | INSUF, fc row and token |

**fc → fetch_head**

| fc | fetch_head |
|---|---|
| F06 | `uncorroborated-refspec` |
| F07 | `uncorroborated-reflog-malformed` |
| F09 | `uncorroborated-no-fetch-event` |
| F10 | `uncorroborated-multiple-fetch-events` |
| F12 | `uncorroborated-multi-remote` |
| F11 | `uncorroborated-not-whole-fetch` |
| F13 | `uncorroborated-foreign-lines` |
| F14 | `uncorroborated-duplicate-lines` |
| F15 | `uncorroborated-no-link` |
| F16 | `uncorroborated-incomplete` |
| F17 | `uncorroborated-no-reflog` if ts\* < mtime_s − W, else `uncorroborated-window` |

**Terminal rows**

| Row | fetch_head |
|---|---|
| F01 | `none` |
| F02 | `malformed` |
| F03 / F08 | `future` |
| F04 | `inadmissible-insteadof` |
| F05 | `inadmissible-remote-url` |
| F23 | `negative-conflict-unattributed` |
| F24 | `corroboration-not-applied` |

**FROW.** The terminal row if F stopped (F24 per step 5); else the decision row; else the fc row. Exactly one FROW per input.

**mtime** is used only in F03 and F17, and only as the file's write time [B-11].

#### 1.6.5 Combination (every field stated)

| Row | R | F | observed_at | fresh | source | reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Cb1 | any | NEG | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb2 | CONTRA | not NEG | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb3 | not CONTRA | CONTRA | none | no | none | blocking order |
| Cb4 | POS(t_R) | POS(t\*) | max | yes iff 0 ≤ now − obs ≤ 86400 | both | ok/stale — GATED |
| Cb5 | POS(t_R) | INSUF/NONE | t_R | yes iff 0 ≤ now − t_R ≤ 86400 | reflog | ok/stale |
| Cb6 | INSUF(R6)/NONE | POS(t\*) | t\* | yes iff ≤ 86400 | fetch_head | ok/stale — GATED [P-09] |
| Cb7 | INSUF(R6/R8/R9/R10) | INSUF/NONE | none | no | none | newest-unaccepted / nonstandard-origin-mapping / nonstandard-origin-url / origin-pin-mismatch / origin-pin-missing (per the R row) |
| Cb8 | NONE | INSUF/NONE | none | no | none | no-evidence |
| Cb9 | INSUF(R8/R9/R10) | POS | none | no | none | as Cb7 — GATED |

- Every (R class, F class) pair has exactly one row.
- **Blocking order:** conflict > conflict-unattributed > dev-absent > conflict-uncorroborated > future > newest-not-T > non-monotonic > malformed > fetch-head-malformed.
- **While G-F is CLOSED, the only positive path is R7 → Cb5.**

#### 1.6.6 Gate G-F

**CLOSED.** Opening it requires all of:
- UR-7, UR-8, UR-9 and UR-10 verified;
- UR-1 measured;
- owner approval of P-08, P-11, P-12 and P-09.

While CLOSED:
- F18, Cb4, Cb6 and Cb9 are unreachable;
- no FETCH_HEAD evidence can make fresh=yes;
- F19, F20, F21 and F23 are still evaluated.

#### 1.6.7 Unchanged-target fetch [B-13]

| Case | Description | Freshness |
|---|---|---|
| UF1 | Other refs moved | never fresh (F22) |
| UF2 | Nothing moved | never fresh — **UR-2, owner question** |
| UF3 | Partial fetch or no-op pull | never fresh |

**UR-2 options:**
- (a) admit FETCH_HEAD mtime as a file-level time;
- (b) `ls-remote`;
- (c) a fetch recorder;
- (d) accept the limitation. **Option (d) applies meanwhile.**


### 1.7 Patch identity, anchoring, landing and content — R28Δ (R27-02, -03, -05); R27Δ (R26-04, -05); R26Δ (R25-01, -02, -04, -06, -07); R25Δ (R24-02, -03, -04, -06, -07); R24Δ (R23-01, -02, -03, -04, -06, -08, -09); R23Δ (R22-01, -03, -05, -06); R22Δ (R21-01, -02, -04, -05); R21Δ (R20-01..R20-06); R20Δ (R19-01..R19-06); R19Δ (R18-01, -02, -03, -04, -07, -08); R18Δ (R17-01, -02, -04, -06); R17Δ (R16-01, -02, -03, -05, -06); R16Δ (R15-02, -03, -06, -07); R15Δ (R14-01, -02, -04, -05); R14Δ (R13-01, R13-02); R13Δ (R12-01, -05, -06, -08); R12Δ (R11-01, -02, -04, -05, -09, -10, -11, -13)

#### 1.7.1 Claims

| Evidence | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Patch-id equality | Net-diff equivalence under the pinned diff (what equality implies is UR-16). It **identifies the integration** (IA3–IA5). It never establishes landing by itself. |
| Entry equality | Identical (mode, type, oid) |
| Line counts | Net per-file counts with **no provenance**. Identical text lines cannot be told apart, so no claim is ever made about *which* copy of a line is C's. |
| **Landing** (R12Δ) | For every relevant path, the integrated state Tip agrees with C's branch end End at that path. **In force (interim, UR-21 open): by state equality only**, entry(Tip,p) = entry(End,p), in every mode. **Only if the owner adopts UR-21 Option A:** **landing-textual** squash paths may also be landed by **count-level** agreement (the target-count guard plus the carried-by-unit check, §1.7.3). That evidence is count-level: it is not provenance, and it does not show that an equal count came from C rather than from an independent equivalent change. |

#### 1.7.2 DIFF and pid

```
DIFF(A,B) := git --attr-source=4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904 diff -U3 --no-relative
             --no-ext-diff --no-textconv --no-color --binary --full-index --diff-algorithm=myers
             --no-renames --ignore-submodules=none --submodule=short --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/
             -O/dev/null <A> <B>
pid(A,B)  := DIFF(A,B) | git patch-id --verbatim
```

0 lines → NULL. Exactly one line matching `^[0-9a-f]{40} [0-9a-f]{40}$` → its first field. Anything else → C11. A and B may be commits or the empty tree.

#### 1.7.3 Anchoring and landing (R12Δ)

**States**

| Name | Definition |
|---|---|
| Pre | parent0(C) (the empty tree if np(C) = 0) |
| Post | C |
| End | I.end |
| Tb | I.base (the target before integration) |
| Tip | I.tip |
| Tn | T |
| Bu | Branch-side unit base: Pre (direct); seg_base (squash: B_X for a first-integration match, X_v for a chained match); the single member of `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` (ancestry-merge; 0 → K28, > 1 → K29) |
| **Ht** (R12Δ, R11-04) | **Absorbed-target base** — the newest target state the unit base has absorbed. Ht = Bu for ancestry-merge and first-integration squash (Bu ⊑ T by construction: it is a merge base with the target side). For a **chained** squash (Bu = X_v, which is not in T), Ht is the single member of `merge-base --all X_v T`; 0 or > 1 members → **K34**. For ancestry-direct, Ht = Pre. |

**State kind (R13Δ, R12-01, R12-06).** For a state X and a path p, kind(X,p) is exactly one of:
- **absent** — entry(X,p) = ⊥;
- **tree** (040000), **gitlink** (160000), **symlink** (120000);
- **binary** — a regular blob (100644/100755) with a NUL in its first 8000 bytes;
- **text** — any other regular blob, **including the empty blob** and a blob whose lines are all whitespace.

A state is **textual** at p iff its kind is text or absent. **blob(X,p)** := the oid component of entry(X,p) if kind(X,p) ∈ {text, binary}, else ⊥. Comparisons of blob(·) and entry(·) are therefore always defined (⊥ equals only ⊥). X(L), the count of line L at p, is defined **only for a textual state** (absent counts as 0) and is never computed on a non-textual state.

**content and mode (R18Δ, R17-01).** content(X,p) := (kind(X,p), oid of entry(X,p)) when kind(X,p) ∈ {text, binary, symlink, gitlink}; := (tree) when kind(X,p) = tree (the directory's oid is ignored: the files inside it are separate paths of C and are judged on their own); := ⊥ when absent. mode(X,p) := the mode component of entry(X,p), or ⊥. entry(X,p) = entry(Y,p) iff mode, type and oid all agree; content and mode separate the "what" from the "permissions".

**Survival key (R19Δ; R21Δ, R20-04: kind-changing paths made explicit).** For a C-opaque path p whose content C changed (content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p)):
- p is **kind-changing** iff kind(Pre,p) ≠ kind(Post,p) (absent, text, binary, symlink, gitlink and tree are distinct kinds);
- **content survives** iff content(End,p) = content(Post,p) (for a tree result, content = (tree), so this equals kind survival);
- **kind survives** iff kind(End,p) = kind(Post,p).

How survival is used depends on **UR-22** (owner question, §2.3). **R22Δ (R21-01): the interim is not one of the options and is not "the stricter of A and B".** It is a third rule. In outcomes it is never looser than any option (MERGED under the interim implies MERGED under Options A, B and C; §11.85). It coincides with the stricter of A and B in H1 and H3, and it is **stricter than both A and B** in H4 (C's only content change is non-text → text and is edited again on the branch: both options give MERGED, the interim gives K21; §11.78). **R23Δ (R22-05), exact predicate:** with every UR-22-independent check passing and b = B under Option A, the interim returns a non-MERGED result while Options A, B and C all return MERGED **iff** K + J = 0, no C-opaque content change's content survives (CS = ∅), and at least one path is a **class-5 Kk** path (non-text → text, kind surviving, content changed; §11.86). Instances: H4 (FX-K21d), H9-Kk (FX-K21f) and H10b-Kk (FX-K21k). The interim then gives K21 (P-06 PDB). With another path that keeps a counted surviving line or surviving opaque content ("+g"), the interim is not in state 2 and returns MERGED like the options (FX-K23az, K23ba). Revision 22's "the same holds in every history with H4's End class" was false for those. In caveats it is not a subset: it can emit CV3 where no option does (FX-K23az, K23ba).
- **Interim (strict, fails closed; in force until the owner answers UR-22):** p is a member of **B** (its current-target state is checked by b) iff its content survives, **or** it is kind-changing and its kind survives. p counts toward **superseded** iff its **content** does not survive — whether the branch edited, replaced, or deleted and re-added the object (so a kind-surviving, content-changed path counts in both). For **content state 2 (K21)** only members of B whose content survives count (**B\***), so a C whose only content change is a kind change whose object was changed again on the branch is K21. **Under the interim, membership of B by kind alone is a scrutiny rule** (the current target is checked) and **not a survival claim**: no outcome reason, field or caveat text says that such a path's change survives (R22Δ, R21-02).
- **Option A (symmetric kind key):** for every kind-changing p, survival is judged by kind; for others by content. B = surviving paths; superseded = non-surviving; state 2 uses B.
- **Option B (Revision 19–20 asymmetric key):** survival by kind only when kind(Post) = tree, or kind(Post) = text and kind(Pre) non-textual; otherwise by content. B = surviving; superseded = non-surviving; state 2 uses B.
- **Option C (R22Δ; the Revision 21 review's correction (ii); described for the owner, not adopted, not the interim):** B = Option A's B; B\* (state 2) = Option B's B; superseded = the paths that do not survive under Option A **or** do not survive under Option B. **R23Δ (R22-06):** as under the interim, B here is larger than the survivors that superseded uses. A class-5 Kc path (for example text → binary, edited on the branch) is in B and S(ii), and is also counted superseded. Under Option C, membership of B or S(ii) by kind alone is **scrutiny** (checked by b), **not survival**, and no text may claim survival from it.
- **What no option can see (R22Δ, R21-02):** the rules read only Pre, Post and End. An edit of C's object, a replacement of it, a delete-then-re-add of unrelated content of the same kind (H9) and a restore-then-re-change to the same kind (H10b) give the **same End state**, so the interim and each option return one result for all of them (the options may still differ from each other). Wherever an option judges a kind-changing path by kind, it credits unrelated re-added content of the matching kind as survival of C's change (Option A for every kind change; Options B and C for non-text → text). §11.86 enumerates every End class.

**sk(X,p)** below denotes the key that decides "content survives" (content, or kind for a tree result); kind survival is used only as stated above.

**C-text and C-opaque paths (R13Δ).** A path p ∈ paths(C) is **C-text** iff Pre and Post are both textual at p; otherwise it is **C-opaque**. This depends on C alone.

**Touched lines.** For a C-text path p whose End state is textual, a line L is **touched** iff S_A(L) + S_R(L) > 0 (§1.7.4). Text comparison drops whitespace-only lines and compares byte-exact.

**"Survives" for line changes (R24Δ, R23-01).** Throughout this packet, a line change of C "survives" (and a "surviving line", "touched line" or S(i)) means only that **by net line count** the branch end still shows it: S_A(L) + S_R(L) > 0. It does not establish that C's own copy of an identical line is present, and "does not survive" (S_A = S_R = 0) does not establish that C's copy is absent (L-4). FX-K23bb is a path with no surviving line change by net count where C's own copy is present. For C-opaque paths "survives" means the UR-22 rule in force (content or kind), never line provenance.

**Relevance and survival — separated (R20Δ, R19-01).** Two different questions are answered by two different rules.

**Relevant (subject to landing).** A path p ∈ paths(C) is **relevant** iff **entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p)** — the branch end still differs from C's parent at p, in content, kind or file mode (⊥ equals only ⊥). Only the entries of Pre and End are compared; Bu, Tb, Tip and Tn never affect it. This is a **scrutiny** rule, not a survival claim: it also selects a path whose branch-end state differs from Pre only because of later branch commits (C's change superseded, or deleted and then re-added differently). Selecting too much can only add AMBIGUOUS results — K25 (candidate anchoring), K30, K31 (landing), K32, K34 (anchoring) — and TOOL_FAILURE rows from the extra reads (C08, C09, C10); since R21 restricts the K22 current-target rule to S(i) paths, it no longer adds K22; it can never add a MERGED (R21Δ, R20-01; §11.77).

**Survival classes (which of C's changes survive to the branch end; R23Δ: for S(ii) under the interim and Option C, which paths are scrutinised — see below).** Used by the content checks (§1.7.4), CV1/CV3 wording, superseded and Option A eligibility — **never to exclude a path from landing**:
- **S(i)** p is C-text, End is textual at p, and p has a touched line;
- **S(ii)** p is C-opaque, content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p), and p is a member of B under the UR-22 rule in force (interim: content survives, or p is kind-changing and its kind survives). **R22Δ; R23Δ (R22-06): extended to Option C.** Under the interim **and under Option C**, S(ii) by kind alone means "checked by b". It does **not** mean that C's content survives (B\* and superseded do not use kind alone there), and no text claims survival from S(ii). Under Options A and B, B equals that option's survivors;
- **S(iii)** a mode change: entry(Pre,p) ≠ ⊥, entry(Post,p) ≠ ⊥, mode(Post,p) ≠ mode(Pre,p), entry(End,p) ≠ ⊥ and mode(End,p) = mode(Post,p);
- **S(iv)** p is C-text, End is textual at p, blob(Pre,p) ≠ blob(Post,p), no line at p has A(L) + R(L) > 0, and blob(End,p) = blob(Post,p).

Earlier revisions called S(i)–S(iv) "rules (i)–(iv)" and used them as the relevance test; fixture text "relevant by (k)" now reads "relevant (End ≠ Pre), with survival class S(k)".

**Every path with a survival class is relevant [R].** S(i): a touched line means End(L) ≠ Pre(L) for some L, so the blobs differ. S(ii) (R28Δ, R27-02: two cases): either content(End) = content(Post) ≠ content(Pre), so content(End) ≠ content(Pre); or p is kind-changing with kind(End) = kind(Post) ≠ kind(Pre), so kind(End) ≠ kind(Pre). Either way entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p). Which case makes p a member of S(ii) depends on the UR-22 rule in force (§1.7.3 survival key), but both cases imply the difference. S(iii): mode(End) = mode(Post) ≠ mode(Pre). S(iv): blob(End) = blob(Post) ≠ blob(Pre). In each case entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p).

**Why no surviving change can drop out of relevance (R20Δ; R26Δ, R25-01: restated in net-state terms) [R].** Relevance compares **states**, not copies. If the branch-end entry at p differs from Pre's in any respect — content, kind or mode — p is relevant. Every survival class implies such a difference ("Every path with a survival class is relevant", above): S(i) has a touched line, so End(L) ≠ Pre(L) by net count; S(ii) has either content(End) = content(Post) ≠ content(Pre) or, for a kind-changing path, kind(End) = kind(Post) ≠ kind(Pre) (R28Δ, R27-02: was "sk(End) = sk(Post) ≠ sk(Pre)", which fails for kind-only members because sk = content for a non-tree result); S(iii) has mode(End) = mode(Post) ≠ mode(Pre); S(iv) has blob(End) = blob(Post) ≠ blob(Pre). So no path with a survival class can drop out of relevance. Contrapositive: a path that is not relevant has entry(End,p) = entry(Pre,p). There, **by net count and by content**, none of C's changes appears: S_A = S_R = 0, content(End) = content(Pre) and mode(End) = mode(Pre). This is a statement about the **state**, not about copies. Which identical copy of a line remains is not established (L-4; §1.7.3 "Survives"): End can equal Pre while C's own added copy of an identical line is still present, because the branch removed a pre-existing copy instead (FX-K23be). The argument uses no kinds and no survival keys, and no counts beyond their definitions. It replaces the per-kind arguments of Revisions 13–19, each of which was falsified (R12-01, R17-01, R18-01, R19-01). A path that is not relevant takes no part in anchoring or landing. Its counted and opaque changes count toward superseded, and CV3's text, which speaks only of net counts and content (§1.10; §11.85), is true for them. (Revision 25 and earlier said "a line … that C put there … no part of C's change exists at the branch end … truthfully, because nothing of them is at the branch end". That is a copy claim, false under FX-K23be, and it is withdrawn.)

**Landing-textual (R13Δ).** A relevant path is **landing-textual** iff Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb and Tip are all textual at p. Only a landing-textual path may ever be landed by line counts (UR-21 Option A). Every other relevant path is landed only by state equality (r = e), or by construction in direct mode.

**Step 1 — Structural checks and anchoring (R14Δ, R13-02: separated)**

**Step 0 — structural checks (R16Δ, R15-02/R15-07: exactly these, nothing else).** These are not anchoring. They run in every mode and under every UR-19 and UR-21 option (and are independent of UR-22 — R22Δ), and none reads Ht, Fk or AS:
- **before FINALIZE (§1.8.2):** the endpoint check (K27, K08), IA1–IA7 selection **except IA7 step 3** (candidate anchoring, below — R17Δ, R16-05), with K25 **for distinct X**, and the no-covering rows K10–K14;
- **in FINALIZE, ancestry-merge only:** Bu := `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` (0 → **K28**, > 1 → **K29**). Bu is needed by landing (ub), so it is structural.

**Step 0 failure continuation (R16Δ, R15-02).** If the Bu count gives K28 or K29, then **AS, Ht, Fk and landing are not computed**; AN := BU_NONE / BU_MULTI (that row) (R17Δ, R16-06), and nothing that reads Bu, ub, Ht or Fk runs. **Events, PR and content still run**, because none of them reads Bu (they read I.base = P^1, I.tip = P, I.end = P^2, Pre, Post and T). A C row they raise preempts K28/K29, as any C row preempts every K row. Otherwise K28/K29 is the decision. FX-K28h pins this.

**Candidate anchoring (R16Δ, R15-07; R17Δ: IA7 step 3).** When IA7 has several candidates for the same X, **IA7 step 3** computes AN for each candidate **before FINALIZE**. This is **anchoring**, not Step 0: it uses each candidate's AS, Ht and Fk exactly as below, runs only where AS ≠ ∅, and its K34/K32 results only feed IA7's K25 decision. A C row raised during it preempts every K row.

**Not structural and not anchoring.** The type guard and landing (Step 2) run **only if AN = ANCHORED**. Events (§6), PR and content (§1.7.4) are **always** computed in FINALIZE, whatever AN is (including K28/K29). A C row raised by any computation that runs preempts every K row; a skipped computation raises nothing. §1.8.3 and §4 state the same order.

**Read discipline and failure mapping (R16Δ; R17Δ, R16-03: staged AN and one canonical order).**
- **Every computation that runs reads its whole read set** (R24Δ, R23-06: this applies to the computations of IA7 step 3 and FINALIZE listed here; before FINALIZE, short-circuiting is unspecified, see "Order guarantees"), for every path in its domain, in the canonical order below; no value is read lazily or skipped because it turns out not to be needed. Read sets:
  - *relevance:* paths(C); entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End;
  - *AS* (only under (b) × Option A): the relevant paths; entries and blobs of Bu, Tb, Tip (kinds), and r, e;
  - *AN — in two stages (R17Δ):* **stage 1** — the decisions that need no entry: np(C) = 0 (no call); for a chained squash, `merge-base --all X_v T` (Ht); unless Ht = Pre, `merge-base --all Ht Pre` (Fk). If stage 1 yields K34, AN := FK_UNDET and **stage 2 is not run**: no entry of Ht, Fk or any member of a non-single Fk is read. **stage 2** — only when Ht and Fk are both single: the entries of Ht and Fk at the paths in AS. (Candidate anchoring in IA7 uses the same two stages per candidate.)
  - *LD:* the relevant paths; entries of Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb, Tip; under Option A also their blobs (kinds and counts);
  - *EV:* §6.1 — u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C (each a DIFF, then `patch-id`), in that order; then **the §6.2 enumeration `rev-list T --not I.tip`** (with its ≤ 5,000 check, C08), then the §6.2 records (pids of the §6.2 commits) in its output order (R18Δ; R27Δ, R26-04: the enumeration call is named and placed after §6.1 and before the record pids; Revision 18's canonical order already implied that position). **R26Δ (R25-02):** EV also issues §6.2's commit parses (`cat-file commit <Q>`, to decide np(Q) and Q^1), §6.3's lineage checks (`merge-base --is-ancestor <I.lineage_root> <Q>`), §6.5's ordering comparisons (`merge-base --is-ancestor <rep(e₁)> <rep(e₂)>`) and, under P-14, CR1's GitLab-pair predicates and CR2's ancestry tests — `Q_X ⊑ Q_M^2`, `Q_X ⋢ Q_M^1`, **and its exclusivity test that no other record of either kind lies in Q_M^2 \ Q_M^1** (R27Δ, R26-04). **These are not part of EV's specified read set or of the canonical order.** Which of them are issued, in what order, and whether any are skipped (§6.3 for every record or only for reverts; §6.5 by all pairs or by transitivity) is unspecified (L-30, UR-23);
  - *PR:* paths(C); entries of I.base, I.tip and T;
  - *CK:* paths(C); entries of Pre, Post, End, Tb, Tip, Tn, and the blobs of those six states for every C-text path (counts).
  - *DG (R18Δ):* entries of T and End at paths(C) (`drift`); `rev-list --count T --not I.tip -- paths(C)` (`later_commits`); `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z I.base I.tip` (`c_paths_in_unit`). Computed on **every** FINALIZE row; printed only where §1.11 says.
- **Canonical order (R18Δ, R17-04: one order, every call assigned; R24Δ, R23-06: for IA7 step 3 and FINALIZE only — before FINALIZE see "Order guarantees").** One sequence, in this order:
  1. **Pre-FINALIZE, IA7 step 3 (candidate anchoring) — R21Δ, R20-06: triggered when IA7 step 2 has found several candidates for the same X and the compiled options allow a non-empty AS for a squash ((a) with either UR-21 option, or (b) with Option A; never (b′) or (b) × B):** relevance (computed once, then reused by FINALIZE — a computed value is never re-read); then, for each candidate in ascending **(OID(seg_base), OID(S))** (byte order of the hex ids): its AS; **its AN stage 1 and stage 2 only if that AS ≠ ∅**.
  2. **FINALIZE:** **Bu** (Step 0; ancestry-merge only) → **relevance** (unless already computed in 1) → **AS** → **AN** stage 1 → AN stage 2 → **LD** (only if AN = ANCHORED) → **EV** (§6.1: u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C, each a DIFF then patch-id, in that order; then the §6.2 enumeration `rev-list T --not I.tip`; then the §6.2 record pids in its output order — R28Δ, R27-03: the enumeration named; the position was already implied) → **PR** → **CK** → **DG** (diagnostics: `drift`, then `later_commits`, then `c_paths_in_unit`).
  3. **Always / skipped:** Bu, AS, AN, LD as stated above; **EV, PR, CK and DG always run** in FINALIZE (also after K28/K29/K32/K34), whatever is later printed; DG values are printed only where §1.11's presence rules say, but DG is computed on every FINALIZE row.
  - **Within a computation:** paths in **byte order** of their names; for each path, states in the fixed order **Pre, Post, Bu, Ht, Fk, End, Tb, Tip, Tn** (absent ones skipped); for each state, the entry (`ls-tree`) before the blob; **each blob read is `cat-file -s <oid>` immediately followed, if the size is at most 8 MiB, by `cat-file blob <oid>`** (R19Δ, R18-07; over 8 MiB → C08 at that position, the blob is not read).
  - **Repeated calls (R21Δ, R20-02; R22Δ, R21-05: the end point is defined for every run).** The **sequence** is every git call the helper issues inside phaseC (§4), in issue order: preflight (its first call is `git version`), resolve_target, snap_refs (snap0b), decide() — the K01–K07 checks (for an **absent** C these include paths(C) at the K04 check and NB = `merge-base --all C T`; for a **present** C decide() does not compute paths(C)), IA1–IA7 — and FINALIZE. freshness() reads only the inputs listed in §1.6.1 and issues no git call. **The sequence ends with the last git call issued, in issue order, before the decision or failure that ends phaseC** (equivalently, the last git call before snap1) — R23Δ (R22-03): this replaces Revision 22's per-run gloss, which named the wrong call for K03 and named calls that do not exist for K11, K13/K14 and K25 step 2. The deciding step need not issue a call: a check on values already held (K03's np(C), K11's `refs = ∅`, K13/K14's `FR.fresh`, K25's distinct-X test, C12's graph predicates, a non-blob C08 count or time limit) ends the sequence at whatever call was issued last before it. On a FINALIZE run, the last issued call is normally DG's `c_paths_in_unit` `diff-tree`; in ancestry-direct mode it is `later_commits`' `rev-list --count` (see below). **R28Δ (R27-05):** "normally" means: when no unordered EV call (§6.2 parse, §6.3 lineage, §6.5 comparison, CR1/CR2 test) is issued after DG. The specification does not confine those calls to EV's slot, so the last call of a FINALIZE run is not determined when an implementation issues some of them later. Confining them to EV's slot, after the §6.2 record pids and before PR, is an **owner option under UR-23, not adopted**. §11.89 tabulates the cases. Where the order before the decision is unspecified (L-30), so is which call is last; the definition stays well formed because issue order is total on any run. **snap1's calls are outside the sequence:** they are always issued afresh, never answered from it, and **never subject to an argv-keyed [harness] injection**, so the final ref record is a fresh read for D02. If S0r = NONE, snap1 issues no snap_refs (§4). **Argv identity (R22Δ):** two calls have the same argv iff their argument vectors are byte-identical. Every revision operand is written as its full 40-hex object id (the empty tree as `4b825dc642cb6eb9a060e54bf8d69288fbee4904`), so two calls that name the same objects in the same positions with the same options have the same argv. **pid pipelines (R24Δ, R23-08; R26Δ, R25-07: the historical note now follows the rule it describes).** (Revision 23 did not state the cache key below; without it the cache would answer every `patch-id` stage from the first.) `patch-id --verbatim` has a constant argv and reads its input from DIFF, so it cannot be cached by its own argv. Each pid(A,B) = DIFF(A,B) | `patch-id --verbatim` is therefore **one cached unit keyed by DIFF(A,B)'s argv**. A repeated pid(A,B) is answered from the first result. An argv-keyed injection on a pid targets the DIFF argv, and names the stage it affects (the DIFF's exit, or the `patch-id` stage's output or exit). **R25Δ (R24-07):** a non-zero exit of either stage → C10; malformed `patch-id` output → C11 (§1.7.2, §6.2). A pid answered from the cache is not issued and does not count toward §1.12's pid-computation limit. **Exit precedence (R26Δ, R25-07):** if the `patch-id` stage both exits non-zero and prints malformed output, the result is **C10**. This follows from §1.3's mapping (any non-zero exit of a git call → C10); C11 applies only to a zero-exit malformed output. A call whose argv was already issued earlier in the sequence is answered from that earlier result and not issued again. An **argv-keyed [harness] injection therefore fails at the first occurrence of that argv** in the sequence, and only there. **Ancestry-direct coincidence (R22Δ, R21-05):** in ancestry-direct mode I.base = parent0(C) and I.tip = C, so DG's `c_paths_in_unit` call `diff-tree -r --no-renames --name-only -z <I.base> <I.tip>` has exactly paths(C)'s argv. For a present C, paths(C) is first issued by **relevance**, so DG's call is answered from relevance's result and never issued, and an injection keyed on that argv fires at relevance, not at DG (FX-C10g). DG's `drift` entries are answered from earlier reads in every mode (entries of End by relevance or LD, entries of T by PR). In ancestry-merge and squash modes the `c_paths_in_unit` argv names (P^1, P) or (S^1, M); it differs from paths(C), which names (parent0(C), C) with C ≠ P and C absent respectively, and from cand's `--cc` call, which runs only when no V entry covers C and so never precedes FINALIZE.
  - **Order guarantees (R23Δ, R22-03; R24Δ, R23-03/R23-04: determinacy restated, unspecified list completed; R25Δ, R24-03/R24-04: FP added, cached FINALIZE calls excluded).** The canonical order above, the whole-read-set discipline and a determinate first failure are specified **only for IA7 step 3 and FINALIZE**. **R25Δ (R24-04):** except where a FINALIZE call's argv may already have been issued by an unspecified pre-FINALIZE computation. Such a call is answered from that earlier result, so its failure (if any) arises before FINALIZE at a position that is not determined. Examples: a §6.2 record pid(parent0(Q), Q) for a non-merge Q that is also in SOTHER, whose DIFF argv a PMAP_O computation may have issued; u_C = pid(parent0(C), C) when parent0(C) = B_C, which IA3 may have computed as pid(B_C, C). With two genuine faults, which one is reported is then not determined. **R26Δ (R25-04): further examples — the list is illustrative, not exhaustive:** a §6.3 lineage check `merge-base --is-ancestor C <Q>` (present C, where lineage_root = C), which `oldest_fp_container` may already have issued for the same commit; a §6.2 record pid for the S of a later SSHAPE pair, pid(parent0(S), S), which is PMAP_S's key in IA2; a §6.2 parse `cat-file commit <Q>`, which IA2's classification of RANGE commits may already have issued (absent C); and u_C reached through IA5, pid(X_v, C) when X_v = parent0(C) for X2 = C (a condition distinct from parent0(C) = B_C). **R26Δ (R25-02):** separately, EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and (under P-14) CR1/CR2 checks (including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records; R28Δ, R27-03) are **not in the canonical order at all** (see the EV item in this paragraph's list; R27Δ, R26-05: self-reference removed), so FINALIZE's determinate first failure covers only the calls that are in it. Before FINALIZE, calls follow §4's **straight-line** steps in §4's order: preflight, resolve_target, snap_refs, then decide()'s checks — K01 (`cat-file -e C`), C15 (`cat-file -t C`), K02 (the parse of C), `C ⊑ T`, then for an absent C: K03, K04 (paths(C)), NB, K06/K07, IA1 (`for-each-ref --contains`, then E's `rev-list`), the endpoint check in ascending ord, then IA2. **Unspecified** (no order and no short-circuit rule is defined):
    - **preflight's Phase C checks** (R26Δ, R25-07): §1.4 lists C01–C07 without a first-match rule or internal call order. Where two conditions hold at once (for example, a bare repository with a non-sha1 object format: C02 or C03), which row is reported is unspecified. Whether to fix "first match in row order" is part of UR-23;
    - **(inside FINALIZE) EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and, under P-14, CR1/CR2 checks (including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records; R28Δ, R27-03)** (R26Δ, R25-02). Not in the canonical order. A **single** genuine fault in one of them can change the outcome between conforming implementations: for example, a natural timeout on `merge-base --is-ancestor <R1> <R3>` among three ordered reverts gives **C09** to an implementation that compares every pair, and **K18** to one that infers R1 ⊑ R3 by transitivity. A **proposed** explicit order — per §6.2 record in `rev-list` output order: parse Q → its pid → its lineage check if e is non-NULL; then every ordered pair of distinct representatives in rank order, with no transitivity shortcut; then CR1/CR2 under P-14 — is offered to the owner under UR-23 and is **not adopted**;
    - **FP's `rev-list --first-parent T`** (R25Δ, R24-03): **when** it is issued is unspecified — eagerly at decide() entry, or on first use by `oldest_fp_container` (present C) or by IA2 (absent C; SSHAPE needs FP ∩ RANGE). With |FP| > 20,000 the FP limit (C08) may therefore preempt K01–K07, K27 or K08 under one conforming implementation and not under another. Placing FP is part of UR-23;
    - **(present C) `oldest_fp_container`** — the search for P = the oldest X ∈ FP with C ⊑ X. It may issue up to |FP| `merge-base --is-ancestor C <X>` calls, linearly from either end or by bisection (R24Δ);
    - **(present C) C12's conjunction** `C ⊑ P^2 ∧ C ⋢ P^1`, including which `--is-ancestor` call is issued first and whether the second is skipped (R24Δ);
    - **IA1's parent reads** for the Kahn order on E (how and in which order the parents of each X ∈ E are read) (R24Δ);
    - the iteration order inside IA2–IA6: PMAP construction (eager or lazy), the V and shape-hit predicates with their `merge-base --is-ancestor` calls, IA5's search, IA6's covering tests;
    - the K10 shape-hit tests (`∃ h ∈ hits : covers(h, C)`), including whether they stop at the first covering hit;
    - cand's `rev-list --no-merges --full-history T --not NB -- paths(C)` and its `diff-tree --cc` loop over merges G ∈ RANGE, including whether `cand ≠ ∅` stops at the first member.

    **Consequences (R24Δ, R23-03: restated).**
    - **On a run in which no git call fails, times out or exceeds a limit**, the outcome does not depend on this order, because each row's condition is a set predicate.
    - **A genuine failure** — a repository fault such as a missing or corrupt object, a natural timeout (C09), or a limit (C08: stdout, pid computations, the 300 s total) — in a call that one admissible order or short-circuit reaches and another skips **can change the outcome between conforming implementations**. Examples: MERGED vs C10 when a lazily built PMAP_O never computes the pid of a corrupt SOTHER commit; K12 vs C09 in cand's `--cc` loop after a member is found; K10 vs C10 in the shape-hit tests. The specification does **not** guarantee identical outcomes in these cases.
    - **No false MERGED** arises from this. A call may be skipped only when no row condition needs its value: for example, PMAP_O is not needed when a V entry covers C, and a second covering hit is not needed once one is found. A MERGED reached by skipping such a call therefore rests on exactly the values its rows are defined on. An implementation that skipped a value a row condition needs would not conform.
    - **Not determined:** which call is issued last, which of two failures comes first, and whether an argv-keyed injection on a call there is reached. **R25Δ (R24-04):** also, which failure is reported when a FINALIZE call's argv may already have been issued before FINALIZE (a §6.2 record pid of a SOTHER commit; u_C when parent0(C) = B_C), and **R25Δ (R24-03)** whether the FP limit preempts an early-exit row.

    **Fixture constraint.** Fixtures inject **at most one** failure before FINALIZE, and only on a call or limit reached on every admissible order. The current pre-FINALIZE TF fixtures name such calls (FX-C09: NB's `merge-base --all <C> <T>`; FX-C10: C's tree object missing, first read by paths(C) at K04 for an absent C, or by relevance for a present C; FX-C12: `cat-file commit <P>` substituted to report one parent — straight-line after `oldest_fp_container` (R25Δ, R24-05); S9b, S11a–c: preflight and snapshot calls). FX-C11 and S11d now inject in EV, which is FINALIZE (R24Δ, R23-08; §11.102). For FX-C08a–j this is a **construction requirement**: the pid-count and total-time cases must exceed their limit under every admissible order and short-circuit. **R26Δ (R25-06):** for the **pid-count** case the requirement is **not known to be satisfiable**. Pids are counted only when issued (§1.12). The Revision 25 review bounded a lazy implementation's pid count at about 16,004 under §1.12's other limits, below 20,000; that bound is reasoned, not verified. That case is therefore relabelled **order-dependent / unproven** (FX-C08a–j row) and is **not** claimed to meet this constraint. P-19 (the counting rule) stays with the owner. Fixtures also inject on no EV call outside the canonical order (§6.2 parses, §6.3, §6.5, CR1/CR2).

    Whether to specify a pre-FINALIZE order is owner question **UR-23** (interim: unspecified; L-30). Nothing here is runtime behaviour; it is the extent of the specification.
- **First failure decides (R17Δ; R23Δ, R22-03: scope stated).** Execution stops at the **first** failing call or limit **in issue order**; its row is the result (C08 limit, C09 timeout, C10 git error, C11 patch-id, C12 graph check, C13 hook/direct I/O). Nothing after it runs, so a second injected failure cannot change the result. **Where the canonical order applies (IA7 step 3 and FINALIZE)**, issue order is that order and no implementation may reorder reads to reach a different first failure. **R25Δ (R24-04):** except where a FINALIZE call's argv may already have been issued by an unspecified pre-FINALIZE computation. Such a call is answered from that earlier result, so its failure (if any) arises before FINALIZE at a position that is not determined. Examples: a §6.2 record pid(parent0(Q), Q) for a non-merge Q that is also in SOTHER, whose DIFF argv a PMAP_O computation may have issued; u_C = pid(parent0(C), C) when parent0(C) = B_C, which IA3 may have computed as pid(B_C, C). With two genuine faults, which one is reported is then not determined. **R26Δ (R25-04): further examples — the list is illustrative, not exhaustive:** a §6.3 lineage check `merge-base --is-ancestor C <Q>` (present C, where lineage_root = C), which `oldest_fp_container` may already have issued for the same commit; a §6.2 record pid for the S of a later SSHAPE pair, pid(parent0(S), S), which is PMAP_S's key in IA2; a §6.2 parse `cat-file commit <Q>`, which IA2's classification of RANGE commits may already have issued (absent C); and u_C reached through IA5, pid(X_v, C) when X_v = parent0(C) for X2 = C (a condition distinct from parent0(C) = B_C). **R26Δ (R25-02):** separately, EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and (under P-14) CR1/CR2 checks (including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records; R28Δ, R27-03) are **not in the canonical order at all** ("Order guarantees", above; R27Δ, R26-05: was "below"), so FINALIZE's determinate first failure covers only the calls that are in it. **Before FINALIZE** issue order follows §4's sequential control flow, but order and short-circuiting **inside** the iterating computations listed under "Order guarantees" above (R24Δ, R23-06: was "below") are unspecified. Which of two failures there comes first is therefore not determined (L-30, UR-23).
- **Failure mapping (unchanged):** a non-zero exit of any git call → **C10** (§1.3), including `cat-file blob` and `ls-tree` used by any read above. **C13** arises only from a direct file read that is not a git call (none occurs in FINALIZE) or from a **[harness] phase hook** that raises an I/O error on entry to a named phase. Every TF-injection fixture names the exact git call or hook (FX-C13, FX-C13b, FX-C13c, FX-K28h, FX-C10b, FX-C10c, FX-C10d, FX-C09b, FX-C09c, FX-C10e, FX-C10f).

**Anchoring scope AS (UR-19).** AS is a subset of C's relevant paths.
- **Option (a) (interim):** AS = every relevant path, for ancestry-merge and squash. For ancestry-direct, AS = ∅.
- **Option (b) (R15Δ, R14-05):** AS = the relevant **count-landing-eligible** paths of a **squash** C, and only if UR-21 Option A is adopted.
