## 4. Pseudocode — R12Δ

```
main(argv):
  r := phaseA(argv)
  if r is failure: emit(r, snapshot=none, caveats=CV4); exit 2
  try:    S0f := snap_files()                            # snap0a
  except SnapshotError: emit(TF(snapshot-unreadable-initial, B01, provisional="-",
                                snapshot=none, caveats=CV4)); exit 2
  now := (--now value) if given else floor(time.time())
  (prov, S0r) := phaseC(S0f, now)
  if prov is K-result and S0r = NONE: prov := TF(internal, C14)            # I-1
  scope := "full" if S0r ≠ NONE else "files-only"
  if scope = "files-only" and prov is K-result: prov := TF(internal, C14)  # I-2
  try:
      S1f := snap_files(); S1r := snap_refs() if S0r ≠ NONE else NONE     # snap1
  except BaseException:
      final := TF(snapshot-unreadable-final, D01, provisional=prov.trace, snapshot=final-unreadable)
  else:
      if S1f ≠ S0f or (S0r ≠ NONE and (S1r.T, S1r.RH) ≠ (S0r.T, S0r.RH)):
          final := TF(snapshot-changed, D02, provisional=prov.trace, snapshot=scope)
      else:
          final := prov with snapshot=scope                                # D00
  final.caveats := caveats(final); final.recommend := recommend(final)
  emit(final); exit (2 if final is TF else 0)

phaseC(S0f, now) -> (prov, S0r):
  S0r := NONE
  try:
      CFG := preflight(); T := resolve_target(); S0r := snap_refs()
      FR := freshness(S0f, S0r, CFG, T, now, GF=CLOSED)
      return (decide(C, T, FR), S0r)
  except HelperFailure as f:  return (TF(f.code, f.trace), S0r)
  except BaseException:       return (TF(internal, C14), S0r)


decide(C, T, FR):
  if not cat-file -e C:              return K01
  if cat-file -t C ≠ commit:         raise C15
  if np(C) ≥ 3:                      return K02
  if C ⊑ T:
      P := oldest_fp_container(C)
      if P = C:                      I := direct(C)
      elif np(P) ≥ 3:                return K05
      elif np(P) = 2:
          if not (C ⊑ P^2 and C ⋢ P^1): raise C12
          I := via_merge(P)
      else:                          raise C12
      return finalize(I, FR)
  if np(C) = 2:                      return K03
  if paths(C) = ∅:                   return K04
  NB := merge-base --all C T
  if |NB| = 0:                       return K06
  if |NB| > 1:                       return K07
  refs, E := IA1(C, T)
  for X in E (ascending ord):
      n := |merge-base --all X T|
      if n = 0:                      return K27
      if n > 1:                      return K08
  IA2(); V, hits := IA3_IA4(E); V := IA5(E, V)
  covering := { v ∈ V : covers(v, C) }                                      # IA6
  if covering ≠ ∅:
      Cmin := argmin_pos(covering)                                          # IA7
      if |distinct X in Cmin| > 1:   return K25
      if |Cmin| > 1 and AN results of Cmin differ (each with its own AS):  return K25   # IA7 step 3: candidate anchoring (not Step 0)
      return finalize(select(Cmin), FR)
  if ∃ h ∈ hits : covers(h, C):      return K10
  if refs = ∅:                       return K11
  if cand(C, NB, V) ≠ ∅:             return K12
  if FR.fresh = yes:                 return K13
  return K14

anchoring(I):                         # §1.7.3 Step 1; scope per UR-19 (interim: option (a)); Bu first, then relevance (REL) and AS (R18Δ)
  if I.via = ancestry-direct:        return ANCHORED            # no git call; covers a root C
  if I.via = ancestry-merge:
      Bu := merge-base --all P^1 P^2
      if |Bu| = 0: return BU_NONE
      if |Bu| > 1: return BU_MULTI
  AS := in_scope_paths(I)           # R15Δ: (a) every relevant path; (b) squash ∧ Option A ∧ count-landing-eligible
  if AS = ∅:                          return ANCHORED            # Ht, Fk not computed; K34/K32 unreachable
  if np(C) = 0:                      return FK_UNDET            # no merge-base call with a tree
  Ht := Bu if (I.via = ancestry-merge or first-integration squash)
        else single(merge-base --all X_v T)  or return FK_UNDET
  Fk := Pre if Ht = Pre else single(merge-base --all Ht Pre)  or return FK_UNDET
  return ANCHORED if ∀ p ∈ AS: entry(Ht,p) = entry(Fk,p) else NOT_ANCHORED

landing(I):                           # §1.7.3 Step 2; per relevant path
  direct:  landed                                                 (by construction; no type guard — R13Δ, R12-05)
  merge:   r = e → landed; t = ub → differs; else unverifiable          (type guard first)
  squash:  r = e → landed
           else if UR-21 = Option A and p landing-textual and C's surviving change at p has no mode change and p has a touched line (R20Δ):
                guard (∀ touched L: Tb(L) = Bu(L)) fails → unverifiable
                carried (S_A ≤ End − Bu; S_R ≤ Bu − End; R14Δ) holds → landed, else not-carried
           else → unverifiable                                          (interim: Option B)

finalize(I, FR):
  AN := anchoring(I)    # BU_NONE | BU_MULTI | FK_UNDET | ANCHORED | NOT_ANCHORED
  LD := landing(I) if AN = ANCHORED else NONE
  EV := events(I); PR := paths_reverted(I); CK := content(I)   # always computed (R15Δ); LD above only if anchored
  DG := diagnostics(I)                                          # always computed (R18Δ); order: Bu → REL → AS → AN → LD → EV (§6.1; §6.2 enumeration; §6.2 record pids — R28Δ) → PR → CK → DG
  if AN = BU_NONE:                   return K28
  if AN = BU_MULTI:                  return K29
  if AN = FK_UNDET:                  return K34
  if AN = NOT_ANCHORED:              return K32
  if LD.differs > 0:                 return K30
  if LD.unverifiable > 0:            return K31
  if LD.not_carried > 0:             return K33                 # Option A only
  if EV = UNDETERMINABLE:            return K15
  if EV = UNORDERED:                 return K16
  if EV = INCOMPARABLE:              return K17
  if EV.kind_final = revert:         return K18
  if EV.has_revert:                  return K26
  if PR > 0:                         return K19
  if CK = unassessable:              return K20
  if CK = superseded:                return K21
  if CK = insufficient:              return K22
  if FR.fresh = yes:                 return K23
  return K24
```

**Mapping**
- Every return or raise site maps to exactly one §3 row, and every terminal row has at least one site.
- C12 has two raise sites; the git-derived C rows have many.
- **R16Δ (R15-02, R15-03):** after BU_NONE/BU_MULTI, AS, AN and LD are not computed; EV, PR, CK still run (none reads Bu). Every computation that runs reads its whole read set (§1.7.3); a failing git call is C10, a harness phase hook or direct file I/O failure is C13.
- **R15Δ (R14-01):** Bu, then AN, then LD **only if AN = ANCHORED**, then EV, PR and CK **always** are computed before any decision. A C row raised by a computation that runs preempts the K rows; LD, when skipped, raises nothing.
- AN cannot yield BU_NONE or BU_MULTI for direct or squash, because Bu is Pre or a single seg_base.
- **R12Δ:** `merge-base` is never called with the empty tree (R11-09). **R14Δ:** AS (the in-scope set) is every relevant path under UR-19 option (a) (empty for direct); under option (b) it holds the relevant count-landing-eligible paths of a squash C (landing-textual, r ≠ e, a touched line (S(i)), no surviving mode change (S(iii)) — R21Δ), and only if UR-21 Option A is adopted (R15Δ). Bu (K28/K29) is structural and computed before AS.
- **R13Δ:** relevance (§1.7.3) reads Pre, Post and End only. Bu, Tb, Tip and Tn are read only by anchoring, landing and content. Line counts are taken only on textual states.
- **R12Δ:** in IA7, candidates for the same X with equal pids have equal End(L) − Bu(L) for every L (I-16) and the same Tb, so they also give the same guard and carried results; comparing anchoring is sufficient.

## 6. Revert and event-ordering specification

**6.1 Patches**
- u_I = pid(I.base, I.tip); u_C = pid(parent0(C), C); r_I = pid(I.tip, I.base); r_C = pid(C, parent0(C)).
- U = {u_I, u_C} \ {NULL}; Rv = {r_I, r_C} \ {NULL}.
- If U ∪ Rv = ∅ → K15.

**6.2 Records.** For each commit Q ∈ `rev-list T --not I.tip` (≤ 5,000, else C08):
- e = pid(parent0(Q), Q) if np(Q) ≤ 1, else pid(Q^1, Q).
- A NULL e means no record. A pid failure → C11. **R25Δ (R24-07), made precise:** a **malformed** `patch-id` output (anything but 0 lines or one line matching §1.7.2's pattern) → **C11**; a **non-zero exit** of the DIFF or of the `patch-id` stage → **C10** (§1.3's failure mapping, as for every git call; FX-C10f).
- Kind: **revert** if e ∈ Rv (this takes precedence when e is also in U); **reapply** if e ∈ U.

**6.3 Lineage** [P-13 · PDB: as stated; if rejected: ignore out-of-lineage reverts]
- A record qualifies iff I.lineage_root ⊑ Q.
- A revert with I.lineage_root ⋢ Q → K16.

**6.4 Event identity and carrier rules (R10-10)** [P-14 · PDB: no deduplication, so every record is its own event]

If P-14 is adopted, exactly two collapses apply, and nothing else is ever collapsed:
- **CR1 (GitLab-pair carrier).** Records for Q_M and Q_S, where (Q_S, Q_M) is a GitLab pair and both have the same kind and the same e, form one event with representative Q_S.
- **CR2 (`--no-ff` carrier).** A record for a merge Q_M (np = 2) and exactly one record Q_X with Q_X ⊑ Q_M^2, Q_X ⋢ Q_M^1, the same kind and the same e — and no other record of either kind in Q_M^2 \ Q_M^1 — form one event with representative Q_X.

**6.5 Ordering** [P-15 · PDB: total order required]
- e₁ precedes e₂ iff rep(e₁) ⊑ rep(e₂) and rep(e₁) ≠ rep(e₂). Timestamps are never used.
- No total order → K17.
- has_revert := at least one revert event exists.

**6.6 Decision order.** These run after anchoring and landing: K15 → K16 → K17 → K18 → K26 [P-22] → K19.

**6.7 Detection boundary**

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| Exact revert | K18 |
| Exact revert then reapply | K26 (PDB) |
| Unrelated change identical to an inverse | revert |
| Partial or drift revert | not an event; backstops are K19, K22, CV2 |
| Semantic revert | not detected |
| Revert outside lineage | K16 |
| No total order | K17 |

Absence of a detected revert never proves that there is none.

## 7. Fixture catalogue — R12Δ (complete; all [P], unexecuted)

### 7.0 Baselines

**Φ (single setup)**
- One scratch repository. No bare origin, no fetch, no remote contact.
- `remote.origin.url = https://example.invalid/team/repo.git` (OU class network-form).
- `remote.origin.fetch = +refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*`; origin is the only remote.
- None of the OU1 keys is set.
- Commits are created locally. `refs/remotes/origin/*`, `logs/refs/remotes/origin/*` and FETCH_HEAD are **written directly as files**, with mtimes set via `os.utime`.
- `--now 2026-01-01T12:00:00Z`, except in FX-CLK2.
- **Default content:** C adds one line to f (K = 1, J = 0, basis added).
- **Default shape:** a GitLab pair; C is the tip of `refs/remotes/origin/br`; no back-merges (seg_base = B_X ⊑ Pre).
- **Default target:** the target did not touch C's paths between the fork and the integration, so entry(Tip, p) = entry(End, p) and every relevant path is landed by state equality.

| Variant | Definition | Freshness result |
|---|---|---|
| Φ-fresh (default) | TR has one line `fetch origin: fast-forward`, new = T, ts = now − 3600 | R7/F01/Cb5, fresh=yes, observed_at=2026-01-01T11:00:00Z |
| Φ-stale | as Φ-fresh with ts = now − 259200 | stale; observed_at=2025-12-29T12:00:00Z |
| Φ-none | no TR and no FETCH_HEAD | R1/F01/Cb8 |

**Common expectations** (unless stated otherwise):
- `snapshot=full`
- `origin_identity=unpinned` — **interim only.** Under a mandatory pin with no pin given it is `pin-missing` on every classified line (§2.2). This is why no classified fixture is labelled B (R11-08).
- `origin_url_class=network-form`
- `freshness_source=reflog` when fresh=yes

**Caveat rule (§1.10):**

| Outcome | Caveats |
|---|---|
| MERGED | CV1 iff basis ∈ {added, removed}; CV2 iff j_inc > 0; CV3 iff superseded > 0; CV6 iff recommend ∈ {yes, historical}; CV7 iff UR-19 is compiled as (b) or (b′) and CV8 does not apply; CV8 iff (b) × A, squash, AS ≠ ∅ (R20Δ) |
| LIVE | CV6 |
| Other K rows | - |
| TF | CV4 iff snapshot ≠ full |

**Owner-dependency rules**
- **P-25(i):** every fixture whose R chain ends at R7 with Cb5 is **O(P-25i)**; its stated expectation is the interim one. Under a mandatory pin, apply §2.2: K13 → K14; K23 → K24 with recommend=no; historical → no; CV6 removed; observed_at → none; origin_identity → pin-missing.
- **UR-21 (R12Δ):** a fixture labelled **O(UR-21)** states the interim (Option B) expectation first and the Option A expectation second.
- **UR-19 (R12Δ; R17Δ):** a fixture labelled **O(UR-19)** states the interim (option (a)) expectation first, the option (b) expectation second, and the option (b′) expectation third wherever it differs from (b).
- **UR-22 (R22Δ, R21-04):** a fixture labelled **O(UR-22)** states the interim expectation first, then Option A, then Option B, then Option C wherever C differs from both A and B (otherwise C is named with the option it equals). Where only a caveat differs, the outcome is binding across the branches and the label says "CV3 O UR-22".
- **Interim combination (R22Δ):** a fixture's interim expectation is computed under all three interims together: UR-19 (a), UR-21 Option B and the UR-22 interim strict rule.

**Text dependencies:** CV texts depend on P-21; **the CV1 text variant depends on UR-19 and UR-21** (R15Δ); **CV3's emission (not its text) depends on UR-22 through superseded** (R22Δ); wording on P-18. **[harness]** marks harness-injected fixtures.

### 7.6 Real cases (runtime only; separately authorised; [M])

[M] SPSM, measured 2026-10-15: 1385, 1454 and 1355 were each integrated as a GitLab squash pair (3/3 squash). The reflog shows `fetch --all --prune`.

| Case | Commits | Integration | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| RC-1..RC-4 | `f1f8b559e`, `ff8c5f308`, `5f7f21871`, `eea0df44a` (1385) | S `04dd54f2a` / M `d74c26e9c` | MERGED via squash if, at every relevant path, the integrated file equals the branch end; or K21/K22/K25; or **K31** if development touched the same files before the squash (interim, UR-21; under Option A: MERGED or K33); or K32 (only if a back-merge after their fork absorbed target changes on their files, including a back-merged squash of the same MR's earlier segment). Earlier commits in the same MR, and earlier squashed segments, do not cause K32. |
| RC-5 | `2f4c63190` (1454) | S `3aa8d0351` / M `895d1d6c2` | as above |
| RC-6 | `b49fdeaeb`, `af7a486d9` (1355) | S `95fffeeb4` / M `aa69d47f4` | as above |
| RC-7 | `96e1eb8e5`, `7c4553875` (1455; not integrated) | — | K13/K14, or K11 under the P-04 PDB |

Freshness for these is likely fresh=no: R6 applies to `fetch --all` under the P-12 PDB. Status: UR-3 plus separate authorisation.

## 8. Acceptance criteria — R12Δ

PV = paper-verifiable design criteria. RT = runtime, only after separately authorised implementation. **No RT criterion has been evaluated.**

| ID | Kind | Criterion (pass iff) | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| PV-01 | PV | **59** terminal rows each have at least one fixture; **12** are conditional (owner-dependent: C08, K08, K13, K16, K21, K23, K25, K26, K32, K33, K34; verification-dependent: K15); **47** have a binding-outcome fixture (design coverage) | B |
| PV-02 | PV | Fixture IDs are unique; each has one trace (or, for O fixtures, one trace per branch) and a §0 status | B |
| PV-03 | PV | Each §4 site maps to one §3 row; each terminal row has at least one site; the order matches §1.8.3 | B |
| PV-04 | PV | Every token is defined in §1.11 | B |
| PV-05 | PV | Every R1–R10, F01–F24 and Cb1–Cb9 row has a fixture or is declared gated/decision-only | B |
| PV-06 | PV | **R12Δ:** no B fixture asserts a field that depends on an open item. Classified fixtures are at most Bo, because `origin_identity` depends on P-25(i). Every field in the §2.2 mapping is either unchanged for the fixture or covered by its label. Every P has a PDB and an if-rejected behaviour; every owner question used by a fixture (UR-19, UR-21, UR-22 — R22Δ, R21-04) has an interim behaviour. | B |
| PV-07 | PV | §8.3 is consistent with B-01..B-19 | B |
| PV-08 | PV | §1.10 covers every outcome × fresh × observed_at × snapshot combination | B |
| PV-09 | PV | One domain per field per trace; denominators defined; paths_unlanded classes disjoint, 0/0 permitted, `-` on K28/K29/K32/K34 | B |
| PV-10 | PV | IA7 tie-breaks are total; distinct X → K25; a seg_base anchoring disagreement → K25 (only while anchoring is in scope) | B |
| PV-11 | PV | G-F CLOSED: no interim fresh=yes from FETCH_HEAD; no F18, Cb4, Cb6 or Cb9 in B or interim expectations | B |
| PV-12 | PV | Every P has a PDB, an if-rejected behaviour and inline tags | B |
| PV-13 | PV | Classified lines are `full`; `files-only` only on TF lines; CV4 on every non-`full` line | B |
| PV-14 | PV | Every §7 caveat list equals the §1.10 rule applied to the fixture. Positive and negative fixtures exist for CV1, CV2, CV4, CV5 (negative) and CV6. The CV3 positive is conditional (P-06). **R16Δ:** CV7 positives are conditional (UR-19 (b)/(b′): FX-K23ai, K23ar); its negative is every (a) MERGED. | B |
| PV-15 | PV | The argv model is total | B |
| PV-16 | PV | RA accepts the listed forms; WF ⊂ RA | B |
| **PV-17** | PV | **R13Δ:** in force (interim), **no landing conclusion uses line counts**: every relevant path is landed only by state equality, or by construction in direct mode. **Relevance reads Pre, Post and End only.** No MERGED unless anchored (scope UR-19) and every relevant path is landed. FX-K31h, K31i, K31j, K31k, **K31l, K31m, K31n** → K31 under every option; FX-K22i → K22; FX-K32a/c → K32 under (a), K31 under (b); FX-K32b/d → K32 under (a) and under (b) × A, K31 under (b) × B; FX-K23ag, ah, al, an, ao, ap → MERGED. Option A, if adopted, lands only landing-textual squash paths, only with the guard and the corrected carried check: FX-K23aj/am/aq → MERGED; FX-K33b/c/d → K33; FX-K31n → K31. | B |

**8.2 V-W (W measurement)**
- 10, 100 and 1000 branches × idle, loaded and large-pack conditions.
- ≥ 50 whole fetches per combination, each moving at least one ref.
- δ = mtime_ns − 10⁹ · ts; D = max\|δ\| + 1 s; W = ⌈2D⌉.
- 120 s is acceptable only if ⌈2D⌉ ≤ 120 and every sample lies within ±W.
- The owner approves.

## 11. Adversarial re-derivations (author's, on paper; not independent)

These are paper derivations only. No fixture was executed. Where a step depends on unverified git behaviour, the UR item is named.

### 11.1 R11-02 exact history (FX-K31h squash, FX-K31i merge)

1. **States.** F0: f lacks L; g = g0. Branch: C0 (adds L to f; edits g to g1), then C (removes L). C is the tip of `refs/remotes/origin/br`. Target: Hr adds the same L to f (F0 → Hr). Then the GitLab squash S, with S^1 = Hr, and M = merge(Hr, S). T = M.
2. **IA1.** E = {C0, C}; NB = F0; B_X = F0 for both endpoints; refs = {br}.
3. **IA2–IA4.** pid(F0, C0) = (f: +L; g) ≠ S's pid. pid(F0, C) = (g only), because f nets to zero over F0 → C. The squash's 3-way merge has base F0, ours Hr and theirs C. At f, theirs = base, so Hr's f is kept, and S's diff against Hr is the g change only. Same pid, GitLab pair, F0 ⊑ Hr → V entry (C, F0, S, M).
4. **IA6–IA7.** Covers C (C ⊑ C; C ⋢ F0). Single entry. I = squash; Tb = Hr; Tip = M; End = C; Bu = F0.
5. **Anchoring.** First-integration: Ht = Bu = F0 ⊑ Pre = C0 → Fk = F0 → trivially anchored. Not evidence.
6. **Relevance.** paths(C) = {f}. f is text in all seven states. Pre(L) = 1, Post(L) = 0, End(L) = 0 → R(L) = 1, S_R(L) = min(1, max(0, 1 − 0)) = 1 → L is touched; f is relevant by (i).
7. **Landing at f.** r = entry(M, f) = Hr's f (has L); e = entry(C, f) = F0's f (lacks L); r ≠ e.
   - **Interim (Option B):** r ≠ e → unverifiable.
   - **Option A:** f is text; no mode change; not (iv)-only. Guard: Tb(L) = Hr(L) = 1, Bu(L) = F0(L) = 0 → 1 ≠ 0 → **unverifiable**. (The Revision 11–13 removal check would have passed: min(S_R, Bu(L)) = 0 ≤ 0. The Revision 14 check fails as well: S_R = 1 > 0.)
8. **Decision.** No differs; one unverifiable → **K31**, paths_unlanded = 1/1. The landed path g took no part: g ∉ paths(C). Even if C had also changed g, a landed g could not offset f (§1.7.3, "unrelated evidence cannot mask").
9. **UR-19 (b).** Anchoring is out of scope, and the result is the same (K31).
10. **Merge variant (FX-K31i).** P = `merge --no-ff` (Hr, C). Bu = merge-base(Hr, C) = F0 ⊑ Pre → anchored. f: ub = F0(f) lacks L; t = Hr(f) has L; e lacks L; r = merge result: the branch side equals the base at f, so the target side wins → has L. r ≠ e ∧ t ≠ ub → unverifiable → **K31**. **Squash and merge now agree** (Revision 11: squash MERGED, merge K31).
11. **Can the output still claim MERGED here?** MERGED needs every relevant path landed. f is landed only if r = e (both options) or, under Option A, if the guard holds. Here r ≠ e and the guard fails. So no option yields MERGED. **The stop condition "R11-02 can still produce MERGED" is not met.**

### 11.2 Genuine removal correctly integrated (FX-K23am; state-equality control FX-K23ad)

1. F0: f holds L (line 5) and line 30 = Y0. Branch: C removes L (X = C). Target: Hr changes line 30 to Y1. Squash onto Hr: the 3-way merge removes L and keeps Y1. S's diff against Hr removes L with the same three context lines as F0 → C (line 30 is far away) → pid equal [UR-16 for the exact pid behaviour]. V entry (C, F0, S, M).
2. Anchored trivially (Ht = F0 = Pre's merge base).
3. f relevant: S_R(L) = 1.
4. r = Hr(f) − L; e = F0(f) − L; r ≠ e (line 30).
   - **Interim:** unverifiable → **K31** (a disclosed L-21 cost: C's removal *is* present).
   - **Option A:** guard Tb(L) = Hr(L) = 1 = Bu(L) = 1 ✓. Removal check (R14Δ form): S_R = 1 ≤ max(0, Bu(L) − End(L)) = 1 ✓ → landed. By I-16, Tip(L) = Tb(L) + End(L) − Bu(L) = 1 + 0 − 1 = 0. Content: K = 0, J = 1, j_abs: Tn(L) = 0 → 1/1; j_inc = min(1, max(0, Tn(L) − Tip(L))) = 0 → state 4 sufficient (P-07 PDB) → **MERGED K23**, basis=removed.
5. **State-equality removal control (FX-K23ad):** the same C with the target not touching f → r = e → landed in every option → MERGED. This is the binding-outcome removal positive in force (O P-25i only for freshness).

### 11.5 Chained / stacked history (R11-04) and the unrelated back-merge control

**FX-K23al (chained only).**
1. Branch br: F0 → A0 (g) → A1 (f line 10) = X1. MR 1: S1 = (g, f10), M1. Sub-branch from A0: C (f line 50). X2 = merge(X1, C), with tree = A1 + f50. MR 2: 3-way merge (base merge-base(M1, X2) = F0; ours M1; theirs X2) is clean, so S2 = f50.
2. IA1: E = {A0, A1, C, X2} (all absent from T). Each endpoint has merge-base(X, T) = F0, which is unique.
3. IA3–IA4: pid(F0, A1) = (g, f10) = S1 → V1 = (A1, F0, S1, M1); V1 does not cover C. pid(F0, C) = (g, f50) and pid(F0, X2) = (g, f10, f50) match nothing.
4. IA5: X2 with v = V1 (A1 ⊑ X2): pid(A1, X2) = f50 = S2; M1 ⊑ S2^1 = M1; pos(M2) > pos(M1) → V2 = (X2, A1, S2, M2). Covers C: C ⊑ X2, C ⋢ A1.
5. IA7: single entry. Bu = A1 (X_v). **Ht = merge-base(A1, T) = F0**. Pre = A0. Fk = merge-base(F0, A0) = F0. entry(F0, f) = entry(F0, f) → **anchored**.
6. Landing: r = M2's f = F0 + f10 + f50; e = X2's f = F0 + f10 + f50 → r = e → landed → **MERGED**.
7. **Revision 11 rule:** Fk = merge-base(A1, A0) = A0; entry(A1, f) (has f10) ≠ entry(A0, f) → K32. That was the R11-04 false block. **Ht removes it without changing the first-integration, merge or direct cases**, where Ht = Bu.

**Reviewer's exact history (FX-K25d).** The sub-branch forks at F0, so pid(F0, C) = f50 = S2 and (C, F0, S2, M2) is also a covering V entry. Cmin then has two distinct X (C and X2) → K25 under the P-24 PDB. Under (a) the chained entry is chosen → anchored via Ht → MERGED. Under (b) → (C, F0) → MERGED. So the R11-04 false block appeared only under P-24(a), and is now gone there too.

**Unrelated back-merge (FX-K23ag), re-derived.** B_X = Hu = Ht (first-integration). Fk = merge-base(Hu, C0) = F0. entry(Hu, f) = entry(F0, f) because Hu touched only h → anchored. Tip(f) = End(f) → landed → MERGED. Unchanged from Revision 11.

**Does the Ht change introduce a new false MERGED?** Under the interim landing rule, MERGED needs r = e on every relevant path whatever anchoring says. Anchoring can only remove MERGED results, never create them. Under Option A, I-16 plus the guard give Tip(L) = End(L) on touched lines whatever the anchoring result. So weakening a false block cannot produce an unsound landing. It can only restore attribution where Ht's absorbed target content left C's paths untouched.

### 11.6 Move / reorder (R11-05)

**FX-K31k.** f: blob(Pre) ≠ blob(Post); for every line A = R = 0; End(f) = Post(f) → relevant by (iv). Merge: ub = F0(f); t = Hr's f ≠ ub; r = Hr's f (the resolver took it) ≠ e → unverifiable → **K31**. g: landed. Result **K31** (Revision 11: f not relevant → MERGED). Squash variant: r ≠ e → unverifiable in both options (an (iv)-only path is never count-landed) → K31.

**FX-K23an.** Target untouched: r = e at f and g → landed. Content: K = 1 (g) → MERGED. CV1 states that reordering is not re-checked after integration.

**FX-K20b (whitespace-only additions).** f relevant by (iv); r = e → landed; content state 1 (ΣA + ΣR + #C-opaque = 0) → **K20**, unchanged.

### 11.7 K34 reachability (R11-03)

**First-integration squash under the P-02 PDB (the reviewer's proof, re-checked).** Here Bu = Ht = B_X, the unique merge base of the endpoint X with T, and Pre ⊑ C ⊑ X. Either Pre ∈ E or Pre ⊑ T. If Pre ∈ E, the endpoint check has made merge-base --all Pre T = {m} unique (otherwise K08 or K27 earlier). If Pre ⊑ T, m := Pre. Every common ancestor of (Ht, Pre) is a common ancestor of (T, Pre), so it is ⊑ m. Also m ⊑ Pre ⊑ X and m ⊑ T, so m ⊑ B_X, which makes m a common ancestor of (Ht, Pre). Hence Fk = {m}, always unique → **K34 is unreachable for first-integration squash**. The Revision 11 FX-K34 (criss-cross with Hb's history) gives |merge-base Pre T| ≥ 2 at the endpoint loop → **K08**, before FINALIZE.

**FX-K34a (ancestry merge).**
1. Target FP: F0 → Ka → Mt → P, with Mt = merge(Ka, Kb). Branch: F0 → Kb → Mb = merge(Kb, Ka) → C → X = merge(C, Mt).
2. present(C): C ⊑ P. P = oldest FP commit containing C, because Mt does not contain C. np(P) = 2; C ⊑ P^2 = X; C ⋢ P^1 = Mt → ancestry-merge.
3. Bu = merge-base --all Mt X = {Mt} (Mt ⊑ X) → not K28/K29. Ht = Bu = Mt.
4. Pre = Mb. Ht ≠ Pre. Fk = merge-base --all Mt Mb = {Ka, Kb}. Ka and Kb are both common ancestors, neither is an ancestor of the other, and no common ancestor is newer → |Fk| = 2 → **K34**.
5. Under UR-19 (b): anchoring is not evaluated for merges. Landing at f: the target never touched f → r = e → MERGED.

**Fixture order.** K28/K29 (Bu) → K34 (Ht/Fk) → K32 → landing. FX-K34a has |Bu| = 1, so it reaches K34.

**Frequency.** K34 needs a criss-cross between the absorbed target base and C's parent, a non-unique merge-base(X_v, T) for a chained match, or a root C. All are rare. Not measured.

### 11.8 Opaque restore (FX-K31f, R11-06)

1. F0: bin1 = v0 (binary); g = g0. C0: bin1 → v1, g → g1. C: bin1 → v0 (X = C). Target Hr: bin1 → v2.
2. Unit diff F0 → C: g only (bin1 nets to v0). Squash 3-way at bin1: base v0, ours v2, theirs v0 → keep v2. S's diff against Hr is g only → pid equal → V entry.
3. bin1 is C-opaque (binary in Pre and Post). Rule (ii): entry(End, bin1) = v0 = entry(Post, bin1) ≠ entry(Pre, bin1) = v1 → relevant.
4. r = v2 ≠ e = v0 → unverifiable in both options → **K31**.
5. The Revision 11 FX-K31f (v0 → v1 on the branch, v2 → v3 in S) could not get a V entry under UR-16. It would be K12, as the reviewer showed.

### 11.9 Mode-only pids (R11-07)

- **FX-K20c:** the unit F0 → C contains g (from C0) and f's mode change → pid non-NULL under either UR-11 answer → V entry. u_I = pid(S^1, S) is non-NULL, so U ≠ ∅ → not K15. f is relevant by (iii); r = e → landed; content state 1 → **K20**.
- **FX-K31g:** the same unit construction; the target edited f's content → r ≠ e; a mode change in C's surviving change → unverifiable (both options) → **K31**.
- **FX-K15:** a mode-only C on FP (ancestry-direct). u_I = u_C = pid(parent0(C), C), and r_I/r_C are the reverses. All four are NULL iff UR-11 says a hunkless patch has no patch-id → K15. Otherwise events are evaluated normally. **V(UR-11)**, the only fixture that still depends on it.

### 11.10 P-25(i)-dependent fields (R11-08)

| Fixture group | Interim | Mandatory pin, no pin given | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| TF lines (Phase A/B/C/D) | no origin_identity field | unchanged | B |
| R1–R6, R8, R9 terminated (K24b, K14c, A05d, CLK2, R2–R6, R8a, R9\*, F04, F05, F06, F09, Cb2x) | identity unpinned | identity pin-missing; nothing else | Bo |
| AMBIGUOUS / GONE on Φ-fresh (K01–K12, K17–K22, K27–K31 fixtures, …) | R7/Cb5, fresh=yes | R10/Cb7, fresh=no; identity pin-missing; outcome, trace, recommend, caveats unchanged | Bo |
| R7 with Cb1/Cb3 (F02, F03, F21, K14b, K24c interim, F19/F20 interim) | RROW R7 | RROW R10; identity pin-missing; outcome unchanged | B(outcome); RROW O(P-25i) |
| R7 with Cb5 (K13\*, K23\*, K24a, R7\*, OU+\*, F-STALE\*, …) | fresh/historical | Cb7; K13 → K14, K23 → K24; recommend=no; CV6 removed | O(P-25i) |
| FX-S2, FX-S3 (D01/D02 with provisional) | Φ-none → provisional=K24 | unchanged (R1 decides) | B |

**AMBIGUOUS and GONE fixtures on Φ-fresh** (for example the K12 or K31 fixtures): the R chain ends at R7 with Cb5, so under a mandatory pin `fresh`, `observed_at`, `freshness_source`, `freshness_trace`, `freshness_reason` and `origin_identity` change, while outcome, trace, recommend (no) and caveats (-) do not. The Bo definition (§0) therefore treats every freshness field as an interim value that follows §2.2; no Bo fixture asserts a freshness field as binding.

### 11.11 Root commit (R11-09)

- **FX-K23v (C is T's root commit, on FP):** P = C → ancestry-direct. Pre = the empty tree. AN = ANCHORED with **no git call**. Landing by construction. paths(C) = `diff-tree … <empty tree> C` (diff-tree accepts a tree-ish). entry(Pre, p) = ⊥. Pre(L) = 0. Events: u_C = pid(empty tree, C) is non-NULL. → MERGED K23 (O P-25i).
- **FX-K34b (root C reached by a merge):** ancestry-merge; Bu = Ht = Ht0 (the back-merged target commit); np(C) = 0 → Fk := ∅ with **no git call** → K34. Under (b): MERGED.
- A root C that is absent from T: NB = ∅ → K06 before any anchoring.

### 11.12 FX-K33b and FX-K25c (R11-12)

- **FX-K33b:** unit F0 → C = g only (f nets to F0's text: C removed C0's copy). pid(F0, C) = g = pid(Hr, S) → V entry (the R11-12 NULL-pid gap is closed). The rest is in §11.3.
- **FX-K25c:** derived in the §7.2 row. Checks: E = {A0, A1, Bm, C, X}. Each endpoint's merge base with T is unique: F0 for A0, A1 and C; M1 for Bm and X. pid(M1, Bm) = NULL (dropped). First-integration V2 needs B_X = M1 ⊑ S2^1 = M1 ✓. Chained V3 needs A1 ⊑ X ✓, M1 ⊑ S2^1 ✓, pos(M2) > pos(M1) ✓. Equal pids ⇒ equal Bu(L) for every L (§4 note), so the guard cannot separate them; only anchoring differs.

### 11.13 Classification and path binding (R11-13; R13Δ after R12-01)

- **Three separate concepts (R13Δ).** (1) *Branch-side relevance* reads Pre, Post and End only (§1.7.3). (2) *State kind* is defined per state and per path (text, binary, symlink, gitlink, tree, absent). (3) *Landing* reads every state, and line counts are allowed only on a **landing-textual** path (Pre, Post, Bu, End, Tb, Tip all textual). Content counts additionally need a textual Tn.
- Revision 12 used one seven-state "opaque" class for all three. That let a target-side state remove a surviving branch change from relevance (R12-01). In Revision 13 a target-side non-textual state can only (a) stop count landing (the path is landed by state equality or is unverifiable) or (b) make content insufficient (K22). It can never make a path irrelevant.
- No count identity is assumed on a non-textual state: X(L) is never computed for one.
- Path binding: Option A needs pid equality to imply the same change **at the same path** (I-16 per path). This is in UR-16 and V-UR16(a). It is not asserted for git 2.43. Under the interim rule, path binding does not affect landing (state equality compares the path's own entry).

### 11.14 Coverage, presence, caveat order, consistency

- **Coverage arithmetic:** 8 + 1 + 15 + 8 + 25 + 2 = 59. Conditional: owner-dependent 11 (C08, K08, K13, K16, K21, K23, K25, K26, K32, K33, K34) + verification 1 (K15) = 12. 59 − 12 = **47**.
- **Binding-outcome fixtures for the 47 rows**, checked row by row: A01–A08, B01, C01–C07, C09–C15 (B); K01–K07, K27 (Bo); K10, K11, K12, K14 (K14c), K28, K29, K30, K31, K17, K18, K19, K20, K22, K24 (Bo / B(outcome)); D01, D02 (B). That is 8 + 1 + 14 + 8 + 14 + 2 = 47.
- **KTRACE:** the 33 K traces (K01–K08, K10–K34) appear in §1.11, §3 and §4. No token was added or removed.
- **Presence rules:** unchanged. `paths_unlanded` is present on K30, K31 and K33 and on K15–K24/K26, and `-` on K28, K29, K32 and K34. K31 fixtures from squash (K31h, j) carry paths_unlanded = 1/1.
- **Caveat order:** ascending tokens; AMBIGUOUS lines carry `-`; the O(UR-21) MERGED branches carry CV1 and CV6 (CV1 Option A text variant).
- **One result row per fixture branch:** checked for every new or changed fixture.

### 11.15 Falsification 1 — the exact R12-01 history (FX-K31l) — R13Δ

1. **Graph.** F0 (target) holds text files f and p. Branch br: C (adds L to f; adds L′ to p) → C1 (adds L2 to p). X = C1. Target: Hr (edits another part of p). P = `merge --no-ff` (Hr, C1), with a conflict at p resolved to Hr's p. Later target commit Z makes p binary. T = Z.
2. **Integration.** present(C): P is the oldest FP commit containing C; np(P) = 2; C ⊑ P^2 = C1; C ⋢ P^1 = Hr → ancestry-merge. Bu = merge-base(Hr, C1) = F0 (single) → not K28/K29.
3. **State entries and kinds.**

| State | Commit | f | p |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pre | F0 | text f0 | text p0 |
| Post | C | text f0+L | text p0+L′ |
| Bu | F0 | text f0 | text p0 |
| End | C1 | text f0+L | text p0+L′+L2 |
| Tb | Hr | text f0 | text p1 (Hr's edit) |
| Tip | P | text f0+L | text p1 (resolution) |
| Tn | Z | text f0+L | **binary** |

4. **Relevance (branch-side only).** f: C-text, End textual, S_A(L) = min(1, End − Pre = 1) = 1 → relevant (i). p: C-text (Pre, Post textual), End textual; S_A(L′) = min(1, max(0, End(L′) − Pre(L′))) = 1 → relevant (i). Tn is not consulted. **Revision 12:** p was "opaque" (Tn binary); rule (ii) needed End = Post, which fails (C1 edited p) → p dropped out.
5. **Anchoring.** Ht = Bu = F0 ⊑ Pre = F0 → Fk = F0 → trivially anchored, under every UR-19 option (merges are outside (b)'s scope anyway).
6. **Landing.** Type guard: no `tree` → continue. f: r = e → landed. p: ub = p0, t = p1 ≠ ub, e = p0+L′+L2, r = p1 ≠ e → **unverifiable**.
7. **Step 2 result.** No result-differs; one unverifiable → **K31**. paths_unlanded = 1/2. Content and events are never consulted for the decision, so CV3 cannot arise.
8. **Caveats.** AMBIGUOUS → `-`. recommend=no.
9. **UR-21.** Merge landing never uses counts, so both options give K31. **UR-19:** (a) anchored trivially; (b) not in scope. All four combinations → K31.
10. **Fails closed:** yes. MERGED is unreachable because p is relevant and not landed.

### 11.16 Falsification 6 — UR-19 × UR-21 for FX-K32a–d — R13Δ (R12-02)

Order in FINALIZE: K28/K29 → K34 → K32 → K30 → K31 → K33 (§1.8.3).

- **FX-K32a (merge).** (a): anchoring in scope; Ht = Bu = Hf; Fk = merge-base(Hf, Pre) = F0; entry(Hf, f) lacks L ≠ entry(F0, f) has L → K32, with either UR-21 option (Option A does not affect merges). (b): merges are never in scope → AN = ANCHORED; landing: ub = Hf(f) lacks L, t = Hr(f) has L, e = G(f) lacks L, r has L (the branch side equals the base at f) → r ≠ e ∧ t ≠ ub → unverifiable → K31, with either option.
- **FX-K32c (merge).** As K32a with the line counts reversed: (a) → K32; (b) → K31 (ub has L, t lacks L, e has L, r lacks L).
- **FX-K32b (squash).** (a) × A and (a) × B: in scope → K32. (b) × A: f is relevant (S_R(L) = 1); kinds all text → landing-textual; r has L (Tb ⊒ Hr restored L and S does not change f) and e lacks L → r ≠ e → **in scope** → Ht = Hf, Fk = F0, entries differ → **K32**, decided before the guard (which would also fail: Tb(L) = 1 ≠ Bu(L) = 0). (b) × B: anchoring requires Option A → out of scope → AN = ANCHORED; landing: r ≠ e → unverifiable → **K31**.
- **FX-K32d (squash).** (a) → K32. (b) × A: in scope (r lacks L, e has L) → K32. (b) × B: → K31.
- **Result.** No combination yields MERGED. Under (b), K32 remains reachable (K32b/d with Option A). The Revision 12 claim "FX-K32a–d → K31 under (b)" was wrong for (b) × A.

### 11.17 Falsification 2 — a path that becomes binary after C's change survives — R13Δ

| Where the binary state appears | Effect | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| End only (a later **branch** commit replaced p by a binary) | p is C-text with a non-textual End → S_A = S_R = 0 by definition; not relevant; A and R count toward superseded | Correct: C's text change did not survive on the branch. If other paths land, MERGED with CV3 ("replaced on its own branch"), which is then true. **[Superseded — R25Δ, R24-01; §0 "Historical records".]** Under the current rules p **is relevant** (entry(End) ≠ entry(Pre), §1.7.3) and must land. "Did not survive" holds only by net count, as a definition: S_A = S_R = 0 at a non-textual End, and a binary may still contain C's text (§1.7.4, residual (j)). CV3's current text (§1.10) applies, not "replaced". |
| Tn only (a later **target** commit, FX-K22i) | relevant by (i); landed (r = e) if the target did not touch p before integration; content: relevant C-text path with non-textual Tn → insufficient | **K22** (L-25). Fails closed. |
| Tb/Tip (the target made p binary before integration; FX-K31m) | relevant by (i); not landing-textual; merge r ≠ e → unverifiable; squash: no V entry → K12 | **K31** / **K12**. Fails closed. |
| Bu only (a back-merge after C absorbed a target binary at p) | relevant by (i); not landing-textual → count landing forbidden; landed only if r = e | MERGED only when Tip's entry equals End's entry; otherwise K31 (or K32 under anchoring). |

In no row does p leave relevance while C's change survives to End.

### 11.18 Falsification 3 — a path that changes type during the integration (FX-K31m) — R13Δ

1. **Merge.** Hr replaces p with a binary; the resolution keeps Hr's binary. p: Pre, Post, End, Bu text; Tb, Tip, Tn binary. Relevant by (i) (branch-side). No `tree` → no type guard. r = binary ≠ e (text); t = binary ≠ ub (text) → unverifiable → **K31**, paths_unlanded = 1/2.
2. **Squash.** S must apply the unit's text diff at p to Hr's binary p. It cannot, so the squash result at p differs from the unit's change. Then pid(S^1, S) ≠ pid(B_X, X) and there is no V entry. cand ≠ ∅ (the target touched p) → **K12**, before FINALIZE. If the squash resolved p to the branch's text, S's diff contains a binary → text change that the unit lacks → pid mismatch → K12 as well.
3. **Directory/file swap during a merge.** If t or r has type `tree`, the type guard makes p unverifiable → K31 (FX-K31c).
4. **Fails closed:** yes, in every mode reachable.

### 11.19 Falsification 4 — a landed unrelated path masking a missing relevant change — R13Δ

- FINALIZE decides K30/K31/K33 from the per-path landing classes **before** events and content. A single relevant path that is not landed decides the row. Landed paths are never subtracted from, or averaged with, unlanded ones (paths_unlanded counts the unlanded ones).
- The only way for a missing change to escape is to drop out of relevance. Rules (i)–(iv) read Pre, Post and End only, so no target-side state can remove it (§1.7.3). A change that does **not** survive to End is, by definition, not C's surviving change (superseded; CV3).
- FX-K31l (f landed, p unverifiable → K31) and FX-K31h (g irrelevant, f unverifiable → K31) are the concrete cases.

### 11.20 Falsification 5 — correct landing with a common line whose count the target changed (FX-K31n) — R13Δ

1. F0: Blade file f with `</div>` × 3. C adds `<p>x</p>` and one `</div>`. Hr (target) edits another block and adds one `</div>`. Squash clean; hunks disjoint → pid(F0, C) = pid(Hr, S) → V entry; anchored trivially.
2. f relevant (S_A(`<p>x</p>`) = S_A(`</div>`) = 1). All six landing states text → landing-textual. r ≠ e (Hr's block).
3. **Interim (B):** r ≠ e → unverifiable → **K31**.
4. **Option A:** touched `</div>`: Tb = 4, Bu = 3 → guard fails → unverifiable → **K31**. (By I-16, Tip(`</div>`) = 4 + 4 − 3 = 5 ≠ End = 4; the guard cannot tell whose `</div>` is whose.)
5. **Truth:** C's two lines are in Tip. So this is a **false block in both options**. It is disclosed where the owner decides (§2.3 UR-21 Option A cost, L-21). Its frequency is unknown and unmeasured.

### 11.21 Falsification 7 — K34 reachability — R13Δ (R12-04)

- **Endpoint check vs Ht vs Fk.** The endpoint check (§1.8.2) runs before FINALIZE on every X ∈ E. Ht and Fk run inside FINALIZE.
- **First-integration squash:** Ht = B_X; Fk = merge-base(Pre, T) = {m}, unique (§11.7 proof) → K34 unreachable under the P-02 PDB.
- **Chained squash, Ht branch:** X_v is a V entry's endpoint, so X_v ∈ E. The endpoint check has already made merge-base(X_v, T) unique (else K27 or K08). If P-02 is rejected, an endpoint with n > 1 is dropped from E before IA3 and cannot become X_v. → **unreachable**; kept as a defensive check.
- **Chained squash, Fk branch:** Fk = maximal common ancestors of Ht and Pre. Every common ancestor of (Ht, Pre) is a common ancestor of (T, Pre), hence ⊑ m. So Fk = merge-base(Ht, m), and both Ht and m are target commits. Several members need a criss-cross **inside the target's own history** between Ht and m. Example shape [R, no fixture]: the target has two long-lived lines that were merged into each other in both directions, with Ht on one side and m on the other. Not otherwise reachable.
- **Ancestry merge:** FX-K34a (criss-cross between Ht = Mt and Pre = Mb) → K34 under (a); MERGED under (b).
- **Root C:** FX-K34b → K34 under (a); MERGED under (b).
- **FX-K23g's graph assumption** is now stated: if Y forked from a newer target commit that touched a relevant path, the result is K32 (L-20), not MERGED.

### 11.22 Falsification 8 — empty-file addition and other ⊥ cases (FX-K20d) — R13Δ (R12-06)

1. C adds `empty1` (the empty blob). kind(Pre) = absent; kind(Post) = kind(End) = text (empty). C-text; End textual.
2. Counts: no lines → A = R = 0 everywhere → no touched line → not (i). blob(Pre) = ⊥ ≠ blob(Post) = empty-blob oid; no line with A + R > 0; entry(End) = entry(Post) → **relevant by (iv)**.
3. Landing (merge): r = e (the target never had `empty1`) → landed. paths_unlanded = 0/1.
4. Events: u_I = pid(P^1, P) covers C0's g edit and the new file → non-NULL → not K15. No revert records.
5. Content: C's ΣA + ΣR + #C-opaque = 0 → state 1 → **K20**, caveats `-`, recommend=no.
6. **Other ⊥ cases.** Deleting an empty file: Pre text (empty), Post absent → (iv) relevant when End = Post = ⊥ → landed iff r = e → K20. A whitespace-only file: lines are dropped from counts → as the empty file. Adding a binary: C-opaque → (ii). Deleting a symlink: C-opaque (Pre symlink) → (ii). Every comparison uses entry(·) or blob(·), both total over ⊥ (§1.7.3).

### 11.23 Falsification 9 — direct-mode path-type changes (FX-K23ao) — R13Δ (R12-05)

1. C on FP replaces text file d by directory d/ with d/x. paths(C) = {d, d/x}.
2. d: Pre text, Post tree → C-opaque; (ii) relevant. d/x: C-text; A(line) = 1 → (i) relevant.
3. Direct mode: Tip = End = C. **Precedence:** direct landing by construction; the type guard is not evaluated. Both landed. paths_unlanded = 0/2.
4. Content: B = {d}; C's result is a directory, so b compares kinds: kind(Tn, d) = tree (R19Δ, R18-06) → b = B. K = 1 (d/x), Tb = Pre → k = 1. State 3 → sufficient → **MERGED K23**, basis=added, CV1, CV6 (O P-25i).
5. The same change through a merge: the type guard makes d unverifiable → K31 (L-18-like cost, fail-closed). Through a squash: as for any squash, r = e → landed; r ≠ e → type guard or unverifiable.
6. Revision 12's two readings (K31 vs MERGED) are now one: MERGED, because direct landing is by construction and compares no entries.

### 11.24 Falsification 10 — ⊥, opaque states and undefined comparisons, all modes; label and identifier checks — R13Δ

| Construct | Defined by | Every mode |
|---|---|---|

| Option A count landing on a non-landing-textual path | forbidden → unverifiable | squash only |
| Type guard | merge and squash only; direct exempt | yes |
| `merge-base` with a tree | never called (PV-21) | yes |

### 11.26 R13-01 — the counterexample, the corrected condition, and the whole class — R14Δ

**The exact counterexample (FX-K33c).**
1. F0 (target): f lacks L; g = g0. Branch br: C0 adds L to f and edits g; C removes L. X = C. Target: Hr edits another line of f. Squash S onto Hr: base F0, ours Hr, theirs C. At f theirs = base (C0 added L and C removed it), so Hr's f is kept; S changes g only. pid(F0, C) = g change = pid(S^1, S) → V entry (C, F0, S, M); covers C.
2. Counts at f: Pre = C0: Pre(L) = 1. Post(L) = End(L) = 0. Bu = F0: Bu(L) = 0. Tb = Hr: Tb(L) = 0. R(L) = 1; S_R(L) = min(1, max(0, Pre − End)) = 1 → L touched; f relevant by (i).
3. Kinds: f is text in all six landing states → landing-textual. r = Hr(f) ≠ e = F0(f) → r ≠ e.
4. Anchoring: Ht = F0 ⊑ Pre → trivially anchored (AS = {f} under (a); under (b) × A, AS = {f} too, still trivially anchored).
5. **Interim (Option B):** r ≠ e → unverifiable → **K31**.
6. **Option A, Revision 13 rule:** guard Tb(L) = 0 = Bu(L) ✓; removal min(S_R, Bu(L)) = min(1, 0) = 0 ≤ max(0, Bu − End) = 0 ✓ → **landed → MERGED** — although the unit's net diff Bu → End contains **no** removal of L. That is R13-01.
7. **Option A, Revision 14 rule:** guard ✓; removal S_R(L) = 1 ≤ max(0, Bu(L) − End(L)) = 0? **No** → **not-carried → K33**. Not MERGED.

**Deriving the condition from the meaning of "carried".** C's surviving change at p, per line, is +S_A(L) copies or −S_R(L) copies. "Carried by the integration unit" means the unit's own net diff Bu → End contains that change. The unit's diff changes L's count by End(L) − Bu(L). It contains C's surviving addition iff End(L) − Bu(L) ≥ S_A(L), and C's surviving removal iff Bu(L) − End(L) ≥ S_R(L). Hence:

```
Additions:  S_A(L) ≤ max(0, End(L) − Bu(L))        (unchanged)
Removals:   S_R(L) ≤ max(0, Bu(L) − End(L))        (R14Δ; was min(S_R(L), Bu(L)) ≤ …)
```

The Revision 11–13 form exempted the part of C's removal that exceeded Bu(L) ("a removal of a line the unit base never held needs nothing carried"). That exemption is exactly the vacuous case: it accepts a removal the unit's diff does not contain.

**Why the whole class is closed [R].** For a touched line, S_A(L) ≥ 1 or S_R(L) ≥ 1 (and not both, since A and R are exclusive per line). The applicable inequality therefore demands that the unit's diff change L by **at least one copy in C's direction**. It cannot hold with 0 ≤ 0. Any history in which the unit's diff carries fewer copies of C's per-line change than C's surviving change — whether because a predecessor added the line, because the unit's base already lacked it, or because duplicates cancel inside the unit — fails it. Fixture outcomes do not establish this; the inequality does.

**What a landed Option A path now implies [R, UR-16].** For every touched L: (1) the guard gives Tb(L) = Bu(L); (2) I-16 gives Tip(L) − Tb(L) = End(L) − Bu(L); (3) carried gives |End(L) − Bu(L)| ≥ C's surviving per-line change, in C's direction. So Tip(L) = End(L), and the squash diff itself added (removed) at least C's surviving copies of L relative to Tb. It is still count-level (L-4, L-5).

| Case | Pre, Post, End, Bu (L) | S_R | Bu − End | Rev 13 | **Rev 14** | Fixture |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Removal of a line present in Bu | 1, 0, 0, 1 | 1 | 1 | landed | **landed** | FX-K23am |
| Removal of one of two duplicates present in Bu | 2, 1, 1, 2 | 1 | 1 | landed | **landed** | FX-K23aq |
| Partial removal (3 → 1) of lines present in Bu | 3, 1, 1, 3 | 2 | 2 | landed | **landed** | §11.27 only |
| Partial removal, one copy later restored on the branch | 3, 1, 2, 3 | min(2, 1) = 1 | 1 | landed | **landed** | §11.27 only |
| Removal of a line absent in Bu (a predecessor added it) | 1, 0, 0, 0 | 1 | 0 | **landed (vacuous)** | **not-carried (K33)** | **FX-K33c** (R13-01) |
| Removal of the predecessor's duplicate (C undoes C0) | 2, 1, 1, 1 | 1 | 0 | not-carried | not-carried (K33) | FX-K33b |

The last-but-one row is a second false-landing case that Revision 13 also admitted, by the same exemption. Revision 14 closes it with the same inequality.

**Positive control (FX-K23aq).** F0: f holds L twice. C removes one copy (Pre = 2, Post = End = 1, S_R = 1). Hr edits another line; Tb(L) = 2 = Bu(L). The squash removes one L; pid equal. Guard ✓; removal 1 ≤ 2 − 1 = 1 ✓ → landed. By I-16, Tip(L) = 2 + 1 − 2 = 1 = End(L). Content: J = 1; Tn(L) = 1 → j_abs = 0/1; j_inc = min(1, max(0, Tn − Tip)) = 0 → state 4 sufficient (P-07 PDB) → MERGED, basis=removed, removed_lines_absent=0/1.

### 11.28 State equality versus count-level landing — R14Δ

Unchanged in Revision 14 and stated again: **state equality** (r = e) lands a path in every mode, under both UR-21 options, and never reads counts. **Count-level landing** exists only under UR-21 Option A, only for squash landing-textual paths with r ≠ e, and only through the guard plus the (corrected) carried check. The interim rule (Option B) has no count-level landing at all, so R13-01 never affected the rule in force: FX-K33c is K31 under Option B.

**Execution order in FINALIZE (§1.8.3):** Step 0 structural (K28, K29) → anchoring over AS (K34, then K32) → landing (K30, K31, K33) → events → content → freshness.

| Fixture | Step 0 | AS under (a) | AS under (b) × A | AS under (b) × B | (a) × A | (a) × B | (b) × A | (b) × B |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FX-K34a (merge criss-cross) | Bu = {Mt} ✓ | {f} | ∅ (merge) | ∅ | K34 (Fk = {Ka, Kb}) | K34 | landing: f r = e → MERGED | MERGED |
| FX-K34b (root C via merge) | Bu = {Ht0} ✓ | {n} | ∅ (merge, root) | ∅ | K34 (np(C) = 0 → Fk := ∅) | K34 | n: r = e → MERGED | MERGED |
| FX-K32a (merge) | Bu = {Hf} ✓ | {f} | ∅ | ∅ | K32 | K32 | f: r ≠ e, t ≠ ub → K31 | K31 |
| FX-K32b (squash) | — | {f} | {f} (landing-textual, r ≠ e) | ∅ | K32 | K32 | **K32** (Ht = Hf ≠ Fk = F0 at f) | f: r ≠ e → K31 |
| FX-K32c (merge) | Bu = {Hf} ✓ | {f} | ∅ | ∅ | K32 | K32 | K31 | K31 |
| FX-K32d (squash) | — | {f} | {f} | ∅ | K32 | K32 | **K32** | K31 |
| FX-K23ai (squash, r = e) | — | {f} | ∅ (r = e) | ∅ | K32 | K32 | MERGED | MERGED |
| FX-K25c (two candidates, r = e) | — | {f} each | ∅ each | ∅ each | K25 (AN differ: K32 vs ANCHORED) | K25 | select → MERGED | MERGED |

- **Why each (b) cell follows the order:** Step 0 runs first and passes (|Bu| = 1). With AS = ∅, anchoring does not run, so K34/K32 are unreachable and the next decision is landing. With AS ≠ ∅ (K32b/d under (b) × A), anchoring runs and K32 is decided before landing's K31.
- **Root commits under (b):** a root C is reachable only through a merge, so AS = ∅ and K34 cannot occur; landing decides by state equality (FX-K34b → MERGED when r = e).
- **Squash under (b):** K34 is possible only if AS ≠ ∅ — through the chained Fk branch (a target-internal criss-cross, L-22) or the first-integration branch if P-02 is rejected.

### 11.30 Remaining paths to a false MERGED — survey — R14Δ

"False MERGED" here means MERGED while some surviving change of C is not reflected in Tip at the level the rule claims (state equality; or, under Option A, count-level).

| Construction | Interim (Option B) | Option A |
|---|---|---|
| Target-independent edit on the same file (r ≠ e) | K31 (r ≠ e never lands) | guard + carried decide; K31/K33 unless the counts carry C's change (FX-K23aj/am/aq) |
| Branch-local addition later removed by C (R13-01) | K31 | **K33** (carried fails: S_R > Bu − End) |
| Branch-local addition that the target also added (R11-02) | K31 | K31 (guard) |
| Identical text added independently on the target | K31 if r ≠ e; MERGED if r = e (C's state is in Tip; L-5 attribution) | guard fails if the target changed the touched line's count; otherwise count-level (L-4, L-5) |
| Multiple relevant paths, one not landed | K30/K31/K33 (every relevant path must land; §11.19) | same |
| A surviving change dropping out of relevance | Revision 17 said "impossible"; that was wrong (R17-01: a later branch `chmod` dropped a binary change). Revision 18 judges survival by content and mode separately (§11.53); none found now | same |
| Target-side type change | K31/K22/K12 (§11.17–§11.18) | same (not landing-textual → no counts) |
| Duplicates cancelling within the unit | K31 | K33 (FX-K33b, K33d) |
| Reorder or whitespace-only change | landed only if r = e (state) | (iv)-only paths never count-landed; on mixed paths reorders are not checked (L-23) |

**Conclusion [R].** No construction above yields MERGED with C's surviving change absent from Tip at the level claimed. What remains is disclosed, not a false claim: count-level evidence under Option A (L-4, L-5, L-23), its dependence on UR-16, and fail-closed costs (L-18, L-20, L-21, L-25). This is the author's reasoning, not a verification; the reviewer is asked to attack it.

### 11.32 R14-01 — Step 0, landing precedence and the reviewer's counterexample — R15Δ

1. **One order, three places.** §1.7.3 (Step 0, "Not structural and not anchoring", Step 2 heading), §1.8.3 ("Compute first … in this order") and §4 (`LD := landing(I) if AN = ANCHORED else NONE`; EV, PR, CK always) now state the same thing: structural Bu → AS → AN → LD only if anchored → EV, PR, CK always → decide.
2. **The reviewer's counterexample** (FX-K32b under (a) × B plus a blob read failure on Tip at f). Under Revision 15: AN = NOT_ANCHORED → landing skipped. But content always runs, and j_inc reads Tip(L) for C's removed line L at f → the same read fails inside content → **C10** (a failing `cat-file blob`; R16Δ, R15-03 — Revision 15 wrongly said C13). So a failure on a state that content (or events, or PR) also reads gives C10 whatever AN is. With the read discipline (§1.7.3) this is determinate (§11.40).
3. **What depends on AN** is only a failure inside landing itself. FX-C13b injects one through a landing-phase hook: (a) → K32 (hook never fires); (b) × B → C13 (landing runs). FX-C13c shows the out-of-scope case in every option (direct: AS = ∅) → C13.
4. **C-row preemption is preserved:** any computation that runs and raises a C row preempts every K row (§1.8.3).

### 11.34 AS invariance across IA7 candidates (R14-04) — R15Δ

Shared by every candidate in Cmin: X (End), M, S, Tb, Tip (same pos(M)), and C (Pre, Post). Under (a), AS = relevant paths, which depend on Pre, Post and End only → identical. Under (b) × B → ∅. Under (b) × A, eligibility adds Bu's kinds. Under UR-16 with path binding (required for Option A), equal pids imply equal per-path diffs from each Bu to X, so, as §1.7.3 states, Bu1 and Bu2 have equal kinds and equal per-line counts at every path — their entries may differ, because patch-id may ignore hunk position (R18Δ, R17-05) — → identical AS. AN can differ only through Ht (FX-K25c). Residual (l) is closed. **Limit:** the (b) × A part rests on UR-16, which is unverified; but Option A cannot be adopted without it.

Revision 15's table here claimed CV1 (a) clause A was true for every (a) MERGED. That was wrong for chained squashes and for files whose change did not survive (R15-01). The corrected check, with the new clause A and CV7, is §11.38 and §11.41.

### 11.36 Remaining paths to a false MERGED — survey extended (R14-07) — R15Δ

Three categories are kept apart:
- **State-level false MERGED:** MERGED while some surviving change of C is not reflected in Tip at the level the rule claims. **None found** (§11.30 and below).
- **Attribution limitation:** C's state is in Tip, but MERGED does not show that the integration carried C's own change rather than an equivalent target change. Disclosed (L-5; UR-19 (b)).
- **Integration-selection risk:** the wrong integration is selected because patch identity is coarser than assumed (UR-16 unverified).

| Construction | Category | Interim (a) × B | Other options |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rows of §11.30 | state-level | none MERGED with C's change absent | same |
| Target makes the same change first; the branch back-merges it after C; r = e (FX-K23ai) | **attribution** | K32 (anchoring catches it) | (b)/(b′): MERGED; CV7 and §1.9 (2) disclose that absorbed target changes were not checked (R18Δ, R17-05) |
| Same, but via a back-merge whose fork point is undetermined (FX-K34a) or a root C (FX-K34b) | attribution | K34 | (b): MERGED, same disclosure |
| pid equality selects the wrong (S, M) — e.g. an unrelated squash with an identical net diff | **selection risk** (UR-16; L-5) | MERGED only if every relevant path has r = e under the selected unit: C's state is in that Tip (content equivalence), so not state-level false; attribution is to the selected integration | Option A additionally relies on I-16; it cannot be adopted unless UR-16 is verified |
| A fixed caveat text claiming more than was checked | **misleading text** | Revision 15's CV1 (a) clause A was false on FX-K23al and unchecked outside AS (R15-01); Revision 16's clause A names the absorbed target state and only the files where C's change survives (§11.38) — none found now | (b)/(b′): no clause A; CV7 discloses on every MERGED (§11.41) — none found now |

No new false-MERGED construction was found. This is the author's reasoning, not verification.

### 11.38 R15-01 — CV1's anchoring clause, regression cases — R16Δ

| Case | Revision 15 clause A (a): "the unit's base agrees with the fork point on this commit's files" | Revision 16 clause A (a): "the target state the branch had absorbed … agrees with this commit's fork point on every file where this commit's change survives to the branch end" |
|---|---|---|
| **FX-K23al** (chained; Bu = X1 = A1 holds f line 10; Ht = F0 = Fk) | **false**: entry(Bu, f) = A1(f) ≠ entry(F0, f) | **true**: the absorbed target state is Ht = F0 = Fk; f is where C's change survives |
| Reviewer's case 2 (g superseded on the branch; Hb edits another line of g; AS = {f}) | **false/unchecked** at g ("this commit's files" includes g) | **true**: g is not a file where C's change survives; f is anchored |
| Any (a) MERGED via merge or first-integration squash | true (Bu = Ht there) | true |
| Direct MERGED | true trivially | true trivially (absorbed state = C's parent = fork point) |
| (b) / (b′) MERGED (K23ai, K23ar, K25c, K34a, K34b) | (b) text did not claim anchoring, but basis=opaque MERGED had no disclosure | no clause A; **CV7** on every MERGED, any basis |

Why Revision 15 failed on FX-K23al: it named the **unit's base** (Bu), but anchoring compares Ht — the target state the base absorbed — with the fork point. For a chained segment, Bu = X_v carries the branch's own earlier changes and is not the target state. Revision 16 names exactly what is compared, on exactly the files compared (AS = relevant paths under (a)).

### 11.39 R15-02 — Step 0 failure continuation — R16Δ

- §1.7.3 ("Step 0 failure continuation"), §1.8.3 ("Compute first", K28/K29 sentence) and §4 (`anchoring()` returns BU_NONE/BU_MULTI before AS; `LD := landing(I) if AN = ANCHORED`) now say the same: after K28/K29, AS, AN and LD are not computed; EV, PR and CK run.
- **No later computation reads the missing Bu:** EV reads I.base, I.tip and §6 records; PR reads I.base, I.tip, T; CK reads Pre, Post, End, Tb (= I.base = P^1), Tip, Tn; relevance reads Pre, Post, End. Bu appears only in AS (via kinds), anchoring (Ht = Bu) and landing (ub), all skipped.
- **FX-K28h:** K28 in every combination; the landing-phase hook never fires. With the hook moved to the content phase instead, the result would be C13 (content runs) — the precedence rule, not an inconsistency.

### 11.40 R15-03 — read discipline and failure mapping — R16Δ

- **Eager vs lazy is no longer open:** every computation that runs reads its whole read set (§1.7.3). The reviewer's case (a K32d variant with J = 0 and a failing `cat-file blob <Tip(f)>`): CK runs (always) and its read set includes blob(Tip, f) for the C-text path f → that git call fails → **C10**, under every option. A lazy implementation that skipped the read is non-conforming.
- **Mapping:** git call failure → C10 (§1.3); harness phase hook or direct file I/O → C13. Revision 15's "C13 (TF io)" for a failed blob read (§11.32 (2)) was wrong; it is C10. Residual (q) is restated.
- **Named injections:** FX-C13 (content-phase hook → C13), FX-C13b and FX-C13c (landing-phase hook), FX-K28h (landing-phase hook, never fires), FX-C10 (C's tree removed → a git call fails → C10).

### 11.42 R15-06 — what AS invariance actually rests on — R16Δ

Equal pids (under UR-16 with path binding) give equal per-path change multisets, modes and headers, **not** equal hunk positions. So Bu1 and Bu2 can differ in entries at a path, but not in kind or in per-line counts. AS ((b) × A) reads kinds, r, e and C's changes; the guard and carried check read counts → identical. Anchoring reads entries of **Ht** (not Bu) → may differ → K25 by IA7. Nothing in the design reads entry(Bu_i, p) across candidates except landing's ub in the merge table, which does not occur for squash candidates. **V-UR16 (d)** asks whether patch-id ignores hunk position (the design assumes it may). UR-21 Option A advice is unchanged: not safe to adopt until V-UR16 (a)–(d) pass.

### 11.43 R15-07 — filing convention, FX-K31o, candidate anchoring — R16Δ

- **Filing convention (R16Δ):** in §3, an option-dependent fixture is listed under **every** row that any of its branches reaches, with the branch named. A fixture counts toward a row's **binding** coverage only if every branch reaches that row (status B, Bo or B(outcome)). FX-K23ai, K31o, C13b, K32a–d, K33b–d, K23aj/am/aq, K25c, K34a/b and K23ar are listed this way.
- **FX-K31o** graph completed (Hr after Hb); all six combinations derived in its row and in §11.41.
- **Candidate anchoring** is classified as anchoring (§1.7.3): it runs before FINALIZE, only where AS ≠ ∅, feeds only K25, and a C row it raises preempts every K row (e.g. a failing `merge-base` → C10).

|---|---|---|---|
| **Reviewer's case:** C swaps two lines of g (A = R = 0); a back-merged target edit Hb changes another line of g, so End(g) ≠ Post(g); f anchored and landed | g: no touched line; (iv) fails (End ≠ Post) → **not relevant**; AS = {f} **[Superseded — R26Δ, R25-05; §0 "Historical records".]** under the current rule g is relevant (entry(End) ≠ entry(Pre)) and, under (a), in AS (compare FX-K23at) | **false**: C's reorder survives in g, so g was claimed, but entry(Hb, g) ≠ entry(F0, g) | **true**: g's reorder was "later edited on the branch" → explicitly not covered; f is covered and anchored |
| FX-K23al (chained) | f relevant | true (R16 fix) | true |
| Superseded file (C's line change replaced on the branch) | not relevant | true | true ("any change that did not survive is not covered") **[Superseded — R25Δ, R24-01; §0 "Historical records".]** Under the current rules such a file is relevant whenever entry(End) ≠ entry(Pre), and "superseded" means by net count or by content, not "replaced" (§1.7.3, §1.10). |
| C's reorder unaltered at End (End = Post) | relevant by (iv) → in AS under (a) | true | true |

### 11.53 R17-01 — survival by content, re-derived for every change kind — R18Δ

| Change kind (C) | Later branch-side edit | Revision 17 relevance | Revision 18 relevance | Outcome if the target then overwrote it |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| binary content v1 → v2 | `chmod +x` | **dropped** (full entry differs) | (ii) content(End) = (binary, v2) → relevant | r ≠ e → K31/K30 (FX-K31p); target-first + back-merge → K32 (a) / K31 (b) (FX-K31q) |
| binary content | content changed again | not relevant (superseded) | not relevant (superseded; CV3 true) **[Superseded — R25Δ, R24-01; §0 "Historical records".]** Under the current rules the path is relevant (End ≠ Pre) and superseded by content. CV3's current text applies | — |
| symlink target change | — (mode fixed at 120000) | as (ii) | (ii) by (symlink, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| gitlink pointer change | — (mode fixed at 160000) | as (ii) | (ii) by (gitlink, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| file → directory (d → d/x) | a file added under d/ | **dropped** (tree oid differs) | (ii) content = (tree) → relevant | merge/squash: type guard → K31 (FX-K31r; L-26); direct: landed |
| directory → file | — | (ii) | (ii) by (kind, oid) | r ≠ e → K31 |
| text line change | `chmod +x` | (i) relevant (counts) | (i) relevant | r ≠ e → K31 |
| reorder / whitespace-only | `chmod +x` | **dropped** ((iv) compared entries) | (iv) by bytes → relevant | r ≠ e → K31 (FX-K31s) |
| reorder / whitespace-only | content edited elsewhere in the file | not relevant (L-23) | not relevant (L-23; CV1 excludes it by "bytes … exactly this commit's") **[Superseded — R26Δ, R25-05; §0 "Historical records".]** under the current rule the path is relevant (End ≠ Pre) and must land; CV1 covers it (§1.10) | not claimed |
| mode-only change (text or binary) | content edited | (iii) relevant (mode survives) | (iii) relevant | r ≠ e → K31 |
| mode-only change on a binary | — | relevant; **B counted it → MERGED basis=opaque** | relevant by (iii); not a content change → state 1 → **K20** (FX-K20e) | — |

**Downstream re-derivation.** B = C-opaque content changes relevant by (ii); b = members of B with content(Tn) = content(Post) (a later target `chmod` does not count as removing C's content; a later target content change does); superseded counts C-opaque content changes with content(End) ≠ content(Post); content state 1 counts C-opaque **content** changes. AS: unchanged in definition ((a) = relevant paths; (b) eligibility excludes mode changes and non-textual states anyway). Landing: unchanged (full entries, r = e). **FX-K31p is a K31, not a MERGED, in every cell.**

**Fail-closed result:** where survival could not be judged safely by content (the directory case), the path is made relevant and the type guard gives K31 for merges and squashes. Cost: L-26.

### 11.54 R17-02 — CV1 states the relevance rules themselves — R18Δ

CV1's covered-files sentence is the text of rules (i)–(iv) in plain words; nothing is paraphrased as "survives". Checks:
- **R16-01 case (FX-K23at):** g's bytes differ at the branch end → not covered → clause A true (only f is covered, and f is anchored).
- **Mixed file** (C adds L and swaps M, N in f; a later branch commit deletes L): (i) fails (no touched line), (iv) fails (A(L) > 0) → f not covered. CV1: f is not "a file where a line … is still present", and not "a text file changed **without** counted lines" → not covered → true.
- **Binary + chmod (FX-K31p/K23as):** covered by "kind and object … exactly this commit's" — consistent with (ii) by content.
- **Directory C created, later extended:** covered ("counts while it is still a directory") — consistent with (ii) by kind.
- **Clause L's antecedent** is now "each covered file" in every cell, including (b)/(b′), where clause A is omitted (R17-06).

### 11.55 R17-03 — FX-C10d re-derived — R18Δ

Sequence (canonical order): relevance reads Pre, Post, End at f then g (End(g) = Post(g), the original blob); AS (none under (a)); AN stage 1 (Ht = Fk = F0, no call beyond those defined); stage 2 reads entry(F0, f), entry(F0, g); **LD at f reads entry(Tip, f) → injected failure (1) → C10**. CK, which alone reads blob(Tn, g) (the distinct 9 MiB blob of Z), is never reached. The argv of (1) is not issued earlier (relevance reads End = C, not Tip = M). Status B: every asserted field is a TF field, independent of open items.

| Call or read | Computation | Position |

| `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` | Bu (Step 0) | FINALIZE 1st (merges only) |
| entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End | relevance | after Bu (or in IA7 step 3, once) |

| DIFF + patch-id for u_I, u_C, r_I, r_C | EV (§6.1) | first in EV |
| `rev-list T --not I.tip`, record pids | EV (§6.2) | after §6.1 |
| entries of I.base, I.tip, T | PR | after EV |
| entries and blobs of Pre, Post, End, Tb, Tip, Tn | CK | after PR |
| entries of T and End (`drift`); `rev-list --count …` (`later_commits`); `diff-tree` on IU (`c_paths_in_unit`) | DG | after CK, **always** |
| candidates' AS, AN stages | IA7 step 3 | pre-FINALIZE, candidates by (OID(seg_base), OID(S)) |

### 11.61 R18-01 — survival key, re-derived for every transition — R19Δ

| C's change at p | Later branch edit | sk | Revision 18 | Revision 19 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| binary → text (FX-K31t) | edits the text | kind | **dropped** → MERGED + false CV3 | relevant → r ≠ e → K31 |
| symlink → text (FX-K31u) | edits the text | kind | dropped | relevant → K31 |
| gitlink → text (FX-K31v) | edits the text | kind | dropped | relevant → K31 |
| directory → file (FX-K31w) | edits the file | kind | dropped | relevant → type guard → K31 |
| file or anything → directory (FX-K31r) | adds a file under it | kind (tree) | relevant | relevant (unchanged) |
| text → binary, binary → binary, symlink/gitlink changes | changes the object | content | superseded | superseded **by sk** — but (R20Δ, R19-03 correction) this row was wrong for relevance: text → symlink/gitlink were kept relevant only by the mode rule, and text → binary not at all (R19-01). Under Revision 20 every such path is relevant (entry(End) ≠ entry(Pre)) and must land; superseded still counts the replaced object |
| any of the above | `chmod` only | as above | relevant (R17-01 fix) | relevant |
| binary → text, clean squash, later target overwrite (FX-K22j) | edits the text | kind | not in B → MERGED | in B; b needs content(Tn) = content(End) → K22 |

**Downstream.** Relevance: (ii) uses sk. **AS:** (a) = relevant paths, so the R18-01 paths now enter AS; (b) eligibility unchanged (they are not landing-textual: Pre is non-textual). **B** = C-opaque content changes with sk(End) = sk(Post); **b** = members of B whose current target state matches the branch end (kind(Tn) = tree for a directory result, otherwise content(Tn) = content(End)) — for content-keyed paths content(End) = content(Post), so this is Revision 18's test; **superseded** counts C-opaque content changes with sk(End) ≠ sk(Post). **Content state 1** unchanged. **Landing** unchanged: state equality (r = e), type guard for trees. **MERGED** therefore requires, for an R18-01 path, that Tip's entry equals the branch end's — C's surviving change is then in Tip. **Merge and squash**: identical (no count landing is possible for these paths in either UR-21 option, because they are not landing-textual). **Caveats:** CV3 no longer counts these paths (Revision 19–20 key); CV1 lists them under item (3). **R22Δ (R21-01):** under the UR-22 interim this sentence no longer holds. A kind-surviving R18-01 path whose content changed again on the branch is counted superseded (FX-K31t, K22j: superseded = 1; outcomes unchanged, K31 and K22). Under Options A, B and C it is not counted. Wherever the interim emits CV3 for such a path on a MERGED line (FX-K23az), Revision 22's CV3 text ("changed again … part of such a change may still be present") — and Revision 23's ("no longer appear … as this commit left them … part or all … may still be present", R23Δ) — is true, unlike R18-01's "replaced" reading.

**Fail-closed cost — L-27 (new identifier; L-26 keeps its meaning).** A non-text object C turned into text, or into a directory, stays relevant while its kind survives, even if a later branch commit rewrote C's text entirely; if the target also changed the path, the result is K31 (or K22 through b), even where a reviewer would call C's change superseded.

**Uncertainty, fail-closed:** a path whose kind changes again later (e.g. binary → text by C, then text → binary on the branch) has sk(End) ≠ sk(Post) → superseded; this is classified deterministically, and the claim made (CV3) is true: **C's text result no longer exists as text at the branch end** (R23Δ, R22-04: was "no longer exists at the branch end", false for a text-in-binary container that still holds C's text; with Revision 23's CV3 text — "no longer appear … as this commit left them … part or all … may still be present" — the caveat stays true in that case).

### 11.62 R18-02 — CV1 covered files = the rules — R19Δ

CV1's items (1)–(4) are rules (i)–(iv) with their preconditions in words: (1) = (i) with "text or absent both before and after" and "still text at the branch end"; (2) = (iv); (3) = (ii) with the survival key; (4) = (iii) with R19's both-present definition. Checks: the R18-01 file (binary → text, later edited) is under (3) — "still a text file" — and is relevant, so CV1 neither over- nor under-claims; a binary → text file unchanged at the branch end is under (3) (Revision 18 under-claimed it); a mixed file (counted change removed later, reorder surviving) is under none (as rules (i) and (iv) exclude it); R16-01's file (FX-K23at) is under none. Under (a), the covered files are AS for merges and squashes.

### 11.63 R18-03 — mode change only between two present entries — R19Δ

(iii) now requires entry(Pre,p) ≠ ⊥, entry(Post,p) ≠ ⊥ and entry(End,p) ≠ ⊥. Additions and deletions are judged by (i)/(ii)/(iv) only. Effects: **Option A** — an added or deleted file is no longer barred from count landing as a "mode change"; it still needs landing-textual and r ≠ e (an added file is text-or-absent in every state, so it can be eligible). **AS under (b) × A** — such files can now be in AS, and CV7's (b) × A "count-confirmed files" can include them. **CV1** item (4) covers only permission-bit changes. FX-K20f (true mode change → K20) and FX-K23au (addition and deletion → no (iii)) distinguish the cases. No owner decision is taken: (b) and Option A remain unapproved.

- **FX-C09c:** V2's Fk argv `merge-base --all <M1> <A0>`; V3's Ht argv `merge-base --all <A1> <T>` is answered from the IA1 endpoint check (repeated-calls rule now explicitly spans IA1–IA6 and FINALIZE); V3's Fk argv `merge-base --all <F0> <A0>`. Injections are on the two Fk argvs only, neither of which IA1–IA6 issues; the result is C09 or C10 according to the candidate order, which is fixed by OIDs.

### 11.65 R18-06 — stale entry-based text — R19Δ

Rewritten in content/sk terms after checking each meaning: FX-K31f (content(End,bin1) = content(Post,bin1) = (binary, v0) ≠ content(Pre,bin1)); FX-K23ak (content(End,bin2) = content(Post,bin2) = ⊥ ≠ content(Pre,bin2)); FX-K23ao (sk = kind: kind(End,d) = kind(Post,d) = tree ≠ content(Pre,d)); §11.23 step 4 (b: kind(Tn,d) = tree). Entry comparisons that remain are deliberate: landing (r = e, t = ub), anchoring (entry(Ht,p) = entry(Fk,p)), PR and drift.

### 11.66 R18-07 — blob-limit scope — R19Δ

The 8 MiB limit applies **only** to `cat-file blob <oid>` reads in the read sets (relevance, AS, LD, CK). Immediately before each such read, at the same position, the helper issues `cat-file -s <oid>` (allowed by §1.3); if the reported size exceeds 8 MiB, the result is **C08** at that position and the blob is not read. DIFF and `patch-id` outputs are bound only by the per-call stdout limit (64 MiB), never by the blob limit. FX-C10d: IA2's DIFF of Z contains the 9 MiB blob but stays under 64 MiB → no failure; CK's `cat-file -s <Tn(g) oid>` would report 9 MiB → C08 — but LD's earlier failure decides first.

### 11.67 R18-08 — editorial and limitations — R19Δ

- **§11.52** was never written in Revision 18 (numbering jumped from §11.51 to §11.53); it is now present as a short index, so §11 is numbered contiguously.
- **CV7** is compiled per via: "only in part" only for squashes under (b) × A; merges get "not checked".
- **K21's reason** `superseded-in-branch` means: no **counted or opaque content** change of C survives (state 2: K + J + B = 0). A **mode change** ((iii)) or an **uncounted change** ((iv)) of C may still survive and have landed; the reason does not claim otherwise (§1.7.4 note; residual (y)).
