### 11.69 R19-01 — relevance re-derived from its definition; the eight required cases — R20Δ

Relevance R(p) := entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p). Survival classes S(i)–S(iv) as §1.7.3. Merge examples unless stated; "t ≠ ub" means the target changed p.

| # | Case (Pre → Post; End; target; merge result) | R? | AS (a) | Landing | Superseded (sk) | Content / K | CV1 / CV3 / CV6 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | binary v0 → text t1; End t2 (branch edit); Hr binary v3; result v3 (FX-K31t) | yes (t2 ≠ v0) | in | r = v3 ≠ e = t2, t ≠ ub → unverifiable | S(ii) by kind holds → not superseded | — / **K31** | — |
| 2 | text t0 → binary v0 (same mode); End v1; Hr text t3; result t3 (FX-K31x) | yes (v1 ≠ t0) | in | r ≠ e, t ≠ ub → unverifiable | S(ii) fails → superseded 1 | — / **K31** | — |
| 3 | ⊥ → binary v0; End v1; Hr adds text t9; result t9 (FX-K31y) | yes (v1 ≠ ⊥) | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 | — / **K31** | — |
| 3′ | ⊥ → symlink / gitlink, later edit, target adds text | yes | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 | **K31** | — |
| 4 | binary v0 → ⊥ (deletion); End t5 (branch re-add); Hr binary v3; result v3 (FX-K31z) | yes (t5 ≠ v0) | in | r = v3 ≠ e = t5 → unverifiable | sk(End) = (text) ≠ sk(Post) = ⊥ → superseded 1 | **K31** | — |
| 4′ | as 4, but the target did not touch p (result = End = t5) | yes | in | r = e → landed | superseded 1 | MERGED possible; **CV3 true** (deletion replaced by a re-add) **[Superseded — R25Δ, R24-01; §0 "Historical records".]** CV3's current text, "no longer appear … as this commit left them", applies; "replaced" makes no current claim | CV1 covers p ("changed again later on the branch") |
| 5 | text → symlink / gitlink; End retargeted; target edits text; result target text (FX-K31aa) | yes | in | unverifiable | superseded 1 (sk = content) | **K31** | — |
| 6a | file → directory d/{x}; later d/y added (FX-K31r) | yes | in | type guard → unverifiable | sk = kind holds | **K31** (L-26) | — |
| 6b | directory → file d; later edit (FX-K31w) | yes | in | type guard → unverifiable | sk = kind holds | **K31** | — |
| 7 | binary v0 → v1; branch restores v0 (End = Pre) (FX-K23av) | **no** | out | — | superseded 1 | from other paths: MERGED possible | **CV3 true** (undone on the branch) |
| 8 | text: C adds L; branch rewrites f (L gone); Hr edits f; conflict → Hr's f (FX-K31ab) | yes | in | unverifiable | S(i) fails → superseded 1 | **K31** (cost L-29) | — |

**The reviewer's candidate, evaluated.** The Revision 19 review proposed content(End,p) ≠ content(Pre,p) for C-opaque paths. Two adjustments were made after checking every case: (1) it is applied to **every** path of C, not only C-opaque ones, and compares **full entries** (so a surviving mode-only change and a later branch `chmod` are also seen) — this makes the "cannot drop out" argument one line long and closes L-23's landing gap; (2) it is used **only** as the relevance (scrutiny) test. **It does not distinguish a surviving C change from a branch-end state that merely differs from Pre** — case 4 (deletion, then re-add) and case 8 (later rewrite) are relevant although C's change did not survive. That is deliberate: being relevant only requires the branch-end state to land; survival is judged separately by S(i)–S(iv) and sk (superseded, B, b, CV3), so no survival claim rests on R. The cost of over-selection is fail-closed K31/K32 (L-29).

**Concepts kept apart.** Relevant (R) — must land. Survives (S(·), sk) — counted by content checks and CV3. Superseded — a C content change with no survival (sk(End) ≠ sk(Post), or S(i) lines lost). Integrated state equals expected — landing (r = e, or Option A counts on S(i) paths). A relevant path may also be superseded (case 4′): it must land, and CV3 counts the replaced change; both statements are true. **[Superseded — R26Δ, R25-05; §0 "Historical records".]** CV3 counts changes that, by net count or content, no longer appear as C left them; "replaced" makes no current claim (§1.10).

**Landing unchanged.** State equality (r = e) for every relevant path; Option A count landing only for landing-textual paths **with a touched line** (S(i)) and no surviving mode change — a relevant path without a touched line can no longer pass the guard and the carried check vacuously.

**Mode change and superseded (R19-03).** A path whose surviving change is a mode change (S(iii)) is relevant and must land. If C also changed the path's content and that content did not survive, the content change counts toward superseded; the mode change does not. CV3 counts content changes only.

### 11.70 R19-02 — CV1 covered files = the relevant paths — R20Δ

CV1's covered files are now stated as the relevance rule itself: "each file this commit changed whose state at the branch end — content, kind or file mode — still differs from its state before this commit". Re-checked: C removes L from f and the branch later deletes f → End(f) = ⊥ ≠ Pre(f) → covered (Revision 19 omitted it); an added or deleted empty file → covered; text → directory (FX-K23ao, K31r) → covered; opaque deletion still absent at End (FX-K23ak) → covered; text → symlink (whole mode) → covered. Under (a), covered = AS for merges and squashes. CV1 adds that covering is not a survival claim (CV3 counts replaced changes). **[Superseded — R25Δ, R24-01; §0 "Historical records".]** CV1's current parenthetical (§1.10) reads "changes that, by net line counts or by content, no longer appear as this commit left them are counted by CV3".

### 11.71 R19-03 — stale text — R20Δ

§1.7.4's "C-opaque paths are compared by entry" is replaced (B and superseded by sk, b by content/kind). §11.61's row for text → symlink/gitlink is corrected: under R20 such paths are relevant by R (no reliance on the mode rule) and superseded if sk(End) ≠ sk(Post). §9's earlier statements "none known to the author" (Revisions 15–19) are historical; each was later contradicted by a review, and §9.00000000 says so.

### 11.72 R19-04 — CV7 / CV8 — R20Δ

CV8 ("checked only in part") is emitted only for a (b) × A squash whose AS ≠ ∅; CV7 ("not checked") in every other (b)/(b′) case, including (b) × A squashes with AS = ∅ (FX-K23ai, K23ar). The helper chooses from AS on the same line; no output field is added. Grammar: CV list adds CV8.

### 11.73 R19-05 — the repeated-calls sequence — R20Δ

The sequence is **every git call the helper issues after snap0a, in issue order**: preflight (§1.4), resolve_target, snap_refs, decide() (K01–K07 checks, including NB = `merge-base --all C T` and paths(C)), IA1–IA7, FINALIZE. Re-derived injections: FX-C09c — V3's Ht argv `merge-base --all <A1> <T>` is first issued by the IA1 endpoint check (NB's argv is `… <C> <T>`, different); the two Fk argvs first occur in IA7 step 3 → unchanged. FX-C10e — `rev-list --count … -- f g` first occurs in DG → unchanged. FX-C10b/c/d, C09b, C10f — none of their argvs is issued by preflight or decide() → unchanged. An injection on paths(C)'s `diff-tree` would fire at decide()'s K04 check (absent C) — no fixture does this.

### 11.74 R19-06 — the 8 MiB blob-read limit — R20Δ

Every blob read (`cat-file -s` then `cat-file blob`) is subject to the 8 MiB limit, and kind detection (the NUL test in the first 8000 bytes) needs a blob read for every regular file of C in Pre, Post and End, and in CK's states for C-text paths. So **any C that touches a file over 8 MiB, in any of those states, ends in C08 (TOOL_FAILURE)**, even though the kind test needs only 8000 bytes: PDFs, images, archives and large generated files are not classifiable. This is fail-closed and user-visible: Observe prints "merge-status failed: limit". Its frequency is unmeasured; the value is an owner choice under P-19 (L-28). Whether git's own binary detection agrees with the 8000-byte rule is **not** claimed; it is V-UR16 (e).

### 11.77 R20-01 — K22 depends on survival, not on relevance — R21Δ

**S(i) re-derived from its definition.** p ∈ paths(C) has survival class S(i) iff: (1) p is **C-text** — Pre and Post are both textual at p (text or absent); (2) **End is textual** at p (text or absent); and (3) p has a **touched line** — some L with S_A(L) + S_R(L) > 0, where S_A(L) = min(A(L), max(0, End(L) − Pre(L))) and S_R(L) = min(R(L), max(0, Pre(L) − End(L))). Exclusions: a C-text path whose End is non-textual has S_A = S_R = 0 by definition (no S(i)); a C-opaque path never has S(i); a relevant path may have no survival class at all.

**Rule (R21Δ).** In content states 3–5, the state is insufficient (K22) if some path **with survival class S(i)** has a non-textual Tn. A relevant path without S(i) never triggers it: by net line count none of C's line changes survives there, so no line change is counted (R24Δ, R23-01: was "none of C's lines survives there, so there is nothing to count". C's own copy may still be present, FX-K23bb, and Tn is then not examined, residual (ff)). A genuinely missing C contribution still fails closed: if an S(i) line's file is non-textual in Tn, K22 (FX-K22i, K22l).

| Fixture | R | Survival | Tn kind | Content state | Superseded | K | CV1 / CV3 / CV6 / CV7 / CV8 | Landing |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FX-K23aw (case 1) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | text | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 | CV1 (f, g), CV3, CV6; (b)/(b′): +CV7, no clause A | both r = e |
| FX-K22i (case 2) | f | S(i) | binary | insufficient | 0 | **K22** | — | r = e |
| FX-K23ax (case 3, R20-01's exact case) | f, g | f none (End binary); g S(i) | f binary | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 (R20: K22) | CV1, CV3, CV6 | both r = e |
| FX-K23ay (case 3 variant) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | f binary | 3 sufficient | 1 | MERGED K23 (R20: K22; R23Δ) | CV1, CV3, CV6 | both r = e |
| FX-K22l (case 4) | f, g | f S(i); g none | f binary | insufficient | 1 | **K22** | — | both r = e |
| FX-K31ab (case 5, merge) | f, g | f none; g S(i) | — | — | 1 | **K31** every cell (landing first) | — | f unverifiable |
| FX-K31ac (case 5, squash) | — | — | — | — | — | **K12** every cell (no V entry) | — | — |

Modes: the rule reads only C-text classes, counts and Tn, so it is the same in direct, merge, first-integration and chained squash; UR-19 and UR-21 do not change it (they act on anchoring and landing, which precede content).

**What over-selection can add (corrected).** Making more paths relevant can only add **AMBIGUOUS** results — **K25** (larger per-candidate AS in IA7 step 3 under (a) can create an anchoring disagreement), **K30, K31** (landing), **K32, K34** (anchoring) — and **TOOL_FAILURE** rows from the extra reads of those paths (C08, C09, C10). With R21's restriction it no longer adds **K22**. It can never add a MERGED: every added path is an added requirement before MERGED, never a removed one.

### 11.78 R20-04 — paired kind-transition histories — R21Δ; R22Δ (R21-01, R21-02: Option C column, H6 split, H9, H10, statement corrected)

| History | Interim (strict) | UR-22 A (symmetric kind) | UR-22 B (R19–R20 key) | UR-22 C (R22Δ) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H1: text → binary v0, branch → v1, target untouched, later target → text t8; plus g (FX-K22m) | K22 (b < B) | K22 | **MERGED + CV3** (Tn(p) unchecked) | K22 |
| H2: binary → text t1, branch → t2, later target → binary; plus g (FX-K22j) | K22 (superseded = 1, R22Δ) | K22 | K22 | K22 |
| H3: C's only path text → binary, branch edits, Tn = End (FX-K21c) | **K21** | **MERGED** basis opaque | K21 | K21 |
| H4: C's only path binary → text, branch edits, Tn = End (FX-K21d) | **K21** (stricter than A, B and C) | MERGED | MERGED | MERGED |
| H4+g (R22Δ): as H4 with g; t2 keeps most of t1 (FX-K23az) | MERGED + **CV3** (true under the R22–R25 texts; R25Δ, R24-08) | MERGED, no CV3 | MERGED, no CV3 | MERGED, no CV3 |
| H5: kind change, then branch restores Pre (End = Pre) | not relevant; superseded; alone K21, with g MERGED + CV3 (true) | same | same | same |
| **H6a** (R22Δ): kind change of an existing p (Pre ≠ ⊥), then branch deletes p (End = ⊥) (FX-K21g) | relevant (⊥ ≠ Pre); must land (Tip(p) = ⊥); kind does not survive; superseded; alone K21, with g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| **H6b** (R22Δ): C adds an opaque p (Pre = ⊥), branch deletes p (End = ⊥) (FX-K21h) | **not relevant** (End = Pre = ⊥; Revision 21's "relevant (⊥ ≠ Pre)" was wrong here); superseded; alone K21 (landing vacuous), with g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| H7: kind change, then branch re-adds an opaque entry of a third kind | relevant; kind does not survive; superseded | same | same | same |
| H8: mode-only change of a kind-changed path (e.g. binary chmod) | content and kind unchanged → survives; S(iii) separate | same | same | same |
| **H9** (R22Δ): kind change, branch deletes p, then re-adds **unrelated** content of the **same kind as Post**; Tn = End. Same End class as H3/H4 | text → binary alone (FX-K21e): **K21**; binary → text alone (FX-K21f): **K21**; binary → text with g (FX-K23ba): MERGED + CV3 (true) | **MERGED, no CV3** in all three (hazard) | text → binary: K21 (alone), MERGED + CV3 (with g); binary → text: **MERGED, no CV3** (hazard) | as B |
| **H10a** (R22Δ): kind change, branch restores Pre, then re-changes to the **kind of Pre** with other content (FX-K21j) | relevant; kind does not survive; superseded; alone K21 | same | same | same |
| **H10b** (R22Δ): kind change, restore, then re-change to the **kind of Post** with other content (FX-K21i). Same End class as H3/H9 | as H3/H9 (K21 alone) | as H3/H9 (MERGED, no CV3) | as H3/H9 | as H3/H9 |
| **H10c** (R22Δ): kind change, restore, then re-apply **C's exact object** (End = Post) | content survives under every rule; alone MERGED basis opaque, no CV3 | same | same | same |

**Which approach the specification justifies.** The survival key's own rationale (Revision 19) is that "the identity of the resulting text or directory may change on the branch later without C's change ceasing to exist". That reasoning applies equally to a binary, symlink or gitlink result of a text file, so **A** is the semantically consistent reading. But A and B differ in both directions: A turns H1's MERGED into K22 (stricter) and H3's K21 into MERGED (looser); B does the reverse. No owner decision (P-06, P-21, UR-19, UR-21) settles which survival meaning the owner wants, so the choice is **UR-22**. **R22Δ (R21-01), corrected statement:** the interim is **not** "in every history, the stricter of A and B". It equals the stricter of A and B in H1 (K22) and H3 (K21). In **H4 it is stricter than both** (K21 where A and B both give MERGED). It is a third rule, and it is never looser than A, B or C in outcomes (§11.85). It is stricter than Revision 20 in H4 and H1. It does not broaden MERGED anywhere. **R22Δ (R21-02):** H9 and H10b produce the same End state as H3/H4, so no option can distinguish them (§11.86). The interim never credits the re-added object (K21 alone, CV3 otherwise). Option A always credits it; Options B and C credit it for non-text → text.

**Effects of the interim** on content (B now includes kind-surviving kind-changing paths, as scrutiny), superseded (counts every content change that was changed again on the branch), CV3 (emitted when content changed again, even if kind survives — **R22Δ:** true only with Revision 22's text (R23Δ: and with Revision 23's, which also removes copy attribution, R22-01; R27Δ, R26-03: and with the current text, §1.10); Revision 21's "replaced" was false for an edited object, FX-K23az), K21 (via B\*; **R22Δ:** its meaning is "no counted line and no C-opaque content survives unchanged", not "only kind-only survivors"; **R23Δ (R22-01):** "no counted line" now reads "by net line counts, none of C's line changes appears as C left it; which copy is not established"), K22 (b checks Tn for kind survivors). Existing fixtures: FX-K22j, K31t–w, K31x, K31aa have unchanged outcomes (landing or b decide); FX-K23ap, K23ao, K23ak are unchanged (content survives). **R22Δ (R21-01):** no binding fixture's **outcome** moves, but `superseded` moves 0 → 1 under the interim for FX-K22j (printed) and FX-K31t (internal only: K31 prints content fields as `-`). Revision 21's "No binding fixture moves" was true only of outcomes.

### 11.79 R20-02 — repeated-calls sequence — R21Δ

The **sequence** begins with the **first git call after snap0a** (preflight's `git version`) and **ends with FINALIZE's last call** (DG's `diff-tree` for `c_paths_in_unit`). Inside it, a repeated argv is answered from its first result and not reissued; an argv-keyed injection fails at that argv's first occurrence. **snap1's calls are outside the sequence: they are always issued afresh and never answered from it**, so the final ref record S1r is a fresh read and the (T, RH) comparison detects ref changes for D02 (FX-S3, S6). Calls outside the sequence never answer calls inside it. The definition does not touch L-28 (each `cat-file -s`/`cat-file blob` is still issued once per oid in the sequence) or L-29 (which paths are read). FX-C09b, C09c, C10b–f, C13b/c and K28h were re-derived; none moves.

### 11.80 R20-03 — owner-facing text — R21Δ

Corrected: §2.3 UR-19 (b) eligibility ("landing-textual, r ≠ e, a touched line (S(i)), no surviving mode change (S(iii))") and disclosure ("CV7, or CV8 when AS ≠ ∅ for a (b) × A squash"); the §4 mapping note (same eligibility); P-21's scope ("caveat texts CV1–CV8"); §1.7.4's b/B row ("the C-opaque content changes counted in B under UR-22's interim rule"). Searched: no other §2 question text still uses "not (iv)-only" or "CV7 on every MERGED".

### 11.81 R20-05 — CV1 parenthetical — R21Δ

CV1 now reads "…still differs from its state before this commit — including, when they meet that condition, files this commit added or deleted …; a file this commit added that the branch later deleted is not covered, because it is absent both before this commit and at the branch end". Re-derived: C adds n, branch deletes n → End(n) = ⊥ = Pre(n) → not relevant → not covered. No other CV1 wording claims coverage without the condition.

### 11.82 R20-06 — canonical-order trigger — R21Δ

Item 1 now triggers on facts decided before relevance: **IA7 has several candidates for the same X** (decided by IA7 step 2) **and the compiled options allow a non-empty AS for a squash** — (a) with either UR-21 option, or (b) with Option A; never (b′), never (b) × B. When triggered: relevance (once), then for each candidate in ascending (OID(seg_base), OID(S)): its AS; **its AN stages only if that AS ≠ ∅**. The order is the same as Revision 20's; only the trigger is now decidable.

### 11.85 R21-01 — what the UR-22 interim is, with proof; CV3 and K21 texts checked — R22Δ

**Sets.** For C's C-opaque content changes (content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p)), write:
- CS: content survives (content(End,p) = content(Post,p));
- KC: kind-changing;
- KS: kind survives;
- Kb: Option B's kind-keyed subset of KC (kind(Post) = tree, or kind(Post) = text with kind(Pre) non-textual).

For a kind-changing path, CS ⇒ KS, because content includes kind. Then:
- B_int = CS ∪ (KC ∩ KS) and B_A = (¬KC ∩ CS) ∪ (KC ∩ KS). Since KC ∩ CS ⊆ KC ∩ KS, **B_int = B_A**. B_C = B_A by definition.
- B_B = (¬KC ∩ CS) ∪ (Kb ∩ KS) ∪ ((KC \ Kb) ∩ CS) ⊆ B_A.
- B\*_int = CS ⊆ B_B, since every CS path lies in one of B_B's three parts. B\*_A = B_A; B\*_B = B_B; B\*_C = B_B.
- The opaque part of superseded: interim ¬CS; A ¬B_A; B ¬B_B; C ¬B_A ∪ ¬B_B = ¬B_B. Since CS ⊆ B_B ⊆ B_A, we have ¬B_A ⊆ ¬B_B ⊆ ¬CS. **The interim's superseded is the largest, and Option C's equals Option B's.**

**Inclusion (outcomes) [R].** Everything outside B, B\*, b and superseded is independent of UR-22: relevance and landing (entries only), anchoring, events, PR, state 1's condition (it counts C's changes, not their survival), K, J, k, j_abs, j_inc, and the S(i) Tn rule. b's per-path test is the same under every rule. Suppose the interim returns MERGED. Then:
1. The interim is not in state 2, so K + J > 0 or B\*_int ≠ ∅. Since B\*_int ⊆ B\*_B = B\*_C ⊆ B\*_A, no option is in state 2.
2. State 1 is not reached under any rule, because its condition is UR-22-independent.
3. States 3–5 are selected by K and J alone, so every rule lands in the same state.
4. b = B under the interim means every member of B_int passes. B_A = B_C = B_int and B_B ⊆ B_int, so b = B under A, B and C.
5. The S(i) Tn rule is the same under every rule.

So every option also returns MERGED. ∎ This restates the Revision 21 review's sketch and extends it to Option C. It is paper reasoning.

**The converse fails, so the interim is a third rule.**
- H4 (FX-K21d), H9 non-text → text alone (FX-K21f) and **H10b-Kk** alone (binary → text, restore, re-change to text: FX-K21k; R23Δ, R22-05, missing from Revision 22's list): interim K21; A, B and C all MERGED.
- **Exact predicate (R23Δ, R22-05).** The interim is non-MERGED while A, B and C are all MERGED iff every UR-22-independent check passes, b = B under Option A, K + J = 0, CS = ∅, and some path is class-5 Kk. Derivation: the options are MERGED only if not in state 2, i.e. B\*_B = B_B ≠ ∅ (K + J = 0). The interim is in state 2 iff CS = ∅ (K + J = 0). With CS = ∅, B_B = Kb ∩ KS. For a tree result content survival equals kind survival, so CS = ∅ removes trees, and B_B ≠ ∅ iff some non-text → text path has its kind surviving and its content changed (class-5 Kk). With +g (K > 0) the interim is not in state 2 (FX-K23az, K23ba).
- H3 (FX-K21c), H9 text → binary alone (FX-K21e) and H10b (FX-K21i): interim K21; A MERGED; B and C K21.
- No history makes the interim looser than any option.

**Caveats.** On a line that is MERGED under every rule, the interim's superseded is at least every option's. The interim can therefore emit CV3 where no option does (FX-K23az, K23ba), so CV3's text must be true for every change the interim alone counts: a kind-surviving object that was changed again on the branch.

**CV3 checked against every source of superseded.** **R25Δ (R24-06, R24-08):** the right-hand column applies to the current text (§1.10; unchanged since Revision 25 — R27Δ, R26-03), quoted next. The only change from Revision 24 is that a line unit is "one missing or extra copy of a line (by net count)"; every row's verdict is unchanged, and superseded = Σ(A − S_A) + Σ(R − S_R) + opaque changes counts exactly such copies. **R24Δ (R23-09):** the right-hand column applies to the text — "… no longer appear at the branch end as this commit left them. Each is counted either as one missing or extra copy of a line (by net count), in a file that is text (or absent) both before and after this commit, or as one change, for a file that is binary, a link, a submodule or a directory before or after it … do not establish which copy … and … do not establish that nothing of the change remains". Every row below stays true under it: line rows count copies in C-text files; opaque rows count one change per C-opaque file, including FX-K23az's bin5, which is text at Post and End. R23Δ (R22-01): the table checked Revision 23's text — "by net line counts … no longer appear at the branch end as this commit left them: the branch changed the file … which copy of an identical line was affected is not established, and part or all … may still be present". Revision 22's version of this table marked A − S_A as "removed" and R − S_R as "undone". Both attribute a net count to C's own copy, which counts cannot show (FX-K23bb).

| Source of superseded | What the states show | Current (Revision 25) text true? (R25Δ, R24-08) |
|---|---|---|
| A − S_A (End has fewer copies of L than C's change implies) | the branch changed the file; by net count C's added copies do not all appear. Which copy was removed is unknown: it may be a pre-existing one (FX-K23bb) | yes ("by net line counts … no longer appear as this commit left them … which copy … is not established") |
| R − S_R (End has more copies of L than C's change implies) | the branch changed the file; by net count C's removal does not appear. The re-added copy may be unrelated (an unrelated `}`) | yes (same) |
| C-text path with non-textual End (residual (j), FX-K23ax) | file replaced by a non-text object, which may still contain C's text | yes ("do not establish that nothing of the change remains"; R25Δ, R24-08: was a quote of the removed Revision 23 phrase) |
| C-opaque, End = Pre ≠ ⊥ (class 1, H5) | restored | yes |
| C-opaque, End = ⊥ (classes 3, 4: H6a, H6b) | deleted | yes |
| C-opaque, kind survives, content differs (class 5: H3, H4, H9, H10b; FX-K23az, K23ba) | edited, or deleted and re-added | yes (counted as one change for a file that is not text before or after C — R24Δ: Revision 23 said "per non-text file", wrong for a text result; "do not establish that nothing … remains" covers an edit) |
| C-opaque, End of Pre's kind or a third kind (classes 6, 7: H10a, H7) | replaced | yes |
| A later rename (residual (ee)) | moved out of paths(C) | yes in substance; the move itself is not detected (L-4) |

Count unit (R24Δ, R23-09): superseded counts net line-copy differences in C-text files and one change per C-opaque file, and the current text (§1.10) says exactly that (R27Δ, R26-03: was "the Revision 24 text"; the unit is now worded per copy, R24-06) ("a file that is text (or absent) both before and after … a file that is binary, a link, a submodule or a directory before or after it"). **No row claims which identical copy was affected** (PV-33 (4)).

**K21's meaning checked** (R23Δ, R22-01: "no counted line" now reads "by net line counts, none of C's line changes appears as C left it; which copy is not established").
- H3, H9 and H10b alone (interim): K + J = 0 (by net counts no line change of C appears as C left it; here C changed no lines at all); B\* = ∅ because the content changed. "No C-opaque content survives unchanged; a kind change may survive" is true.
- H4 alone: true, including the note that much of C's text may remain uncounted.
- H5, H6a, H6b, H7 and H10a alone: true, because nothing of C's object survives.
- Under Options A, B and C, wherever K21 is reached, "no C-opaque content change of C is in B\*" is the same sentence and is true by definition.

**Locations corrected:** §1.7.3 survival key and S(ii); §1.7.4 superseded, b/B and state 2; §1.10 CV3; §2.2 P-21; §2.3 UR-22; L-24; L-27; FX-K31t, K22j, K21c, K21d, K22m; §11.61; §11.78; residual (dd). The interim rule's sets are word-for-word the Revision 21 sets (B: content survives, or kind-changing with kind surviving; superseded: content does not survive; B\*: members of B whose content survives). **No outcome changes.**

### 11.86 R21-02 — every End class of a kind-changing C-opaque path — R22Δ; R23Δ (R22-07: mutually exclusive predicates)

**Setup.** p is C-opaque with content(Post,p) ≠ content(Pre,p) and kind(Pre,p) ≠ kind(Post,p).
- **Alone:** p is C's only path, p lands (r = e) and Tn = End.
- **+g:** C also adds a line to text file g (S(i), landed, Tn(g) = End(g)).
- **Kc:** Option B judges p by content — anything → binary, symlink or gitlink, including absent → opaque.
- **Kk:** Option B judges p by kind — non-text → text.
- A directory result has content (tree), so class 5 does not exist for it.
- If C deleted an opaque file (Post = ⊥), End = ⊥ is class 2 and a re-add is class 6 or 7.

**R23Δ (R22-07).** Revision 22's class 1 (End = Pre) contained class 4 (End = Pre = ⊥), and with Post = ⊥ class 2 overlapped class 3, so "exactly one of" was false. The predicates below are mutually exclusive and jointly exhaustive (proof under "Completeness"); every fixture and history keeps the class Revision 22 gave it.

| # | End class (R23Δ predicates) | Histories | Relevant? | Interim | Option A | Option B | Option C |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | End = Pre **≠ ⊥** (entries equal) | H5 with Pre ≠ ⊥ (with Pre = ⊥ it is class 4, H6b) | no | alone K21; +g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| 2 | content(End) = content(Post) (mode may differ: H8) | no later change; H10c | yes | alone MERGED (opaque), no CV3; +g MERGED, no CV3 | same | same | same |
| 3 | End = ⊥, Pre ≠ ⊥, **Post ≠ ⊥** | H6a (FX-K21g) | yes (must land: Tip(p) = ⊥) | alone K21; +g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| 4 | End = ⊥, Pre = ⊥ (then Post ≠ ⊥) | H6b (FX-K21h) | no | alone K21 (landing vacuous); +g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| 5 | **End ≠ ⊥**, kind(End) = kind(Post), content(End) ≠ content(Post) | H3, H4 (edit); **H9** (delete, then re-add the same kind); **H10b** (restore, then re-change to Post's kind); FX-K23az, K23ba | yes | Kc and Kk: alone **K21**; +g MERGED + **CV3** | Kc and Kk: alone **MERGED, no CV3**; +g MERGED, no CV3 | Kc: alone K21; +g MERGED + CV3. Kk: alone **MERGED, no CV3**; +g MERGED, no CV3 | as Option B |
| 6 | **End ≠ ⊥**, End ≠ Pre, kind(End) = kind(Pre) ≠ kind(Post) (includes Pre's content with another mode) | H10a (FX-K21j); a branch conversion back | yes | alone K21; +g MERGED + CV3 | same | same | same |
| 7 | **End ≠ ⊥**, kind(End) ∉ {kind(Pre), kind(Post)} | H7; FX-K31z's shape (a merge) | yes | as class 6 | same | same | same |

**Current target changed after integration (Tn(p) ≠ End(p)).**
- Class 5 (H1, H2): interim K22 (b < B); Option A K22; Option B Kc MERGED + CV3 (Tn(p) never checked, H1), Kk K22; Option C K22.
- Class 2: K22 under every rule (b).
- Classes 1, 3, 4, 6 and 7: p ∉ B under every rule, so Tn(p) is not checked. With +g the result is MERGED + CV3, which is true, because content(End) ≠ content(Post): by content, C's result does not appear at the branch end as C left it. (R28Δ, R27-04: was "because C's object is not at the branch end", false when C's object reappears under another kind — for example C turns text p into a symlink to "target" and the branch turns it back into a regular file whose bytes are "target": same blob, class 6. CV3 and every outcome are unchanged.)

**Completeness and exclusivity [R] (R23Δ, R22-07).** Survival, superseded and relevance read only entry(Pre,p), entry(Post,p) and entry(End,p); landing and b add Tip and Tn. Every End falls in exactly one class by this decision tree:
- **End = ⊥:**
  - Post = ⊥ → class 2 (content(End) = ⊥ = content(Post); then Pre ≠ ⊥, because content(Pre) ≠ content(Post));
  - otherwise Pre ≠ ⊥ → class 3;
  - otherwise Pre = ⊥ → class 4.
- **End ≠ ⊥:**
  - content(End) = content(Post) → class 2;
  - otherwise kind(End) = kind(Post) → class 5;
  - otherwise kind(End) = kind(Pre): End = Pre → class 1, End ≠ Pre → class 6 (Pre ≠ ⊥ here, since kind(End) ≠ absent);
  - otherwise → class 7.

Pairwise disjointness follows from the same tests. Class 1 requires End ≠ ⊥ (so it excludes 3 and 4) and kind(End) = kind(Pre) ≠ kind(Post) (so it excludes 2 and 5). Class 2 with End = ⊥ requires Post = ⊥, which classes 3 and 4 exclude. Classes 5, 6 and 7 are separated by kind(End). A branch history matters only through its End, so the table covers every history: edits, deletions, re-adds, restores and their combinations.

**The hazard [R].**
- Class 5 holds two kinds of history that no state-reading rule can tell apart: an **edit** of C's object, where part of C's contribution may remain, and an **unrelated** delete-then-re-add or restore-then-re-change of the same kind, where nothing of C's object remains.
- Any rule that admits class 5 to survival by kind credits the unrelated content as survival of C's change: Option A always; Options B and C for Kk. The result is MERGED with no CV3 (basis opaque when p is alone).
- This is not a state-level false MERGED (Tip agrees with the branch end), but the output omits that C's object was changed again.
- **The interim never credits it.** B\* and superseded use content, so class 5 gives K21 alone and CV3 otherwise. Its b check of the re-added object at Tn is an added requirement, not a credit.
- **The interim's cost is the mirror image.** In an edit-only class-5 history where most of C's content survives (FX-K21d, K23az), it reports K21, or MERGED + CV3, where a reviewer might call C's change surviving.

**Fixtures:** FX-K21e (class 5, Kc, H9), K21f (class 5, Kk, H9), K23ba (class 5, Kk, H9, +g), K21i (class 5, H10b), K21c and K21d (class 5, edit), K23az (class 5, Kk, edit, +g), K21g (class 3), K21h (class 4), K21j (class 6), FX-K31z (class 7, merge). Class 1 has no kind-changing fixture; FX-K23av is its content-only analogue. No UR-22 option is selected, and P-06 governs every K21 cell.

### 11.87 R21-03 — Revision 21's movement toward MERGED, disclosed — R22Δ; R23Δ (R22-02: both cases)

**R23Δ (R22-02).** Revision 22 disclosed only case (a). The Revision 22 review showed a second case (FX-K23ay; FX-K23bc). Both are stated here from the actual predicates.

**Exact condition.** A commit moves from K22 (Revision 20) to MERGED (Revision 21 onward; R26Δ) iff some path p satisfies all of the following, and no other K22 cause exists:
- p ∈ paths(C) is **relevant** (entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p)) and **C-text** (Pre and Post textual);
- p has **no survival class S(i)**, in one of two ways:
  - **(a)** End is non-textual at p (a later branch commit replaced the file by a binary, symlink, gitlink or directory), so S_A = S_R = 0 by definition (FX-K23ax); or
  - **(b)** End is textual at p but p has **no touched line**: every S_A(L) + S_R(L) = 0. C's line changes at p are then entirely superseded by net count (FX-K23ay, K23bc), or C's change there is a mode change S(iii) or an uncounted change S(iv), or nothing of it survives;
- **Tn(p) is non-textual**;
- every other check passes: landing (p and every other relevant path), events, PR, and the content state of the other paths (states 3–5 sufficient), **evaluated under the rule in force: the UR-22 interim** (R24Δ, R23-07). Under the interim a commit that also has a class-5 Kk path and no counted surviving line goes to K21, not MERGED (FX-K21l; header "Reason movement").

(The brief for this revision phrased case (b) as "End is textual, but no touched line has a textual Tn". Tn is a per-path state, so the defined predicate is the one above: End textual, no touched line, Tn(p) non-textual. Nothing broader is claimed.)

**What changed.**
- **Revision 20:** the Tn rule applied to every relevant C-text path with a non-textual Tn → **K22**, with the rationale "C's surviving lines cannot be counted".
- **Revision 21 onward** (R26Δ): the rule applies only to S(i) paths. p has none, so the result is **MERGED**, with **CV3 iff superseded > 0**. superseded > 0 when C added or removed lines at p (case (a): its A and R count by definition; case (b): its superseded lines count). It is 0 when C's change at p was mode-only or uncounted, and then no CV3 comes from p.

**Rationale.**
- Under the definitions p has, by net line count, no surviving line change of C, so Revision 20's rationale ("C's surviving lines cannot be counted") claimed something the definitions do not support (R24Δ, R23-01: wording; C's own copy may still be present).
- Landing still requires Tip(p) = End(p).
- CV3 (current text, §1.10; R25Δ, R24-08: was "Revision 23 text … part or all may still be present") says that C's changes no longer appear as C left them, counted per copy of a line or per C-opaque file, and that the counts do not establish which copy changed or that nothing remains.

**What stays unexamined (residuals (j), (ff)).**
- **(a)** The branch's non-text object may embed C's line (a text-in-binary container), and nothing checks Tn for it.
- **(b)** Net counts cannot tell which copy the branch removed, so C's own line may be present at End and in Tip (FX-K23bb) while Tn(p) is never examined (FX-K23bc).
- Textconv is forbidden, and counts are never taken on non-textual states.

**Status.** Not validated at runtime; frequency unmeasured. The Revision 21 review called this the first movement from AMBIGUOUS to MERGED in the series; the author has not audited every earlier revision to confirm "first".

### 11.89 R21-05 — the end of the repeated-calls sequence — R22Δ; R23Δ (R22-03: table corrected; pre-FINALIZE order stated as unspecified)

**Definition (§1.7.3; R23Δ).** The sequence is every git call issued inside phaseC. It ends with the **last git call issued, in issue order, before the decision or failure** that ends phaseC. The deciding step itself may issue no call. **R23Δ (R22-03):** Revision 22's table named the commit parse for K03 (np(C) is cached from K02's parse; the last call is `merge-base --is-ancestor C T`), named calls that do not exist for K11, K13/K14 and K25 step 2, and omitted C12 and the non-blob C08 limits. The table below follows §4's control flow.

| Run | Deciding step | Last call of the sequence |
|---|---|---|
| C01 | `git version` output check | `git version` |
| C02–C07, or C08/C09/C10 in preflight, resolve_target or snap_refs | that call's result | the deciding or failing call. **R27Δ (R26-03):** when several Phase C conditions hold at once, which row is reported — and therefore which call is last — is **unspecified** (§1.7.3 "Order guarantees"; L-30; UR-23) |
| K01 | `cat-file -e C` | `cat-file -e C` |
| C15 | `cat-file -t C` | `cat-file -t C` |
| K02 | np(C) from the commit parse | the commit parse (`cat-file commit C`) |
| K03 | np(C) = 2 after C ⋢ T (np(C) held from K02's parse; no call) | `merge-base --is-ancestor C T` |
| K05 | np(P) ≥ 3 | the parse of P. P is found by `oldest_fp_container`, whose call order is unspecified (L-30); but P's parse follows it in §4's straight-line flow, so it is the last call (R24Δ, R23-04) |
| C12 (present C) | the `C ⊑ P^2 ∧ C ⋢ P^1` test or np(P) = 1 (no call of its own) | for np(P) = 1: P's parse. For the conjunction: the last `--is-ancestor` call issued by it. **Which one that is, and whether the second is issued at all, is unspecified** (L-30; R24Δ, R23-04) |
| K04 | paths(C) = ∅ | paths(C)'s `diff-tree` |
| K06, K07 | \|NB\| | `merge-base --all C T` |
| K27, K08 | the endpoint check, in ascending ord | `merge-base --all X T` for the deciding X |
| K25 (IA7 step 2) | distinct X in Cmin (no call) | the last call issued by IA2–IA6. **Which call that is depends on their unspecified internal order (L-30)** |
| K25 (IA7 step 3) | candidate AN results differ | the last call **issued** in candidate anchoring, in canonical order. If the last candidate's AN reads are answered from the cache or need no call (np(C) = 0; Ht = Pre), it belongs to an earlier candidate, to its AS, or to relevance (R24Δ, R23-04) |
| K10 | a shape hit covers C | the last call issued by the shape-hit tests (order and short-circuit unspecified, L-30) |
| K11 | refs = ∅ (no call) | the last call issued before it: of the K10 shape-hit tests if any hit exists, otherwise of IA2–IA6 (unspecified, L-30) |
| K12 | cand ≠ ∅ | the last call issued computing cand (order and short-circuit unspecified, L-30) |
| K13, K14 | FR.fresh (computed before decide(); no call) | the last call issued computing cand (cand = ∅ is established by it; unspecified order, L-30) |
| FINALIZE, ancestry-merge or squash | the §1.8.3 decision | DG's `c_paths_in_unit` `diff-tree` (its argv names (P^1, P) or (S^1, M) and occurs nowhere earlier) — **R28Δ (R27-05): when no unordered EV call (§6.2 parse, §6.3 lineage, §6.5 comparison, CR1/CR2 test) is issued after DG.** The specification does not confine those calls to EV's slot, so an implementation may issue some after DG; the last call is then one of them, and which one is unspecified (L-30, UR-23) |
| FINALIZE, ancestry-direct | the §1.8.3 decision | DG's `later_commits` `rev-list --count` (`c_paths_in_unit` is answered from relevance's paths(C)) — **R28Δ (R27-05): with the same condition as the row above**; otherwise the last unordered EV call issued (unspecified, L-30) |
| C08 blob limit | `cat-file -s` reports > 8 MiB | that `cat-file -s` |
| C08 per-call stdout limit | that call's output exceeds 64 MiB | that call |
| C08 count limits (FP, RANGE, E, SSHAPE, pid computations, revert candidates, paths(C)) | a count exceeds its limit (no call of its own) | the call whose output gave the count (FP, RANGE, E, paths(C), revert candidates), or the last call issued before the count was exceeded (pid computations, SSHAPE). Before FINALIZE the latter depends on the unspecified order (L-30) |
| C08 total 300 s | the monotonic total exceeds 300 s | the call during or after which it was exceeded. **Whether a running call is interrupted is not specified** |
| C09, C10, C11 | that call's timeout, exit or output | that call |
| C13 hook, C14 exception | the hook or exception | the last call issued before it |

**Pre-FINALIZE order (R23Δ; R25Δ, R24-03; R27Δ, R26-03: refers to the current list).** The computations listed as unspecified in §1.7.3 "Order guarantees" have no specified internal order or short-circuit rule. That list has **nine** items: preflight's Phase C checks (C01–C07), FP's `rev-list`, `oldest_fp_container`, C12's conjunction, IA1's parent reads, IA2–IA6, the K10 shape-hit tests and cand — the eight pre-FINALIZE ones — plus EV's unordered calls inside FINALIZE. §1.7.3 governs. Revision 25's "seven items" is withdrawn. The rows marked L-30 above are therefore well defined for any single run, but they are not determined by the specification. Whether to specify them is UR-23.

**snap1.** Its calls are outside the sequence: always issued afresh, never answered from the cache, never matched by an argv-keyed injection.
- FX-S9b re-derived: an injection on snap_refs' `for-each-ref` fires at snap0b → C10, files-only. S0r = NONE, so snap1 issues snap_files only → unchanged.
- FX-S3, S5 and S6 (D02): snap1's `for-each-ref` has snap0b's argv. That is why snap1 must stay outside the sequence, and with it outside, D02 still sees a fresh record → unchanged.

**Existing injection fixtures re-derived against the definition and argv identity:**
- FX-C09b: `merge-base --all P^1 P^2` first occurs in Bu → C09.
- FX-C09c: V3's Ht argv is answered from IA1; the Fk argvs first occur in IA7 step 3.
- FX-C10b and C10c: `ls-tree` of Mt or Ht0 first occurs in AN stage 2 or LD.
- FX-C10d: `ls-tree <Tip> -- f` first occurs in LD.
- FX-C10e: `rev-list --count` first occurs in DG and is issued in merge mode.
- FX-C10f: in merge mode the u_I DIFF is the first DIFF of (I.base, I.tip), because IA2 does not run for a present C.
- FX-S11b: `git version` is the first call.
- FX-S11d: unchanged.
- FX-C13, C13b, C13c and K28h use phase hooks, not argvs.
- **None moves.**

**New: FX-C10g.** In ancestry-direct mode an injection keyed on paths(C)'s argv (which is also `c_paths_in_unit`'s) fires at relevance → C10. An injection meant only for DG's `c_paths_in_unit` cannot be expressed in direct mode, by design (residual (x), (ii)). In merge and squash modes the argv is distinct and DG issues it.

### 11.92 R22-01 — CV3, K21 and net line counts — R23Δ

**Finding (independent review).** CV3 (Revision 22) said that superseded changes "were changed again … removed … undone". Net counts cannot show which identical copy changed (L-4). Counterexamples: C appends a second L and the branch deletes the pre-existing L (FX-K23bb); C removes one `}` and the branch adds an unrelated `}`.

**Correction (author).** CV3's PDB text now reads: "By net line counts per file, and per non-text file, <superseded> of this commit's changes no longer appear at the branch end as this commit left them: the branch changed the file after this commit. Which copy of an identical line was affected is not established, and part or all of such a change may still be present." (R24Δ: superseded by Revision 24's text for R23-09; see §1.10 and §11.99.)
- §1.7.4's superseded row states the measure and the limit.
- State 2's meaning (K21) now says "by net line counts, none of C's line changes appears … as C left it; which identical copy … is not established".
- The §11.85 table was rewritten for the new text.
- PV-33 (4) now requires that no text attributes a net count to a particular copy.
- FX-K23bb was added.

**Check (author, paper).**
- **FX-K23bb:** End(L) = 1 < Post(L) = 2, so "no longer appear as this commit left them" holds by count; the file changed (End ≠ Post); the copy is not named. The text is true.
- **Removal mirror:** End(`}`) > Post(`}`) — the same reasoning holds.
- **Every other source of superseded:** checked in §11.85's table; no row names a copy.
- **K21:** with K + J = 0 the stated meaning is a count statement, so it is true even when C's own copy is present.
- **Other text searched** for copy attribution: CV2 already says "does not establish who reintroduced them"; CV1 says counts "do not establish which lines are this commit's"; §1.7.3's relevance argument ("nothing of them is at the branch end") applies only to paths with End = Pre, where it is a state statement, not a copy statement. **[Superseded — R26Δ, R25-01; §0 "Historical records".]** The Revision 25 review showed that wording to be a copy claim (FX-K23be). §1.7.3's argument is restated in net-state terms.
- **Not changed:** the CV3 condition (MERGED ∧ superseded > 0) and the superseded formula.

**Needs independent verification:** whether any other caveat, reason or §11 statement still attributes a count to a copy.

### 11.100 R23-03 and R23-04 — determinacy and the unspecified list — R24Δ

**R23-03.** The claim "outcomes without injected failures do not depend on order" was false. A genuine fault in a call that only some admissible orders reach (a corrupt SOTHER commit under a lazily built PMAP_O; a natural timeout in cand's `--cc` loop after a member is found) gives MERGED, K12 or K10 under one conforming implementation, and C10, C09 or C08 under another.

Restated everywhere (§1.7.3 "Order guarantees", L-30, UR-23, residuals (q) and (kk), PV-30):
- outcomes are order-independent **only on runs in which no git call fails, times out or exceeds a limit**;
- with a genuine failure, they can differ between conforming implementations;
- this never produces a false MERGED, because a skipped call is one no row condition needs;
- RT-02's reproducibility across implementations is not guaranteed for such repositories.

**R23-04.** Added to the unspecified list:
- `oldest_fp_container` (up to |FP| `--is-ancestor` calls in any order, including bisection);
- C12's conjunction (order and skipping of its two `--is-ancestor` calls);
- IA1's parent reads for the Kahn order.

The straight-line steps of decide() are listed explicitly. PV-30's "position" is defined only for the canonical order and §4's straight-line steps. §11.89's K05, C12 and K25 (step 3) rows were corrected.

**Check.**
- §1.7.3 "Order guarantees", L-30, UR-23, PV-30, (q) and (kk) carry the same list (scripted check: each of the six names — `oldest_fp_container`, C12's conjunction, IA1's parent reads, IA2–IA6, the K10 shape-hit tests, cand — appears in each). They state the same determinacy scope. §11.89 marks the rows that depend on the list (K05, C12, K10–K14, K25 step 2, C08 count limits) instead of repeating it.
- K05's last call stays determinate, because P's parse follows the unspecified search in straight-line flow.
- C12's last call is unspecified.

**No fixture's outcome moves.** FX-C12 is decided by np(P) = 1, or by the conjunction's truth value, which is order-independent.

**Owner decision still required:** UR-23.

**R23-08.** Three fixtures now name exact calls, each reached on every admissible order:

**pid pipelines (R24Δ; found while fixing R23-08, not raised by the review).** `patch-id --verbatim` has a constant argv. Under Revision 23's argv-keyed cache, every `patch-id` stage after the first would have been answered from the first result. §1.7.3 now states that each pid(A,B) is one cached unit keyed by DIFF(A,B)'s argv, and that injections on a pid target the DIFF argv and name the affected stage. This is a **clarification of intended behaviour**: every earlier use (IA2's PMAPs, IA3–IA5, EV, §6.2) already treats a pid as a function of (A,B). It **requires independent review**.

### 11.104 R24-01 — historical records and PV-33 (4) — R25Δ

**What this does and does not establish.**

- It does **not** establish that the historical records are otherwise correct.

### 11.105 R24-02 — the K22 → K21 predicate and the binary_present movement — R25Δ

**Derivation.** Revision 20's content rules equal Option B's key plus the K22 Tn rule on every relevant C-text path; the interim is that of Revision 21 onward. Landing, events, PR and state 1 are identical in both. Therefore:
- **K22 (Revision 20) → K21 (interim)** requires the interim in state 2: K + J = 0 and B\*_int = CS = ∅.
- It requires Revision 20 **not** in state 2: B_B ≠ ∅. With CS = ∅, this holds iff some path is class-5 Kk (§11.85).
- It requires Revision 20 **insufficient in state 5** (K = J = 0): either its Tn rule fired, i.e. a relevant C-text path with non-textual Tn (iii-a), or b < B over B_B (iii-b).

**Counterexamples resolved.**
1. FX-K21l plus a surviving opaque r violates (i) (CS = {r}), so it is not a K22 → K21 case. Under the interim it is MERGED, consistent with the corrected predicate.
2. A > 0 is not required: any change at the C-text path makes it relevant, which is all (iii-a) needs.
3. Case (b) (End textual, no touched line, Tn non-textual) is (iii-a).
4. FX-K21d with a later target change of p is (iii-b): FX-K21m.

**binary_present.** b/B is computed over B. The interim's B contains class-5 Kc paths and Option B's does not. On a MERGED line with such a path and another path keeping a counted line, the interim prints 1/1 where Revision 20 printed 0/0 (FX-K23bd). superseded and CV3 are equal in both, because the path is superseded under both rules.

**"Toward AMBIGUOUS":** now lists FX-K21f and K21k, which residual (dd) already listed.

**Not checked:** that no further field movement exists between Revision 20 and the interim (fields compared: superseded, CV3, binary_present, content_basis, K/J counts). content_basis follows the state, and the state differs only in the movements listed.

### 11.106 R24-03, R24-04 and R24-07 — FP, cached FINALIZE calls, and the pid key — R25Δ

**R24-03 (wording; consistent with UR-23's interim).** FP's `rev-list --first-parent T` is not placed by §4. Revision 25 declares **when** it is issued unspecified (eagerly, or on first use by `oldest_fp_container` or IA2), as a seventh list item in §1.7.3, L-30, UR-23, PV-30, (q) and (kk); §11.89's paragraph refers to the list. Consequence: with |FP| > 20,000, C08 may preempt K01–K07, K27 or K08 under one conforming implementation and not another. **Placing FP was not chosen**, because that would decide part of UR-23 (§0 authority).

**R24-04 (narrowing).** In FINALIZE, the canonical order fixes the order of calls actually issued. A FINALIZE call whose DIFF or argv an unspecified pre-FINALIZE computation may already have issued is answered from the cache. Its failure therefore appears earlier, at an undetermined position. Examples: a §6.2 record pid(parent0(Q), Q) for Q ∈ SOTHER; u_C when parent0(C) = B_C. The guarantee now excludes such calls (§1.7.3 twice, L-30, (kk), PV-30, (q)). Every current FINALIZE injection fixture avoids this: FX-C10b–f, C11 and S11d use present-C histories, where IA2–IA6 do not run, or argvs not issued earlier (§11.102). **[Superseded in part — R26Δ, R25-04/R25-05.]** FX-C10d uses the **default absent-C squash**, not a present-C history. It avoids pre-issue because its injected argvs (`ls-tree -z --full-tree <Tip> -- f`; `cat-file -s <Tn(g) oid>`) are issued by no pre-FINALIZE computation in that setup: IA1–IA6 issue `for-each-ref`, `rev-list`, `merge-base`, DIFF/`patch-id` and commit parses, never `ls-tree` or `cat-file -s`; IA7 step 3, which does read entries and blobs, does not run, because the default setup has a single candidate. Present-C histories are also not free of pre-issue: §6.3's lineage checks can repeat `oldest_fp_container`'s calls (R25-04), though no current fixture injects there.

**R24-07.**
- **(1) Semantic clarification, under P-19:** a pid answered from the cache is not a pid computation and does not count toward the 20,000 limit (§1.12, §1.7.3). The opposite rule would count work never done. The owner may set either under P-19.
- **(2) Wording:** §6.2 now maps a malformed `patch-id` output to C11 and any non-zero exit (DIFF or `patch-id`) to C10. This matches §1.3's failure mapping, §1.7.2's output rule and FX-C10f. Revision 24's "A pid failure → C11" was ambiguous.

### 11.110 R25-01 — the relevance argument in net-state terms — R26Δ

**Finding (independent review).** §1.7.3's argument said "a line … that C put there … no part of C's change exists at the branch end … truthfully, because nothing of them is at the branch end". Counterexample: Pre `L L`, C appends `L`, and the branch deletes the first line, so End = Pre. The path is not relevant, yet C's own copy may be the one that remains.

**Correction (wording; no rule change).** The argument now reasons over states:
- a difference in content, kind or mode between End and Pre makes p relevant;
- every survival class implies such a difference;
- the contrapositive says only that, by net count and by content, none of C's changes appears at a non-relevant path;
- it states explicitly that which identical copy remains is not established (L-4).

FX-K23be gives the counterexample as a fixture. PV-33 (4) records that Revision 25 claimed it while this argument was in place.

**Check (author, paper).**
- "Every survival class implies End ≠ Pre" holds for each class: S(i) by a touched line; S(ii) by sk; S(iii) by mode; S(iv) by blob.
- The contrapositive's three equalities follow from entry equality.
- The statement uses no copy identity.
- FX-K23be derives as stated: f not relevant, superseded 1; g S(i); MERGED with CV1, CV3 and CV6.
- CV3's text is true for f.

**Needs independent verification:** that no other statement in the current text, including the sections now incorporated by reference, makes a copy claim. The author's check is a pattern scan of the current text (§11.114).

### 11.111 R25-02 and R25-04 — EV's unordered calls and pre-issue routes — R26Δ

**R25-02 (narrowing; owner decision not taken).** EV issues calls that no read set or canonical position covers:
- §6.2's commit parses (np(Q), Q^1);
- §6.3's lineage checks;
- §6.5's representative comparisons;
- under P-14, CR1's pair predicates and CR2's ancestry checks.

Revision 26 does **not** specify an order, because UR-23 is the owner's. It declares the following:
- these calls are outside EV's read set and the canonical order (§1.7.3 EV read set; "Order guarantees");
- FINALIZE's determinate first failure, and PV-30's "every call assigned", hold only for the canonically ordered calls;
- a **single** genuine fault in an unordered EV call can change the outcome between conforming implementations. Example: three ordered reverts with a timeout on `merge-base --is-ancestor <R1> <R3>` give C09 for an all-pairs implementation and K18 for a transitive one;
- no false MERGED results, because a skipped comparison is implied by transitivity and the row conditions are set predicates;
- fixtures inject on none of these calls.

A **proposed** order (the review's suggestion) is described in §1.7.3 and UR-23, explicitly **not adopted**.

**R25-04 (examples; not exhaustive).** Four routes were added to the pre-issued-call examples (§1.7.3, twice):
- §6.3 lineage versus `oldest_fp_container` (present C);
- §6.2 pid of a later SSHAPE pair's S, which is PMAP_S's key;
- §6.2 parse of a RANGE commit, already parsed by IA2's classification;
- u_C through IA5 (X_v = parent0(C)).

§11.106's FX-C10d clause is corrected and marked. FX-C10d is an absent-C squash; its injected argvs (`ls-tree`, `cat-file -s`) are issued by no pre-FINALIZE computation in its single-candidate setup.

**Check.** Each existing FINALIZE injection fixture was re-read:
- FX-C10b and C10c: `ls-tree` of Mt/Ht0 in LD;
- FX-C10d: `ls-tree` Tip f in LD; `cat-file -s` in CK;
- FX-C10e: `rev-list --count` in DG;
- FX-C10f: §6.1 u_I DIFF;
- FX-C11: §6.1 u_I `patch-id` stage;
- FX-S11d: §6.1 u_C `patch-id` stage;
- FX-C09b: Bu `merge-base`;
- FX-C13b and C13c: hooks.

None injects on an unordered EV call. In FX-C11 and S11d (present-C merge), u_I and u_C use DIFF argvs that no pre-FINALIZE computation issues, because IA2–IA6 do not run for a present C and `oldest_fp_container` issues only `--is-ancestor`. **None moves.**

### 11.112 R25-03 — field movements on every content-field line; the "toward AMBIGUOUS" predicate — R26Δ

**Correction (disclosure).**
- **binary_present** moves for every line printing content fields (K15–K24, K26) that has a class-5 Kc path passing the Tn test (FX-K21c: K21 line, 0/0 → 1/1). **[Superseded — R27Δ/R28Δ, R26-02, R27-06; §0 "Historical records".]** This is Revision 26's text, restored in Revision 28 (Revision 27 had rewritten it). The current rule (header "Field movement"; §11.117) is that the denominator grows by one per class-5 Kc path and the numerator only when that path passes the Tn test (FX-K21c with Tn ≠ End: 0/0 → 0/1).
- **superseded** moves for every such line with a class-5 Kk path.
- The "toward AMBIGUOUS" predicate is now exact against Revision 20 (the review's derivation): either K + J = 0 ∧ CS = ∅ ∧ a class-5 Kk path (→ K21), or otherwise a class-5 Kc path with content(Tn) ≠ content(End) (→ K22). **[Superseded — R27Δ/R28Δ, R26-02, R27-06; §0 "Historical records".]** This is Revision 26's text, restored in Revision 28 (Revision 27 had rewritten it). The current statement (header "Toward AMBIGUOUS"; §11.117) holds only **given that Revision 20 is MERGED**.
- FX-K21n is the review's second counterexample: Revision 20 MERGED + CV3; interim K21; A and C K22; B MERGED.

**Derivation check [R].** Suppose Revision 20 is MERGED and the interim is not, with every UR-22-independent check passing.
- **Interim in state 2:** then K + J = 0 and CS = ∅. Revision 20 was not in state 2, so B_B ≠ ∅, which forces a class-5 Kk path (§11.85). → K21.
- **Interim not in state 2:** then Revision 20 is not in state 2 either (B\*_B ⊇ CS), and both rules are in the same state 3–5.
  - Revision 20 sufficient means its Tn rule did not fire. So no relevant C-text path has a non-textual Tn, and the interim's S(i) rule cannot fire either.
  - The interim is insufficient only through b over B_int ⊋ B_B. The extra members are exactly the class-5 Kc paths.
  - → K22.
- **Converse:** each branch yields "Revision 20 MERGED, interim not", given Revision 20 sufficient.

### 11.117 R26-02 — the conditional predicate and binary_present — R27Δ

**(a) Precondition (disclosure).** The predicate is now stated as: given that Revision 20 is MERGED (every UR-22-independent check passing), the interim is non-MERGED iff either K + J = 0, CS = ∅ and some class-5 Kk path; or, otherwise, some class-5 Kc path with content(Tn) ≠ content(End). The same wording appears in the header, here and in residual (dd). FX-K21l and FX-K21m satisfy the first branch without the precondition, and they are K22 → K21 cases, which is why the precondition is needed.

**(b) binary_present (disclosure).** b/B is computed over B. The interim's B contains every class-5 Kc path, and Option B's (Revision 20's) does not. So on every line that prints content fields:
- the denominator grows by one per class-5 Kc path;
- the numerator grows by one only when that path passes the Tn test.

Checked:
- FX-K21c, Tn = End: 0/0 → 1/1.
- FX-K21c with a later target change of p: 0/0 → 0/1, with outcome K21 under both rules (Revision 20: K + J + B\*_B = 0; interim: B\* = ∅).
- FX-K23bd with Tn ≠ End: under the interim, b < B gives K22. Under Revision 20, p ∉ B and the outcome is MERGED. So this is the predicate's second branch, an outcome movement, not just a field movement.
- With a later exact revert, the event rows decide first (K18 under both), and only the field moves.

**Not changed:** the UR-22 options and the interim rule.

### 11.121 R27-01 and R27-02 — PV-33 (4)'s scope; the S(ii) proof steps — R28Δ

**R27-01 (wording; no rule change).** PV-33 (4) restated a current-text range (§11.110–§11.116) that §0 had moved on from, so it missed the residual list holding (bb) and vouched for historical sections. It now refers to "every §11 section that §0 'Historical records' defines as current text", naming the present range (§11.121–§11.126 including the residual list, plus the six incorporated sections) only as an illustration. A future change to §0 therefore carries into PV-33 (4) without editing it.

**R27-02 (proof wording; no rule change).** §1.7.3's two S(ii) proof steps went through sk, which by its single definition is content for a non-tree result. A kind-only member of S(ii) (kind-changing, kind surviving, content changed — for example FX-K21d under the interim) does not satisfy sk(End) = sk(Post). Both steps now state the two cases:
- content(End) = content(Post) ≠ content(Pre); or
- for a kind-changing path, kind(End) = kind(Post) ≠ kind(Pre).

Either case implies entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p). FX-K31t, K31u, K31w and K22j used "sk = kind" in its Revision 19–20 meaning. They now say "kind-changing with kind surviving → in S(ii) / in B under every UR-22 rule", which holds:
- Option B keys non-text → text by kind;
- Options A and C use A's B;
- the interim includes kind-changing kind survivors.

FX-K23ao's "sk = kind" for a directory result already matches the definition and is unchanged.

**Check (author, paper).** The outcomes of FX-K31t (K31), K31u (K31), K31w (K31) and K22j (K22) are unchanged, because their deciding steps are landing or b, not sk. (bb) and §1.7.3 now state the premise the same way.

### 11.122 R27-03 and R27-05 — EV accounting and the FINALIZE last call — R28Δ

**R27-03 (accounting wording).**
- CR2's exclusivity test is now named wherever the unspecified EV calls are reproduced: §1.7.3's "Order guarantees" (both the opening note and the EV list item), "First failure decides", residuals (q) and (uu). The read set, L-30, UR-23, PV-30 and (kk) already named it. Scripted check: every line in the current text that mentions "CR1/CR2 checks" now also mentions the exclusivity test, except the historical Revision 26 summary in the header.
- The §6.2 enumeration `rev-list T --not I.tip` is named in the canonical order (§1.7.3 item 2), in §1.8.3's sequence and in §4's comment, between §6.1 and the record pids. That position was already implied by "§6.2 records in `rev-list` output order". **No new order is adopted.**

**R27-05 (uncertainty kept explicit; owner option recorded).** Nothing confines EV's unordered calls (§6.2 parses, §6.3 lineage, §6.5 comparisons, CR1/CR2 tests) to EV's slot, so an implementation may issue some after DG. Revision 28:
- conditions §11.89's two FINALIZE rows ("when no unordered EV call is issued after DG");
- states the same in §1.7.3's "Repeated calls";
- adds "a FINALIZE run in which unordered EV calls are issued after DG" to L-30's "Not determined";
- records "confine EV's unordered calls to EV's slot (after the §6.2 record pids, before PR)" as UR-23 option (c), alongside the proposed EV order (a) and the Phase C first-match rule (b). **None is adopted.**

No outcome changes. Only the determinacy of the last call, and with it injection reachability on such runs, is affected, and it is now stated as not determined.
