### 11.126 Residual risks the author could not exclude (R28Δ update; was §11.121; earlier §11.116, §11.110, §11.104, §11.98, §11.92, §11.85)

- **(a) UR-16** may not hold for some diff category, or patch-id may not bind paths. This blocks Option A only; the interim rule does not depend on it.
- **(b) L-21 cost under the interim rule** is unmeasured and may be large for SPSM (squash-only [M]). It may make the helper report K31 for most real squash integrations where the target also changed the same files. The owner's UR-21 decision should weigh this. A measurement on real cases needs separate authorisation (RT-10).
- **(c) IA7's anchoring comparison** may produce K25 more often than expected (FX-K25c shows one natural shape).
- **(d) The guard is stricter than necessary** under Option A. It blocks correct cases whenever the target changed the count of any line C touched, anywhere in the file; common lines make this likely (FX-K31n). This is now stated in the UR-21 decision text and L-21. Frequency unmeasured.
- **(e) Rule (iv) and End ≠ Post:** uncounted changes overwritten later on the branch are unchecked (L-23).
- **(f) FX-K23ak** still admits two readings of rule (ii) for a deleted branch-local opaque file. Both give MERGED.
- **(g) The Ht definition** assumes that a back-merged target state is represented by merge-base(X_v, T). If a branch absorbed target content by cherry-picking rather than merging, Ht does not see it, and anchoring passes. Landing is unaffected (it still needs state equality, or the Option A guard).
- **(i) (R13Δ) L-25 cost:** a current target that made one of C's text files non-textual gives K22 even when C's change landed. Fail-closed; frequency unknown.
- **(j) (R13Δ) Branch-side relevance and superseded:** a C-text path whose End is non-textual is treated as superseded. If a branch commit made the file binary while keeping C's text inside it (a text-in-binary container), the change is not tracked. Accepted as superseded; not otherwise detectable without textconv, which is forbidden. **R22Δ (R21-03):** since Revision 21 this case can end **MERGED + CV3** where Revision 20 gave K22, if the target later replaces f by another non-text object: Tn(f) is not examined (residual (ff), §11.87).
- **(k) (R13Δ) Chained Fk-K34** has no fixture; its reachability rests on reasoning only (§11.21).
- **(l) (R13Δ) — closed in R15 (R14-04).** AS is identical across IA7 candidates for the same X (§1.7.3; §11.34); AN differs only through Ht.
- **(o) (R15Δ, R14-02/R14-07)** Under UR-19 (b), MERGED gives up attribution outside AS (the target made the same change first and the branch absorbed it). CV7 (compiled per cell, R17Δ) and §1.9 (2) say so; it is not a state-level false MERGED.
- **(p) (R15Δ, R14-05)** Under (b), a mode-change or (iv)-only path with r ≠ e is outside AS and gives K31 instead of K32 (FX-K31o). Trace only; both AMBIGUOUS.
- **(q) (R15Δ; R16Δ corrected)** A failing git call in a computation that always runs (events, PR, content) raises C10 even when landing was skipped; a phase hook raises C13; only failures inside landing itself depend on AN (FX-C13b vs §11.40). Determinate under the read discipline: staged AN reads, one canonical order, first failure decides (R17Δ, §11.47; the Revision 16 claim of determinacy was premature — R16-03). **R23Δ (R22-03), narrowed:** this determinacy holds for IA7 step 3 and FINALIZE only. Before FINALIZE, order and short-circuiting inside IA2–IA6, the K10 shape-hit tests and cand are unspecified, so double failures and injection reachability there are not determined (L-30, UR-23). **R24Δ (R23-03/R23-04):** the list also includes `oldest_fp_container`, C12's conjunction and IA1's parent reads. A **genuine** failure (fault, natural timeout, limit) in a call that some admissible orders skip can change the outcome between conforming implementations (MERGED, K10 or K12 vs C08, C09 or C10), never into a false MERGED. **R25Δ (R24-03/R24-04):** FP's `rev-list` is added to the list, and FINALIZE calls already answered from the cache are excluded from FINALIZE's determinacy (§1.7.3). **R26Δ (R25-02):** inside FINALIZE, EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and (under P-14) CR1/CR2 checks (including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records; R28Δ, R27-03) are not in the canonical order. A **single** genuine fault in one of them can change the outcome between conforming implementations (C09 vs K18 when one implementation skips a comparison by transitivity). Fixtures inject on none of them. A proposed explicit EV order is offered under UR-23 and not adopted.
- **(r) (R16Δ)** CV1/CV7 texts are still O P-21; the owner may reword them, but PV-28's constraint (no unchecked anchoring claim) should bind any rewording.
- **(s) (R16Δ; R17Δ restated)** Under (b′) with Option A, anchoring never runs, even where landing rests on counts; CV7 says so. The carried check still catches a target-first identical addition absorbed by the branch (S_A > End − Bu → K33), so the practical attribution loss is smaller (§11.46).
- **(dd) (R21Δ; R22Δ corrected, R21-01)** In outcomes the UR-22 interim is never looser than any option (§11.85). It is stricter than Revision 20 (= Option B) in:
  - H1 (FX-K22m: MERGED + CV3 → K22);
  - H4 (FX-K21d: MERGED → K21; here it is also stricter than Options A and C);
  - class-5 delete/re-add histories for non-text → text (FX-K21f: MERGED → K21);
  - **class-5 restore-then-re-change histories for non-text → text (FX-K21k: MERGED → K21)** (R24Δ, R23-07; R26Δ, R25-03: the predicate against Revision 20 is in the header's "Toward AMBIGUOUS" bullet, and it holds **given that Revision 20 is MERGED** (R27Δ, R26-02) — §1.7.3's predicate compares the interim with the options);
  - **MERGED → K21 with an H1-type path present** (FX-K21n; R26Δ, R25-03), and `binary_present` / `superseded` movements on every content-field line, not only MERGED lines (FX-K21c; R26Δ, R25-03);
  - the **reason** movement K22 → K21, whose exact predicate is in the header: (i) K + J = 0 and CS = ∅; (ii) some class-5 Kk path; (iii) Revision 20 insufficient through a relevant C-text path with non-textual Tn (FX-K21l) or through b < B (FX-K21m) (R24Δ, R23-07; R25Δ, R24-02: corrected);
  - on MERGED lines, `binary_present=1/1` where Revision 20 printed `0/0`, for a class-5 Kc path with another path that keeps a counted line (FX-K23bd; R25Δ, R24-02).
  In caveats it adds CV3 where Option B gives none (FX-K23az, K23ba); The current CV3 text (§1.10) keeps that CV3 true (R26Δ, R25-07). Frequency unmeasured.
- **(ee) (R21Δ)** The relevance claim's scope is paths(C): a later branch rename moves C's content to a path outside paths(C) and it is not examined; CV3's "changed again" then includes a move, which is not itself detected (consistent with L-4) (R22Δ: was "'replaced' then means 'moved'").
- **(ff) (R22Δ, R21-03; R23Δ, R22-02 extended)** Revision 21 moved commits from K22 to MERGED (+CV3 iff superseded > 0) wherever a relevant C-text path has no survival class S(i) and a non-textual current target. Two cases: (a) End non-textual (FX-K23ax); (b) End textual with no touched line (FX-K23ay, K23bc). Tn at that path is not examined. In (a), residual (j)'s container may still hold C's line. In (b), net counts cannot tell which copy the branch removed, so C's own line may be at End and in Tip and unchecked in Tn (FX-K23bb/bc). Defensible under the definitions, but it loosens Revision 20 (§11.87, §11.93). Frequency unmeasured; no runtime validation.
- **(gg) (R22Δ, R21-02)** State-reading rules cannot tell an edit of C's object from an unrelated delete-then-re-add or restore-then-re-change of the same kind (End class 5, §11.86). Option A, and Options B and C for non-text → text, then report MERGED with no CV3 although nothing of C's object may remain. The interim avoids this at the mirror cost: K21, or MERGED + CV3, for edit-only histories where most of C's content survives (FX-K21d, K23az). Detecting the difference would need history inspection between C and End, which this design does not do.
- **(hh) (R22Δ)** Option C is described for the owner and not adopted. Its superseded equals Option B's, so it inherits B's non-text → text hazard. If adopted, H4 becomes MERGED (FX-K21d). The author has not searched for histories where C differs from both A and B other than those in §11.78/§11.86.
- **(ii) (R22Δ, R21-05)** Argv identity assumes every revision operand is written as a full object id (§1.7.3). A harness cannot fail DG's `c_paths_in_unit` separately in ancestry-direct mode (FX-C10g), and it cannot inject into snap1 by argv. The per-run end points of §11.89 are derived from §4's control flow, not measured.
- **(jj) (R22Δ; R23Δ updated; R24Δ, R23-02: every caveat)** All caveat texts CV1–CV8 are PDBs under P-21; CV3 was reworded in Revisions 22, 23 and 24, and CV1's parenthetical in Revision 24. If the owner rewords **any** caveat, PV-33 (4) should bind. No caveat may say "replaced" alone for changes CV3 counts, imply that nothing of a change remains, attribute a net count change to a particular copy of an identical line, or use "survives" for a line change other than by net count.
- **(kk) (R23Δ, R22-03; R24Δ, R23-03/R23-04)** L-30: before FINALIZE the order and short-circuiting inside preflight's Phase C checks (C01–C07), the FP `rev-list --first-parent T`, `oldest_fp_container`, C12's conjunction, IA1's parent reads, IA2–IA6, the K10 shape-hit tests and cand, and — inside FINALIZE — EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and (under P-14) CR1/CR2 checks, including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records (R25Δ, R24-03: FP added; R26Δ, R25-02/R25-07: Phase C and EV added; R27Δ, R26-04: CR2's exclusivity test named) are unspecified. Double failures there, injection reachability, the last call of an early-exit sequence, and the work-counting limits (pid computations, 300 s total) are not determined by the specification. **Genuine** failures (faults, natural timeouts, limits) in calls that some orders skip can make **outcomes differ between conforming implementations** (MERGED, K10 or K12 vs C08, C09 or C10), never into a false MERGED; RT-02's reproducibility across implementations is not guaranteed for such repositories. Fixtures are constrained to at most one pre-FINALIZE failure. UR-23 is open. The author did not specify an order, because the design establishes none. **R25Δ (R24-04):** a FINALIZE call whose argv an unspecified pre-FINALIZE computation may already have issued (a §6.2 record pid of a SOTHER commit; u_C when parent0(C) = B_C) is answered from that result, so with two genuine faults, which failure is reported is not determined. The FP `rev-list`'s position is unspecified as well (R24-03). **R26Δ (R25-02):** inside FINALIZE, EV's §6.2 commit parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 ordering comparisons and (under P-14) CR1/CR2 checks are not in the canonical order. A **single** genuine fault in one of them can change the outcome between conforming implementations (C09 vs K18 when one implementation skips a comparison by transitivity). Fixtures inject on none of them. A proposed explicit EV order is offered under UR-23 and not adopted.
- **(ll) (R23Δ, R22-01)** CV3 and K21 now speak only in net counts. They remain count-level statements: they can neither confirm nor deny that C's own copy of a line is present (L-4). Whether the owner wants a stronger statement is part of P-21.
- **(mm) (R23Δ)** The Revision 22 review rated four of Revision 22's own "RESOLVED IN DESIGN" claims as PARTIALLY RESOLVED. Revision 23's dispositions are again the author's claims and need independent checking.
- **(nn) (R24Δ, R23-08)** The pid-pipeline cache key (a pid(A,B) is one unit keyed by DIFF(A,B)'s argv) is a clarification that Revision 23 lacked. If an implementation cached the `patch-id` stage by its own constant argv, every pid after the first would be wrong. This clarification has not been independently reviewed.
- **(oo) (R24Δ, R23-01)** The removal of line-copy claims was checked by a pattern scan (§11.98), not by a full re-read of every sentence. Phrasings outside the patterns may remain. The Revision 23 reviewer did not read the historical §9/§11 records either.
- **(pp) (R24Δ, R23-03)** Under the UR-23 interim (accept L-30), two conforming implementations can give different outcomes on a repository with a genuine fault in a call only one of them reaches (never a false MERGED). Whether that is acceptable is the owner's decision (UR-23).
- **(qq) (R25Δ, R24-01)** The §0 historical-records rule resolves conflicts between old records and current rules **by precedence**, not by correcting the records. A reader of a historical §9/§11 section must apply §0. Only the five known conflicting statements are marked inline.
- **(rr) (R25Δ, R24-03)** FP's position is unspecified. On a repository whose first-parent history exceeds 20,000 commits, an early-exit row (K01–K07, K27, K08) or C08 can result, depending on the implementation. Placing FP is part of UR-23.
- **(ss) (R25Δ, R24-04)** FINALIZE's determinate first failure excludes calls that pre-FINALIZE computations may already have issued. The list of such calls (§6.2 record pids of SOTHER commits; u_C when parent0(C) = B_C) is the author's and may be incomplete.
- **(tt) (R25Δ, R24-07; R26Δ, R25-06)** The rule that cache-answered pids do not count toward the pid limit is a definitional choice under P-19. Under L-30 the count before FINALIZE still depends on order. Under this rule the limit may be **unreachable** by lazy orders (about 16,004, a reasoned bound), so FX-C08's pid case is order-dependent / unproven. The alternative noted by the Revision 25 review — counting every pid the specification defines, issued or not — is the owner's choice under P-19 and is **not adopted**.
- **(uu) (R26Δ, R25-02)** Inside FINALIZE, EV's §6.2 parses, §6.3 lineage checks, §6.5 comparisons and CR1/CR2 checks (including CR2's exclusivity test over the other records; R28Δ, R27-03) are unordered. A **single** genuine fault there can change the outcome between conforming implementations (C09 vs K18). RT-02's reproducibility across implementations is not guaranteed for such repositories. A proposed EV order exists under UR-23 and is not adopted.
- **(vv) (R26Δ, R25-07)** Preflight's Phase C rows have no first-match rule. A repository meeting two Phase C conditions may report either row, depending on the implementation. This is part of UR-23.
- **(ww) (R26Δ, R25-05)** The current text now includes six earlier sections by reference. Their prose was written for earlier revisions and was amended only where reviews pointed. A full re-read of them for consistency with the current rules is part of the next review. **R27Δ (R26-03):** the Revision 26 review read them in full and found two stale passages (§11.89, §11.93), now corrected. A further scan found three more stale references (§11.78, §11.85), also corrected.
- **(xx) (R27Δ, R26-02)** The `binary_present` and `superseded` movements between Revision 20 and the interim are disclosed by rule (denominator per class-5 Kc path; superseded per class-5 Kk path), not by enumerating every row. A reader comparing outputs across revisions must apply that rule.
- **(yy) (R28Δ, R27-05)** The last call of a FINALIZE run is not determined when an implementation issues unordered EV calls after DG. Injection reachability on such runs is therefore not determined either. UR-23 option (c), confining EV's calls to EV's slot, would close this; it is not adopted.
- **(bb) (R20Δ; R27Δ, R26-01: restated in state terms)** The relevance argument (§1.7.3) is kind-independent. It uses only that every survival class — S(i) by net line count, S(ii) by content or kind under the UR-22 rule in force, S(iii) by mode, S(iv) by blob — implies entry(End,p) ≠ entry(Pre,p). It makes **no** claim about which copy of an identical line is C's: End can equal Pre while C's own added copy remains (FX-K23be; L-4). The next reviewer should attack that implication — that each survival class forces a state difference — first. (Revision 20–26 wording, "it assumes only that 'part of C's change is present at the branch end' implies a difference of entry", is a copy claim, false under FX-K23be, and is withdrawn.)
- **(cc) (R20Δ)** L-29 (over-selection) and L-28 (blob limit) are fail-closed costs of unmeasured frequency; together with L-21 (Option B's K31 on squashes) they may make many real SPSM integrations AMBIGUOUS. Measuring them needs separate authorisation (RT-10).
- **(y) (R19Δ)** K21's reason `superseded-in-branch` does not claim that a mode or uncounted change of C was superseded; such a change may have survived and landed (§1.7.4 state 2).
- **(z) (R19Δ)** L-27 is a new fail-closed cost; its frequency is unmeasured. R18-01 was the third false MERGED in the relevance family (R12-01, R17-01, R18-01); the author offered the owner a structurally simpler relevance rule (a path is relevant iff the branch end still differs from C's parent there), which the owner did not adopt for this revision — the targeted fix was chosen. The next reviewer should again attack the universal survival claim first.
- **(aa) (R19Δ)** `cat-file -s` returning an exact size, and its argv being answered from a cache, are assumptions on git behaviour (UR-16-style verification).
- **(v) (R18Δ)** R17-01 shows that a normative claim ("a surviving change cannot drop out") was false for five revisions without any reviewer catching it until R17. Other universally stated claims (§11.30, §11.36) are reasoned, not proven; the next reviewer should attack them first.
- **(w) (R18Δ)** L-26 (file ↔ directory through merge or squash → K31) and FX-K20e (mode-only binary change → K20, was MERGED) are new fail-closed costs.
- **(x) (R18Δ)** The argv first-occurrence rule assumes a call's result can be reused; a harness that wants to fail a later identical call cannot express it — by design.
- **(t) (R17Δ)** CV1 clause A enumerates the change kinds in words; if the owner rewords it (P-21), PV-28 must still hold: the clause must cover exactly the relevant paths.
- **(u) (R17Δ)** The canonical read order fixes which of several simultaneous failures is reported; it is a specification choice, not a claim about which failure is more important.
- **(m) (R14Δ)** The corrected carried check adds K33 cases under Option A whenever the unit contains branch-local churn on a line C touches (a predecessor added it, C removed it). Their frequency is unmeasured, like the guard's.
- **(n) (R14Δ)** No Revision 13 review report is stored; R13-01 and R13-02 were taken from the owner's brief. A reviewer should judge them on the restatement in §9.00.
- **(h)** While writing §11.10 the author found that AMBIGUOUS fixtures on Φ-fresh also change freshness fields under a mandatory pin; the Bo definition was widened to cover it. A reviewer should check that no other field-level P-25 dependency remains.

## 12. Authority and scope boundaries

- No owner decision is taken here: no P-n, no UR-2/3/13/14/17/19/20/21/22/23, no UR-22 option (A, B or C — C is described, not adopted), no UR-23 answer (R23Δ), no P-25(i)–(iii). Interim behaviours are fail-closed defaults, not decisions (R22Δ, R21-04: UR-22 added).
- No implementation, runtime test, commit, push, publication or A2.6 is authorised.
- G-F stays CLOSED. SPSM was not accessed. [M] items are earlier measurements only.
- A review PASS of this packet is not an implementation authorisation.

## 13. Independent-review instructions (Revision 28, text-only)

```
You are an INDEPENDENT adversarial reviewer with no prior context. Your permitted inputs
are exactly:
  (1) PRIMARY — this "A2.5.1 Revision 28 — Specification Packet"
      (handoffs/2026-10-17-a2.5.1-revision-28-review-packet.md); only its text is in force;
  (2) EVIDENCE — the Revision 27 independent review
      (handoffs/2026-10-17-a2.5.1-revision-27-review.md), the controlling finding list;
  (3) HISTORY — the Revision 27 packet
      (handoffs/2026-10-17-a2.5.1-revision-27-review-packet.md), as needed.
Read nothing else (in particular not the author's note or any hand-off). Use only a file
reader on these three files; run no commands; access no repository or network; implement
nothing; execute no tests; change no files or memory. Do not suggest executing textconv,
external diff drivers or repository-provided programs.

COVERAGE REQUIREMENT: read the ENTIRE primary packet (every line), and the complete
Revision 27 review. Read the Revision 27 packet as needed to compare changed text. In your
report, state every file read with its complete line range(s), and explicitly disclose any
```
